A candidate fix (a `pendingClearResetViewId_` member, lazily reset inside `ReadBackbuffer()` *after* a frame flush already happened, to avoid Task 951's own "eager reset discards still-pending same-frame draws" regression) was implemented and initially looked promising, but **two independent, controlled repro attempts disproved it**: a plain unbound `RenderTarget2D` forced through 5 unrelated backbuffer-only frame flushes before being sampled back — with a black-clear-then-magenta-draw fill (not a bare `Clear()`, which would make a re-clear-to-the-same-colour indistinguishable from correct behaviour) — survived correctly with or without the candidate fix; adding a `Depth24Stencil8` attachment to that same simple 2D-RT-plus-SpriteBatch-fill repro *still* survived correctly either way. Since the candidate fix could not be shown to change any observable behaviour, it was **reverted, not committed** (the count of `"  1  "` debug-group occurrences in the trace was in fact identical with and without it — the original "gets reprocessed multiple times" premise itself does not hold under direct comparison). **Also ruled out, all via direct experiment**: stencil presence (`Depth24Stencil8` vs. `Depth24`), specific depth format (`Depth24`/`Depth16` reproduce identically), and framebuffer incompleteness (`glCheckFramebufferStatus` always reports `GL_FRAMEBUFFER_COMPLETE`). **Tooling wall hit**: `glretrace`/`eglretrace` (both installed) cannot replay this trace for direct pixel/visual inspection — every attempt fails with "no current context" starting almost immediately (call ~112 of ~13000+), a known apitrace limitation with multi-threaded GL apps; bgfx renders on its own dedicated render thread, which apitrace's single-context-timeline replay model does not track correctly. `apitrace dump --blobs`/`-v` also does not capture `glReadPixels`' output bytes (only input args are ever recorded — the tool assumes replay will regenerate real output, which is exactly what fails here). RenderDoc (bgfx already attempts to `dlopen` it at startup — see `programs.md` §7) is not available in this sandbox's Debian 13 apt repos. **What's still needed, for whoever picks this up next**: either RenderDoc via a manually-downloaded `.deb`/AppImage from renderdoc.org (would let bgfx auto-integrate for lossless frame/texture capture, the most direct path), or a from-scratch minimal raw-GL (no bgfx) reproduction of "cube-face colour attachment + companion 2D depth/stencil attachment in one FBO, recreated fresh on every bind" to isolate whether this is a genuine llvmpipe/Mesa driver bug or a bgfx-internal one. Given the structural comparison against `RenderTarget2D`'s own working depth-attachment code and EasyGL's own working cube+depth implementation (`EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend`, which reuses one persistent FBO + a renderbuffer depth attachment across all 6 faces, rather than recreating the whole framebuffer object fresh on every `BindAsRenderTargetFace()` call the way `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` does) turned up no further leads via code review alone, the FBO-recreation-per-bind pattern remains the most plausible remaining CNA-side suspect, but is NOT yet confirmed. |
| 954 | XNA 4.0 culling/`RasterizerState.CullMode` compatibility audit — cross-repo investigation of a SimpleAnimation turret rendering discrepancy (`../cna-samples` finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED (RESOLVED — both framework and asset portions) — 2026-07-11. Full write-up: `docs/xna_culling_compatibility_audit.md`.** Investigated a dark, disc-shaped underside surface visible under `SimpleAnimation`'s turret in CNA but confirmed absent in an authoritative live XNA 4.0 (Windows 7/VirtualBox) reference screenshot the project owner captured mid-investigation. **Framework conclusion (now independently confirmed against real XNA, not just FNA source reading): CNA's `RasterizerState.CullMode` — `None`/`CullClockwiseFace`/`CullCounterClockwiseFace`/default — is self-consistent and correct on all 3 backends (EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx)** across identity/orthographic/perspective projection, a real `Matrix.CreateLookAt` view (SimpleAnimation's own exact camera), positive- and negative-determinant World transforms, both the simple `DrawUserPrimitives`/`DrawColoredPrimitives` path every pre-existing CullMode test used and the real indexed/`BasicEffect`/lighting path `Model`/`ModelMesh::Draw()` actually uses — verified via 2 new NDC-signed-area-prediction-based reproducer tests (`examples/rasterizerstate_cullmode_camera_test.cpp`, `rasterizerstate_cullmode_indexed_basiceffect_test.cpp`, 36/36 checks PASS across all 3 backends), extending the pre-existing identity-transform-only Tasks 323-325/765 coverage. **Decisive confirmation**: a new minimal XNA 4.0 C# project (`cna-samples/tools/xna-reference/CullModeTest/`) was run by the project owner on the same real Windows 7/XNA 4.0 VM — real XNA's default `CullMode` state keeps the same "negative NDC signed area" triangle and culls the other, pixel-for-pixel matching CNA's own default behavior. **No CNA framework change made — none justified by any evidence found.** **Asset-level finding, corrected in scope after project-owner pushback**: the first pass wrongly concluded the defect was localized to `turret_geo`'s own disc sub-region (a ~52/48% survive/cull split on that one mesh looked like a normal, correctly-wound mesh). A follow-up screenshot from the project owner showed the wheels were *also* visibly wrong (hollow/inside-out), which a whole-model (not per-mesh) diagnostic `CullClockwiseFace` override confirmed: **all 12 of `tank.fbx`'s converted mesh parts have their triangle winding reversed relative to real XNA's content-pipeline convention**, not just `turret_geo`. Edge-adjacency orientation checks (0 inconsistencies on all 12 meshes) explain why this wasn't visually obvious per-mesh — a uniformly-reversed mesh is still internally self-consistent and its outward convex silhouette barely changes; only exposed interior/underside detail (the turret disc, the wheel treads) revealed it. **Fixed**: all 12 `cna-samples/samples/SimpleAnimation/Content/tank_*_idx.bin` files had their triangle winding reversed (swap `idx[i+1]`/`idx[i+2]` per triangle — same vertex data, same file sizes). Verified visually at 3 different rotation angles against the authoritative XNA reference screenshot: closed turret dome, solid wheel drums, no new holes elsewhere, animation unaffected. Originals backed up before modification. No SimpleAnimation-specific `CullMode` workaround anywhere — the fix is entirely in the data; the app renders correctly under CNA's real, unmodified default `RasterizerState`. `Tank.hpp`/`SimpleAnimationGame.hpp` confirmed byte-identical to their pre-investigation state (several diagnostic edits made and fully reverted). `CameraShake`/`CustomModelClass`/`ReachGraphicsDemo` have their own independent, untouched copies of the same tank mesh files and almost certainly share the same defect — flagged, not fixed (out of scope). `fbx_ascii2model.py`'s own root cause for producing reversed winding is not yet pinned to an exact line — a future conversion through this tool could reproduce the defect; tracked as a follow-up. **Also found, fixed-then-reverted**: a real, confirmed, unrelated Bgfx bug (`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` silently discards `GpuDrawParams::startIndex`/`baseVertex` in its non-wireframe path) — a fix was attempted but caused a worse regression on retest (root cause not isolated in the time available) and was reverted rather than shipped unverified; tracked as a new, separate, NOT-yet-fixed follow-up (not part of this task's own scope). **New cna-samples deliverable**: `tools/xna-reference/CullModeTest/` — the minimal standalone XNA 4.0 Windows project described above, now confirmed run successfully on real XNA 4.0. Full regression: 0 new failures on any backend (EasyGL 188/190, Vulkan 125/126, Bgfx 102/104 — all remaining failures already-documented pre-existing) + `CnaTests` clean on all 3. |
| 955 | XNA 4.0 depth-occlusion compatibility audit — cross-repo follow-up investigation of a SimpleAnimation part-occlusion discrepancy, found after Task 954's asset fix was applied (`../cna-samples` finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-11. Full write-up: `docs/xna_depth_occlusion_compatibility_audit.md`.** After Task 954's winding fix was applied and verified, the project owner reported the tank was closer to the original but still wrong in a new way: some parts render fully visible when they should be occluded by other parts in front of them — a depth/occlusion problem, not placement/winding. **Root cause: `GraphicsDevice`'s constructor only ever pushed its `RasterizerState` default to the backend (Task 896) — `BlendState`/`DepthStencilState` were left as C++-level fields only, never applied via `ApplyBlendState()`/`ApplyDepthStencilState()`.** `Tank.hpp::Draw()` never explicitly sets any of the 3 (correct, idiomatic XNA code mirroring real `Tank.cs`), so it was exercising each backend's own hardcoded internal default instead of XNA's real `DepthStencilState.Default`. **On EasyGL this meant depth testing was OpenGL's raw default: disabled** — `easygl::Device::initialize()` never calls `set_depth_test_enabled(true)`, confirmed via direct source read — any two overlapping opaque `Model`/`ModelMesh` draws simply painted in draw order regardless of Z, exactly matching the reported symptom. Confirmed authoritative via direct FNA source read: real `GraphicsDevice.cs`'s own constructor unconditionally sets `BlendState = BlendState.Opaque; DepthStencilState = DepthStencilState.Default; RasterizerState = RasterizerState.CullCounterClockwise;` — Task 896 had ported only the 3rd line. **Fix**: added the other 2 lines to CNA's `GraphicsDevice` constructor (`src/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/GraphicsDevice.cpp`), matching FNA exactly — no backend-specific patch, no `cna-samples` change. **Diagnostic isolation before writing the fix**: a temporary explicit `setDepthStencilStateProperty(DepthStencilState::Default)`-only override in `SimpleAnimationGame::Draw()` (no `BlendState`/`RasterizerState` touched) reproduced the exact same corrected render as overriding all 3 (`compare -metric AE`: 0 differing pixels) — isolating the whole bug to `DepthStencilState` alone; removing the diagnostic entirely and applying the real constructor-level fix instead reproduced that same pixel-identical result with zero `cna-samples` changes, confirming the fix's true ownership boundary. **Static-pose verification** (`WheelRotation`/`SteerRotation`/`TurretRotation`/`CannonRotation`/`HatchRotation`=0, fixed camera): before/after screenshots show the turret dome closed (previously showed dark "window" gaps letting the background bleed through) and the engine tube no longer bleeding through the turret's silhouette — 61,236 of 748,800 pixels (~8.2%) differ, concentrated exactly in those regions. **Bonus fix found via the same root cause**: Bgfx's own `blendFlags_` member defaulted to `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_ALPHA` (alpha blending silently on by default) instead of matching `BlendState.Opaque` — corrected as a direct side effect of the same constructor fix; Vulkan was never actually affected (its own member defaults already happened to match XNA by coincidence). **New shared 3-backend regression test** `examples/graphicsdevice_default_state_occlusion_test.cpp` — deliberately unlike every other depth/blend test in this project (which explicitly set state before drawing): this one never touches `BlendState`/`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState` at all, exercising only `GraphicsDevice`'s own untouched defaults (two overlapping opaque quads, near green drawn first then far red second — draw order deliberately defies depth order, so only a genuinely working default depth test produces the correct GREEN result). **Discriminating power verified via `git stash`**: reverted state reproduced the exact predicted failure (Check A FAIL, centre=(255,0,0) instead of (0,255,0)); restored and reconfirmed 2/2 PASS. **Full regression, 0 new failures on any backend**: EasyGL `ctest -R "^EasyGL_"` 188/190 (2 already-documented pre-existing: Task 145 MRT, Task 872 ReferenceStencil) + `CnaTests` 4371/4373 (2 hardware skips, exact baseline); Bgfx `ctest -R "^Bgfx_"` 103/105 (2 already-documented pre-existing: Task 878/879 MSAA limitation, Task 952 deferred); Vulkan `ctest -R "Vulkan_"` 126/127 (1 already-documented pre-existing `Vulkan_DepthBias`). **A second, separate, still-open state-leak bug found while investigating (deliberately NOT fixed here, tracked as Task 956)**: `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::Begin()` enables blending unconditionally but `End()`/`FlushBatch()` never restores the prior blend state — any 3D draw issued after a `SpriteBatch.Begin()`/`End()` pair inherits its blend state instead of whatever was active before. Not implicated in this bug (SimpleAnimation's own `DrawHelpOverlay()`'s `SpriteBatch` never runs in either repro), confirmed via direct source read only. |
| 956 | `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend`'s blend state leaks into subsequent 3D draws — `Begin()` enables blending but nothing restores it after `End()` (found while investigating Task 955) | ✅ | **Stale row, corrected 2026-07-17 — actually CLOSED 2026-07-11, commit `481c0293`.** This task's own "likely fix" guess (`End()` re-applying the device's *previously*-active state, "mirroring FNA's `SpriteBatch.End()`") was based on a false premise — checked directly against `EffectHelpers`/`SpriteBatch.cs` in `481c0293`'s own investigation: real FNA's `SpriteBatch.End()` does **not** restore prior device state at all, it leaves `GraphicsDevice.BlendState` at whatever `Begin()` applied. The real bug was narrower: `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::Begin()` unconditionally hardcoded `set_blend_enabled(true)`+`SrcAlpha`/`OneMinusSrcAlpha`, clobbering whatever `GraphicsDevice::setBlendStateProperty()` had just applied for the real requested `BlendState` — both silently ignoring non-`AlphaBlend` requests and leaving that hardcoded GL state stuck after `End()`. Fixed by deleting the hardcoded call entirely (`SpriteBatch::Begin()` already applies the correct blend state via `setBlendStateProperty()` before the backend's own `Begin()` runs). Verified Vulkan/Bgfx don't have the identical gap (neither backend's `Begin()` touches blend state). New dedicated regression test `EasyGL_SpriteBatch_BlendStateLeak` (`examples/easygl_spritebatch_blendstate_leak_test.cpp`), discriminating-power-verified via `git stash`; also fixed a masked bug it exposed in `easygl_sample_layered_blend_test.cpp`. Re-confirmed still passing 2026-07-17 (this session, before correcting this row) — no code changes needed, only this plan's own bookkeeping was out of date. |
| 952 (cont.) | Same investigation, continued 2026-07-11 with a real RenderDoc 1.45 install (project owner downloaded it to `~/Downloads/renderdoc_1.45` after the apitrace round above hit its wall — see `programs.md` §7, now updated with real findings instead of "not available"). | ⬜ | **DEFERRED (2026-07-11) — explicitly paused by the project owner after this round; resolve later, not this session.** No code changes are pending (all temporary diagnostics were reverted and confirmed via a clean `git status` + successful rebuild before this pause). Do not resume investigating this without explicit direction — see `NEXT.md` §9 for the same "do not resume" framing used for Tasks 863/869/WebGPU. **Real progress made before pausing: captured and statically analyzed a genuine RenderDoc frame, narrowing the bug significantly further — but a full GUI/Python-based pixel inspection remains blocked in this sandbox, documented below so a future session doesn't re-attempt the same dead end.** **Getting a capture at all required 3 real fixes, none of them CNA changes** (all applied as temporary, reverted-before-commit diagnostics — nothing here is a landed change): (1) bgfx only calls its own `loadRenderDoc()` when `bgfx::Init::debug` or `::profile` is true — CNA never sets either, so RenderDoc was silently never loaded despite the library being present; temporarily set `init.debug = true`. (2) `librenderdoc.so` must be loaded via `LD_PRELOAD`, not a plain runtime `dlopen()` from within the test binary — RenderDoc's hooking only intercepts the target's own subsequent `dlopen("libGL.so.1")` (exactly like `apitrace`'s own interposer) if it's preloaded *before* that happens. (3) bgfx only triggers a capture when `_render->m_capture` is set, which only happens via `bgfx::frame(BGFX_FRAME_DEBUG_CAPTURE)` (a real, public bgfx API flag) — temporarily changed `ReadBackbuffer()`'s own `bgfx::frame()` call to pass it. With all three in place plus a manual `RENDERDOC_API_1_6_0::StartFrameCapture`/`EndFrameCapture` pair added directly to a scratch copy of the test (dlopen'ing `librenderdoc.so` and calling `RENDERDOC_GetAPI` itself, entirely outside CNA/bgfx — see this row's own commit-free scratch history, not present in the tree), a real 588KB `.rdc` capture was produced and confirmed valid (`renderdoccmd thumb` successfully extracted a thumbnail). **`qrenderdoc --python <script>.py` (the only way to get the `import renderdoc` Python API — this RenderDoc release ships no standalone importable module, only qrenderdoc's own embedded interpreter) hangs indefinitely under this sandbox's Xvfb, with or without `QT_DEBUG_PLUGINS=1` tracing** — the script's own log file is never even created, meaning the hang happens before the `--python` script gets a chance to run at all; `qrenderdoc --version` (no GUI) returns instantly, and a plain `xterm` under the same `DISPLAY=:99` works fine, so it's specific to Qt/XCB's own window-system initialization in this sandbox (no window manager is installed — `share/doc/renderdoc/html/window/python_shell.html` documents the Python shell as a GUI-only feature, consistent with this). **`renderdoccmd convert -c zip.xml` (fully non-GUI, no hang) unblocked real analysis**: converts the `.rdc` to a structured, fully human/`grep`/`python`-readable XML of every recorded GL call with its exact parameters (an even more detailed, more precisely-orderable log than `apitrace dump` — includes real chunk-processing order, not just call-issue order). Cross-referencing every `glBindFramebuffer` against every `glDrawArraysInstanced`/`glBindTexture(GL_TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP,...)` in the captured frame (a small Python script over the XML, not the RenderDoc API) established, precisely and with hard evidence for the first time this investigation: **(a)** `bgfx::createFrameBuffer()`'s returned handle for the color+depth cube-face attachment combo *is* valid (confirmed directly via a temporary `bgfx::isValid(fbo)` diagnostic print — `valid=1`); **(b)** `SubmitViewProgram()`'s `currentViewId_` *is* correctly `1` (the cube's own view) for both quad draws, confirmed via a temporary print; **(c)** in the actual GL call trace, both quad draws *do* execute while the cube's real FBO (GL object `2945`) is bound — the apparent "first draw hits FBO 0" reading from the raw call-issue-order log was a misread of bgfx's ascending-view-ID *processing* order (the retry loop's own first sample draw, submitted to view 0, gets processed *before* view 1's draws in the same flushed frame, not because of a targeting bug); **(d)** the cube's real texture handle (`2943`) is correctly bound for `EnvironmentMapEffect` sampling on **every single one of the ~20 retry-loop attempts**, not just the first. Despite all of (a)-(d) being correct, the sample still never picks up any drawn content on any attempt. **This rules out FBO-handle validity, view-id targeting, and texture-handle-identity as the bug** — leaving either a genuine llvmpipe/Mesa GL driver defect in *executing* draws against this specific attachment combination (despite them being correctly *issued* and *targeted*), or something at the raw pixel/fragment level (depth/stencil interaction, sRGB, blend, or scissor state specific to this exact FBO) that only a real pixel-history/texture-viewer tool (the exact capability blocked by the qrenderdoc GUI hang) can distinguish from a driver bug. **Root cause still not found.** Next step, if resumed: either get `qrenderdoc`'s Xvfb hang fixed (a minimal WM like `xvfb-run` + a lightweight WM, or an `offscreen`/`minimal` Qt platform build of RenderDoc, neither available in this exact 1.45 Linux tarball) to unlock `PipelineState`/pixel-history inspection, or write a raw-GL (no bgfx, no CNA) minimal reproduction of this exact attachment shape to test directly against llvmpipe. | 
| 953 | Vulkan segfaults creating a 0-length index/vertex buffer — 3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` tests crash (found during Task 950/952 regression sweeps, `NEXT.md` §5) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-11.** Root cause confirmed exactly as `NEXT.md`'s own prior diagnosis: `VulkanIndexBufferBackend`'s and `VulkanVertexBufferBackend`'s constructors (`VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp`) compute their `VkBuffer`'s byte size directly from the caller-supplied `index_capacity`/`vertex_capacity` (`capacity * elemSize`/`capacity * 64`) with no lower bound — 3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` fixtures load a model part with a genuinely empty (0-length) `.idx.bin`, producing `index_capacity=0`, and `CreateBuffer()` then calls `vkCreateBuffer`/`vkAllocateMemory` with `size=0`, which the Vulkan spec forbids (`VUID-VkBufferCreateInfo-size-00912` / `VUID-VkMemoryAllocateInfo-allocationSize-07897`) — the validation layer logs the violation and the process segfaults shortly after in `vkBindBufferMemory` (binding a real `VkBuffer` handle to a `VK_NULL_HANDLE` memory object). **Fix**: both constructors now clamp their computed `VkDeviceSize` to a minimum of 1 byte via `std::max<VkDeviceSize>(1, ...)` before calling `CreateBuffer()` — the *logical* `capacity_`/`indexCount_`/`vertexCount_` fields are untouched (still legitimately 0), only the underlying GPU allocation is guaranteed non-empty; `SetData16`/`SetData32`/`SetData`'s own `memcpy` calls with a 0-length source are already no-ops, so nothing downstream needed to change. Scoped to exactly the 2 constructors — `CreateBuffer()` itself was deliberately left unclamped since it's shared by texture staging/UBO call sites where a 0-size call would indicate a different, real bug that should keep surfacing loudly. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash`** of the 1 production file: baseline reproduced the exact predicted segfault (exit code 139) with the exact predicted 4 Vulkan validation errors (`vkCreateBuffer`/`vkAllocateMemory` size-is-zero ×2/`vkBindBufferMemory` null-memory); restored and reconfirmed clean. **Verification**: `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest.*` (9 tests, previously 3 segfaulting) now 9/9 PASS, no crash. Full Vulkan regression: `ctest -R "Vulkan_"` 121/122 (1 already-documented pre-existing `Vulkan_DepthBias`, +1 net new passing test vs. Task 950's baseline) — 0 new failures; full `CnaTests` binary (4372 tests, all suites including the fixed one) 4369 passed / 2 hardware-skipped / 1 failed (`DrawUserIndexedPrimitivesArgumentGuardTest.VD_16bit_ZeroCount_Throws`, already-documented pre-existing test-order-dependent flakiness under Vulkan/llvmpipe — see Task 883's own write-up of the same test appearing in unrelated flaky full-suite runs; passed 3/3 in isolation, confirming it's not a regression from this fix). EasyGL/Bgfx untouched (fix is Vulkan-only, in a Vulkan-only source file) — not rebuilt. |
| 951 | Root-cause and fix 6 pre-existing Bgfx `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` `ctest` crashes (NEXT.md §4/§8 item 1) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-11. 5 of 6 crashes genuinely fixed (not masked); the 6th no longer crashes but reveals a separate, real, pre-existing correctness bug, logged below as a new open item rather than fixed here.** All 6 crashed identically: `bgfx fatal: glReadPixels(...) GL error 0x502: GL_INVALID_OPERATION`, asserted inside bgfx's own vendored `renderer_gl.cpp` `requestScreenShot()`. **Root cause (2 distinct mechanisms, both rooted in the same architectural fact):** bgfx processes every configured view in ascending view-id order once per `bgfx::frame()`, and `bgfx::setViewFrameBuffer(viewId, fbo)` is a per-view-**per-frame** setting (last call before `frame()` wins for the *whole* frame, not per-submit) — confirmed directly from this codebase's own Task 910 comment and independently re-derived while debugging a regression (below). Since `CNA::Internal::Backends::Bgfx::Detail::AllocateRtViewId()` deliberately reserves view id 0 for the backbuffer and hands every render target an id > 0 (Task 910), *any* render target with pending/queued work in the same bgfx frame as a backbuffer read is — by ascending-order construction — always the *last* view bgfx processes that frame, leaving its FBO the one still GL-bound when `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer()`'s `bgfx::requestScreenShot()`-driven `glReadPixels()` fires immediately afterward (that function never explicitly rebinds framebuffer 0 itself — confirmed by reading the vendored source). For a plain color-only RT this merely reads back stale/wrong pixel values (already silently tolerated by this project's established GetBackBufferData-retry-loop convention); for an RT with a depth/MSAA/mip-enabled attachment, directly `glReadPixels`-ing that non-trivial FBO at the *backbuffer's* size hits a genuine GL error under this sandbox's llvmpipe-backed legacy-GL-2.1 bgfx OpenGL context (confirmed: `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN` — the existing Task 878/879 workaround for this environment's separate, already-documented MSAA-quality limitation — silently falls back to `active renderer: OpenGL 2.1` in this sandbox too, so it does not route around this bug either; a genuine environment-support regression worth its own future investigation, not chased here). **Fix (all in `BgfxGraphicsBackend.{hpp,cpp}`, Bgfx-only, zero shared-code changes):** ~~an initial attempt~~ eagerly releasing the outgoing render target's own view (`bgfx::setViewFrameBuffer(oldViewId, BGFX_INVALID_HANDLE)`) inside `SetRenderTarget2D`/`SetRenderTargetCubeFace`/`SetRenderTargets` was tried first and **reverted** — per the per-frame-not-per-submit semantics above, this silently discarded that view's own still-*unflushed* queued draws whenever a 2nd render target (or the backbuffer) was bound within the same un-advanced frame, breaking `Bgfx_ConcurrentRenderTargets` (0 pre-existing failures → 1 new regression, caught by full-suite `ctest`) and, worse, silently corrupting 3 of the 6 target tests' own RT content into flat black instead of merely "not crashing" (their `centre=(0,0,0)` readback matched exactly what "the fill draw got discarded" predicts). **Real fix**: reserved the single highest bgfx view id (255, `Detail::kBackbufferFlushViewId`) permanently for a dedicated "backbuffer flush" view — never handed out by `AllocateRtViewId()` (its `kMaxBgfxViews` cap tightened from 256 to 255) — and in `ReadBackbuffer()`, explicitly `bgfx::setViewFrameBuffer(255, BGFX_INVALID_HANDLE)` + `bgfx::touch(255)` immediately before `requestScreenShot()`. Since 255 is now always numerically higher than every real render-target view, it is always the *last* view bgfx processes, guaranteeing the true backbuffer is what's GL-bound at `glReadPixels` time — without ever touching (and so without ever discarding) any render target's own view/fbo state, fixing `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_{DepthBuffer,MipChain}` and `Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_{MipChain,MsaaResolve}` outright (confirmed genuinely correct, not corruption-masked: real green depth-test win, real ~7:1 weighted mip average `(224,0,32)` vs. expected `(223,0,32)`, real blue MSAA/mip cube-face samples) with zero regression to `Bgfx_ConcurrentRenderTargets` (still 3/3 PASS). **A 6th, genuinely separate crash** (`Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat`) was root-caused independently: `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend::BindAsRenderTargetFace()`'s depth attachment used `bgfx::Attachment::init(depthTex, bgfx::Access::Write)` with its default `_resolve = BGFX_RESOLVE_AUTO_GEN_MIPS` (correct/desired for the *color* cube-face attachment right above it, per the existing Task 907 comment, but `depthTex` is never created with `hasMips=true`) — bgfx hard-asserts "Depth textures do not support MSAA resolve" the instant a 2nd (depth) attachment is built this way. Fixed by passing `BGFX_RESOLVE_NONE` explicitly for the depth attachment only. This test no longer crashes but now surfaces (not introduces) a separate, real, pre-existing bug — depth testing does not actually gate `RenderTargetCube` face draws on Bgfx (`got=(0,0,0)` where `(0,255,0)` — the nearer quad — was expected) — logged as a new open row rather than fixed here (out of this crash-focused task's scope). `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` remains an expected/known 7th failure (see this table's own Task 878/879 entry — the same pre-existing, already-diagnosed legacy-GL-2.1 MSAA-quality environment limitation, unrelated to this task's crash fix). **Discriminating power verified via `git stash`**: reverting both changed files reproduced all 5 fixed crashes identically (`Subprocess aborted`/`SIGTRAP`, same `GL_INVALID_OPERATION` assert) while `Bgfx_ConcurrentRenderTargets` still passed on that same unmodified baseline (3/3), confirming the regression was real and is fully resolved by the final fix, not just no-longer-observed. **Full regression**: `ctest -R "^Bgfx_"` 97/99 (was 93/99 — 2 remaining are the pre-existing MSAA-quality limitation + the newly-logged, separately-scoped `RenderTargetCube` depth-gating bug below, neither a crash); `CnaTests` 4375/4377 (2 hardware skips), exact match to the pre-existing baseline, 0 new failures. Change is entirely confined to `include/CNA/Internal/Backends/Bgfx/BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp` + `src/CNA/Internal/Backends/Bgfx/BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp` — no shared (`GraphicsDevice.cpp`) or other-backend files touched, so EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer builds are structurally unaffected. |
| 872 | Implement `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil` as a real, independent, backend-connected override on all 3 backends (Task 319 finding) | ⬜ | **Status corrected (2026-07-09) — this row's own original diagnosis was already stale, found while investigating Task 838/871 in the Phase 72/73 follow-up.** `IGraphicsBackend::SetReferenceStencil` DOES now exist and IS implemented on Vulkan (`VulkanGraphicsBackend::SetReferenceStencil`, `referenceStencil_ = value`, threaded per-deferred-draw into `Pending3DDraw::referenceStencil` and applied via a real `vkCmdSetStencilReference` dynamic-state call before each 3D draw) — this was added as an undocumented side effect of Task 870's own stencil dynamic-state overhaul, never folded back into this row. **Confirmed via direct empirical re-run**: `cna_test_vulkan_graphicsdevice_reference_stencil` (the exact same discriminating test described below) genuinely PASSES on current Vulkan, both in isolation and as part of the full 4444/4448 Task 925 regression (not in the failure list). **EasyGL and Bgfx remain genuinely broken** — confirmed via grep, neither backend overrides `SetReferenceStencil` at all (falls to the shared no-op default); `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` is still failing in the current, live test baseline (Task 924's own regression, 2026-07-09). Real remaining scope is now 2 backends, not 3. Original diagnosis preserved below for the EasyGL-relevant parts (still accurate for that backend): `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty(int value)` **on EasyGL/Bgfx** is a pure local field write with zero backend connection. Confirmed via FNA source (`GraphicsDevice.cs`) that `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil` is meant to be a real, independent device property (`FNA3D_Get/SetReferenceStencil`), settable without reassigning the whole `DepthStencilState` and expected to immediately affect subsequent stencil compares — FNA's own source even has a `FIXME: Does this affect the value found in DepthStencilState?` comment confirming this is intentionally an override, not a read-through proxy. `examples/easygl_graphicsdevice_reference_stencil_test.cpp` proves the gap empirically on EasyGL (Vulkan's own copy of this same test now passes, see above): stamp stencil=0x05, assign a compare `DepthStencilState` with a baked-in `ReferenceStencil=0x05` (would PASS at face value under `StencilFunction::Equal`), then call `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty(0x99)` directly (not via a new state) and draw — a correct implementation must REJECT (0x99≠0x05); EasyGL/Bgfx still incorrectly PASS (override never reaches the GPU, the state's own stale baked-in 0x05 is still what gets compared), Vulkan now correctly REJECTS (fixed, see the status-correction note above). (`GraphicsDevice::setDepthStencilStateProperty` DOES correctly propagate a *newly-assigned* state's own `ReferenceStencil` into `GraphicsDevice`'s local `referenceStencil_` field as of Task 319's fix — that part works; this bug is specifically about changing the reference *independently*, without reassigning the whole state, which XNA/FNA supports and this project currently cannot.) Fix shape: add a `SetReferenceStencil(int value)` method to `IGraphicsBackend`; EasyGL needs to cache the currently-active `StencilFunction`/masks (not currently stored as members, only passed straight through to `glStencilFunc`/`glStencilFuncSeparate` per `ApplyDepthStencilState` call) so it can reissue the same compare with just the new reference; Vulkan is now done (see the status-correction note above — `vkCmdSetStencilReference` genuinely wired in as dynamic pipeline state, a side effect of Task 870); Bgfx needs to patch just the reference bits of its already-packed `stencilFront_`/`stencilBack_` state via `BGFX_STENCIL_FUNC_REF_MASK`/`BuildBgfxStencil`-style helpers without requiring a full `ApplyDepthStencilState` call. Not fixed here for EasyGL/Bgfx — needs its own dedicated task and full regression pass across those 2 backends, matching Task 868/870/871's precedent of tracking-not-fixing large multi-backend gaps found during a verification task. |
| 873 | Fix `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw`'s wrong-handle-type cast when sampling a `RenderTarget2D` (Task 333 finding) | ✅ | **Fixed as a hard prerequisite of Task 878/879's Bgfx MSAA test** (its RT-then-backbuffer-sample methodology cannot produce a meaningful result without this fix). Implemented exactly the fix shape this row's own writeup predicted: a new `IBgfxSamplable` interface (`virtual bgfx::TextureHandle GetBgfxTextureHandle() const`) implemented by both `BgfxTextureBackend` (`return textureHandle;`) and `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` (`return colorTex;`), with `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` now `dynamic_cast`-ing to it instead of blindly `static_cast`-ing to `BgfxTextureBackend`; `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SubmitSprite`'s signature changed from taking a `const BgfxTextureBackend&` to a raw `bgfx::TextureHandle` + width/height so it no longer assumes a concrete type either. Scoped only to the `SpriteBatch::Draw` path this task actually needed — the ~8 other unsafe `static_cast<const BgfxTextureBackend&>` sites in `DrawPrimitivesEx`'s stock-effect texture binding (unrelated, much larger surface area) were deliberately left untouched. **Real, severe, previously-only-suspected bug, now confirmed.** `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` (`BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp`) unconditionally does `static_cast<const BgfxTextureBackend&>(texture)` on whatever `ITextureBackend` it receives. `RenderTarget2D`'s backend is a `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`, which inherits `IRenderTargetBackend`/`ITextureBackend` directly — **not** `BgfxTextureBackend` (an unrelated sibling class) — so this is a `static_cast` between unrelated concrete types, sharing only a common interface ancestor. Because `bgfx::FrameBufferHandle` and `bgfx::TextureHandle` are both `struct { uint16_t idx; }` (identical layout, confirmed in `bgfx.h`'s `BGFX_HANDLE` macro) and both are the first data member of their respective classes, the cast compiles and doesn't crash, but reads `BgfxRenderTargetBackend::fbo` (the framebuffer handle) where `BgfxTextureBackend::textureHandle` would be — i.e. it hands bgfx a **framebuffer-pool handle index where a texture-pool handle index is expected**, sampling whatever unrelated texture (or nothing) happens to occupy that index in bgfx's separate texture-handle pool, not the RT's actual `colorTex` color attachment. Confirmed via direct layout analysis (deterministic, not speculative) and via a new runtime smoke test, `examples/bgfx_render_target_sample_test.cpp` (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_SampleAfterUnbind`), which exercises exactly this path — it does not crash (bgfx has no strong handle-type validation to catch this), consistent with silently sampling the wrong data rather than throwing. This upgrades Task 179's informal suspicion ("SpriteBatch casts textures to BgfxTextureBackend, not BgfxRenderTargetBackend") into a confirmed, precisely-diagnosed bug. Cannot be pixel-verified — Bgfx has no usable GPU pixel-readback path in this project (established precedent: Tasks 179/249/278). Fix shape: give `ITextureBackend` (or a Bgfx-specific extension point) a virtual accessor for "the `bgfx::TextureHandle` to sample", implemented correctly by both `BgfxTextureBackend` (`return textureHandle;`) and `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` (`return colorTex;`), and have `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` (and any other Bgfx code path that samples an arbitrary `ITextureBackend`, e.g. stock-effect texture binding) call that accessor instead of blindly `static_cast`-ing to `BgfxTextureBackend`. Verification without pixel readback: assert the extracted handle's `.idx` equals `BgfxRenderTargetBackend::colorTex.idx`, not `fbo.idx`, after the fix. Contained to the Bgfx backend only (`BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`) — does not touch the XNA API surface or other backends. Not fixed here — needs its own dedicated task per this project's discipline of not bundling backend-internal fixes into a verify-only audit task, and because no pixel test can confirm the fix visually, only structurally. |
| 874 | Fix Bgfx's wrong-handle-type cast when sampling a `RenderTargetCube` via `EnvironmentMapEffect` (Task 334 finding) | ✅ | **Backlog-hygiene closure (2026-07-09) — no new work, already fixed under a later task number, left unmarked.** Fixed by Task 907 as a verification prerequisite: added the exact `IBgfxCubeSamplable` interface (mirroring `IBgfxSamplable`, Task 873's own fix shape) this row's own diagnosis predicted, implemented by both `BgfxTextureCubeBackend`/`BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`, dispatched via a safe `dynamic_cast` instead of the blind `static_cast`. See `plan_graphics.md` Task 907 for full detail. **Same bug shape as Task 873, one level over — confirmed by direct layout analysis.** `BgfxGraphicsBackend`'s `envMapping` draw branch does `static_cast<const BgfxTextureCubeBackend&>(*params.envMap)`. A `RenderTargetCube`'s backend is a `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`, which inherits `IRenderTargetCubeBackend`/`ITextureCubeBackend` — **not** `BgfxTextureCubeBackend` (an unrelated sibling class). `BgfxTextureCubeBackend`'s first data member is `bgfx::TextureHandle handle`; `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s first data member is `bgfx::FrameBufferHandle fbo` (its actual colour texture, `cubeTex`, is the SECOND member) — both handle structs are `struct { uint16_t idx; }` (same `BGFX_HANDLE` macro as Task 873), so the cast compiles and doesn't crash, but reads `fbo` where `handle` should be: a framebuffer-pool handle passed where a texture-pool handle is expected. Confirmed via a new runtime smoke test, `examples/bgfx_render_target_cube_sample_test.cpp` (`Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`) — no crash, consistent with silently sampling wrong data. Fix shape: identical to Task 873's — give `ITextureCubeBackend` a virtual accessor for "the `bgfx::TextureHandle` to sample", implemented by `BgfxTextureCubeBackend` (`return handle;`) and `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` (`return cubeTex;`), and use it in the `envMapping` branch instead of the blind `static_cast`. Likely worth fixing together with Task 873 in the same pass, since both need the identical accessor pattern on the 2D and cube interfaces respectively. Not fixed here — same reasoning as Task 873 (own dedicated task, no pixel-verifiable fix confirmation on Bgfx). |
| 875 | Fix Vulkan: `Clear()` with no draw call into a bound render target never records a render pass (Task 334 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, confirmed bug fixed per this row's own predicted fix shape (a).** Added a new `std::vector<VulkanRTSource*> clearedRTs_` member to `VulkanGraphicsBackend`: `Clear()`/`ClearColorAndDepth()` now push `currentRT_` onto it (when a render target is bound, deduplicated) in addition to their existing clear-colour-scalar bookkeeping; `RecordCommandBuffer`'s `usedRTs` construction now seeds from `clearedRTs_` before scanning `activeBatches_`/`pending3D_` (its previous, draw-call-only sources); `clearedRTs_` is cleared alongside `activeBatches_`/`pending3D_` once per frame. **A key nuance discovered while designing the regression test**: `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget()`'s own existing `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents`-triggers-an-implicit-clear behavior (the default usage) dispatches through `ClearColorAndDepth()`, not `Clear()` — meaning `ClearColorAndDepth()`'s copy of this fix is actually the one doing the real work for the common case; `Clear()`'s own copy only matters for `RenderTargetUsage::PreserveContents` targets or a `ClearOptions::Target`-only clear. Both were fixed identically since both needed it regardless. New `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_ClearOnlyRoundtrip` test (`vulkan_rt_roundtrip_test.cpp`, port of `easygl_rt_roundtrip_test.cpp`/Task 180): fills 2 separate `RenderTarget2D`s via `Clear()`-only (no draw call) then samples each back via `SpriteBatch` after unbinding (the same proven-good RT-as-texture path Task 148 already uses) — **deliberately does NOT read the RT directly while still bound**, unlike the EasyGL original it ports from; investigation confirmed Vulkan's `GetBackBufferData`/`ReadBackbuffer` unconditionally reads the swapchain image regardless of whatever RT is currently bound (unlike EasyGL, which tracks `currentRtHeight_` and redirects) — an initial direct port that tried reading "while bound" only appeared to pass by coincidence (the swapchain's own backbuffer clear shares the same global clear-colour scalar as whichever RT was most recently `Clear()`-ed in the same recorded frame), and was caught failing at its 3rd, more revealing assertion rather than accepted as a false 2/3 pass — the same discriminating-power discipline this project applies throughout. The sample-after-unbind redesign also needed one more fix: forcing a frame boundary (dummy `GetBackBufferData` read) immediately after each RT's Clear-only fill, since Vulkan has no per-RT-remembered clear value — the shared global clear-colour scalar would otherwise be overwritten by the backbuffer's own subsequent `Clear()` call before the RT's render pass actually recorded, corrupting its content. **Discriminating power independently verified**: temporarily disabled both `clearedRTs_.push_back()` call sites (matching the true pre-fix state, not just one of the two) and reran — reproduced pure black `(0,0,0)` on both RTs instead of their fill colours; restored and reconfirmed 2/2 PASS. Full regression: `CnaTests` (Vulkan, filtered) 4262/4264 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); targeted `ctest -R "RenderTarget\|Mip\|Msaa\|MultiSample"` 64/64 (was 63/63 before this task's new test). |
| 876 | Investigate why sampling a `RenderTargetCube` via `EnvironmentMapEffect` renders black instead of its actual rendered content on Vulkan (Task 334 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED, verify-only — the bug no longer reproduces.** Rebuilt and reran `examples/vulkan_rendertargetcube_sample_test.cpp` (`Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`) unmodified while investigating this task: it now **passes cleanly and consistently** (`centre=(0,0,255)`, exact expected blue — confirmed across 5 repeated runs plus a dedicated `ctest -R` invocation, no flakiness observed). This is the SAME test/methodology that originally found the bug (real `SpriteBatch` draws into all 6 `RenderTargetCube` faces, unbind, sample via `EnvironmentMapEffect`) — not a weakened or false-positive reproduction. Root cause not retroactively bisected to an exact prior commit (would require a from-scratch vendored-submodule build environment at an older checkout, not worth the cost once the bug is confirmed gone) — most likely an incidental side effect of this session's substantial `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` rework for Task 907 (full-mip-range `cubeView_`, construction-time full-range initial layout transitions, per-face `FaceProxy` changes), though Task 907's own new initial-transition fix was gated on `levelCount_ > 1` and this test uses `mipMap=false` (`levelCount_ == 1`), so that specific fix is not obviously the direct cause either — genuinely unclear which change fixed it, only that something did. Both of this row's own previously-proposed candidates ((a) unverified `SpriteBatch`-into-cube-face content, (b) `GetOrCreateEnvMapDescSet`'s per-frame descriptor-set cache) are now moot since the whole round trip demonstrably works end-to-end. |
| 877 | Wire `DepthStencilFormat`'s exact requested value into each backend's render-target depth/stencil attachment (Task 335 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED on EasyGL and Bgfx — real, pixel-verified fix. Vulkan deliberately scoped down to a documented architectural limitation, split to new Task 911.** Changed `IGraphicsBackend::CreateRenderTarget2D`'s `bool hasDepth` param to `int depthFormat` (raw ordinal of `Microsoft::Xna::Framework::Graphics::DepthFormat`, mirroring the existing `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` `surfaceFormat`-as-`int` convention so this backend-agnostic header doesn't need to include the XNA namespace), and added the same new `int depthFormat` param to `CreateRenderTargetCube` (previously had no depth param at all). `RenderTarget2D.cpp`/`RenderTargetCube.cpp` now pass `static_cast<int>(preferredDepthFormat)` instead of a reduced `!= DepthFormat::None` bool. All 4 backend implementations (EasyGL, Vulkan, Bgfx, SdlRenderer) updated to the new signature. **EasyGL**: new file-scope `MapDepthFormat(int, InternalFormat&, FramebufferAttachment&)` helper maps `Depth16`→`DepthComponent16`+`Depth` attachment, `Depth24`→`DepthComponent24`+`Depth` attachment, `Depth24Stencil8`→`Depth24Stencil8`+`DepthStencil` attachment (`None` returns `false`, meaning no attachment at all) — applied to both `EasyGLRenderTargetBackend` and `EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s `CreateResources()`. **Also fixed a second, independent, real bug found while doing this**: `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::CreateRenderTargetCube()` previously hardcoded `hasDepth=true` unconditionally, ignoring whatever `DepthFormat` (including `None`) the XNA layer actually requested — now correctly threads the real value through. **Bgfx**: analogous `MapDepthFormat(int, bgfx::TextureFormat::Enum&)` maps to `D16`/`D24`/`D24S8`; `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` now honors the exact format instead of hardcoded `D24S8`. **Also added depth/stencil attachment support to `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` for the first time** — it previously had NO depth attachment capability at all, on any request (a real, separate feature gap beyond the format-fidelity issue this task was originally scoped for): added a shared 2D `depthTex` (mirroring `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s shared `depthImage_` across all 6 faces), created only when `depthFormat != None`, attached alongside whichever face is bound in `BindAsRenderTargetFace`. **Vulkan — deliberately NOT given per-RT format fidelity, a real architectural constraint discovered while scoping this**: Vulkan's RT render passes (`rtRenderPass_`/`rtRenderPassLoad_`/`rtRenderPassMsaa_`) are lazily created ONCE per backend and shared by every `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` instance, using the device-global `depthFormat_` chosen once at startup — and, critically, the SAME pipelines are deliberately reused across `renderPass_` (backbuffer) and every RT render pass to avoid a pipeline-per-render-pass explosion (confirmed via the `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`/Task 904 comment: "that same pipeline is reused to draw into `rtRenderPass_`/`rtRenderPassLoad_` whenever a 3D primitive is drawn into a `RenderTarget2D`"). Vulkan render-pass-compatibility rules require attachment formats to match exactly for this pipeline sharing to be valid — varying the depth format per RT would require a depth-format-keyed render-pass *and* pipeline cache across every one of the 10+ `GetOrCreatePipelineXXX` functions, a genuinely large, systemic change in the same class as Tasks 902/910 ("needs its own scoping pass," not an opportunistic fix). `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`'s vestigial, always-`true`, never-read `hasDepth_` field (confirmed via grep: set once at construction, read nowhere) was removed as dead code while touching this constructor. Both Vulkan `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` accept and ignore the new `depthFormat` param (documented inline), same accepted-and-ignored pattern already established for `surfaceFormat`/`mipMap` elsewhere in this codebase. Split into new **Task 911** (own scoping pass required before attempting, matching Task 902/910's precedent). **New tests, discriminating power verified via targeted backend-only sabotage (not full `git stash`, to keep the new interface signature intact for a clean comparison)**: `EasyGL_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat` (`easygl_rendertargetcube_depthformat_test.cpp`) — near-GREEN-then-far-RED quads drawn into all 6 faces (uniform content so the result doesn't depend on which face `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s reflection vector samples) with `DepthStencilState::Default`; `DepthFormat::None` correctly shows RED (last draw wins, no depth buffer), `DepthFormat::Depth24Stencil8` correctly shows GREEN (real depth test gates the draws) — confirmed FAILING (`None` case incorrectly shows GREEN) when `CreateRenderTargetCube`'s factory is sabotaged back to the old hardcoded-`true` behavior, confirming genuine discriminating power. `Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat` (`bgfx_rendertargetcube_depthformat_test.cpp`) — same near/far-quad method into a single face, read back **directly while still bound** (not via `EnvironmentMapEffect` after unbind — see Task 912 below for why). Confirmed structurally correct via debug instrumentation (`numAttachments=1`/invalid handle for `None`, `numAttachments=2`/valid handle for `Depth24Stencil8`), but this sandbox's software GL path (Mesa llvmpipe, bgfx's "OpenGL 2.1" renderer) was observed performing an identical, seemingly-correct near-wins z-comparison **regardless of whether a depth attachment exists at all** — confirmed by sabotaging `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s constructor to never create `depthTex` and rerunning: the `Depth24Stencil8` assertion still passed identically, proving zero pixel-level discriminating power for this specific assertion in this environment. Kept the `Depth24Stencil8`-gates-draws assertion (real and true, just not exclusively provable as a consequence of this fix in this sandbox) and dropped any strict pass/fail on the `None` case (logged as `[INFO]` only) — documented as an environment/driver quirk this task's fix does not control, mirroring the project's existing `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb-limitation precedent. **A second, more severe, unrelated pre-existing bug found and NOT fixed here, split to new Task 912**: while designing the Bgfx cube test, sampling a render target back out via `EnvironmentMapEffect`/`SpriteBatch` after a real 3D depth-tested draw into it turned out to be broken for *any* Bgfx render target (2D or cube) — confirmed by running the existing, completely unmodified `rendertarget2d_depth_test.cpp` (Task 335's own EasyGL/Vulkan test) against Bgfx for the first time ever (it was never registered for this backend): it fails, reading back pure black. Out of scope for this task (which is about depth *format* selection, not this deeper sample-after-unbind gap). **Full regression, all 3 backends**: EasyGL `CnaTests` 4262/4264 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline); Vulkan `CnaTests` 4262/4264 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline); Bgfx `CnaTests` 4265/4268 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline). Targeted `ctest -R "RenderTarget|Mip|Msaa|MultiSample"` on all 3 backends: EasyGL 36/36, Vulkan 63/63, Bgfx 56/58 (the 2 failures are the already-documented `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb limitation, and `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` reconfirmed flaky-under-batch/passes-in-isolation 4/4 times — both pre-existing, not regressions from this task). |
| 911 | Give Vulkan render targets true per-instance `DepthStencilFormat` fidelity (split out of Task 877) | ✅ | **CLOSED — every `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` on Vulkan now gets a real, distinct depth `VkFormat` picked for its own instance (or genuinely no depth attachment at all for `DepthFormat::None`), independent of the backbuffer's device-wide `depthFormat_` and of every other render target.** New `PickDepthFormat(VkPhysicalDevice, DepthFormat)` maps `Depth16`→`VK_FORMAT_D16_UNORM`, `Depth24`→`VK_FORMAT_X8_D24_UNORM_PACK32` (no-stencil), `Depth24Stencil8`/default→`{D24_UNORM_S8_UINT, D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT}`, each candidate probed via `vkGetPhysicalDeviceFormatProperties`+`VK_FORMAT_FEATURE_DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT_BIT` with a final fallback to `{D32_SFLOAT, D16_UNORM}`; `DepthFormat::None` is handled by callers (skip calling it entirely), not inside it. The old single shared `rtRenderPass_`/`rtRenderPassLoad_`/`rtRenderPassMsaa_` members became 3 `std::unordered_map<VkFormat, VkRenderPass>` caches, lazily populated by new `GetOrCreateRTRenderPass(VkFormat depthFmt, bool discardContents)`/`GetOrCreateRTRenderPassMsaa(VkFormat depthFmt)` (a format's entry shared by every RT requesting it; `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED` = no depth attachment at all). `VulkanRenderTargetBackend` (RenderTarget2D) and `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` (RenderTargetCube) both now accept and genuinely act on their `depthFormat` constructor parameter (previously silently dropped/ignored, see Task 877's own note on this row before the fix) — depth image/view creation is conditional on `hasDepth`, and framebuffer/`GetRenderPass()` select the depth-format-keyed render pass instead of a shared one. New `VulkanRTSource::GetDepthFormat()` virtual (default `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED`) lets `draw3DFor`/`drawSpritesFor` discover "what depth format does the currently-bound target actually use" generically across the backbuffer (`depthFormat_` directly, no `VulkanRTSource`), `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`, `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s per-face `FaceProxy`, and `VulkanMRTProxy`. Every one of the 9 3D pipeline-creation functions (`GetOrCreatePipeline3D` et al.) and the 2D sprite pipeline (`GetOrCreatePipeline2D`/`GetOrCreatePipeline2DMsaa`, converted from eagerly-created singletons into the same depth-format-keyed-cache pattern, lazily created on first use from `drawSpritesFor` instead of eagerly in the constructor) gained a `VkFormat targetDepthFmt` parameter, folded into their cache key via a new `FoldDepthFormatIntoKey()` helper (bits 45+, clear of `Make3DKey`/`MakeExt3DKey`'s own bits) and used to select the render pass via a new shared `PickRTPipelineRenderPass(colorAttachmentCount, msaa, targetDepthFmt)`: MRT (`colorAttachmentCount > 1`) deliberately stays out of scope, always using `GetOrCreateMRTRenderPass()`'s device-wide `depthFormat_` (XNA/FNA's own semantics for "which bound target's depth format wins in a multi-target draw" are inherently ambiguous); a single-target draw reuses the backbuffer's own `renderPass_`/`renderPassMsaa_` whenever the target's real depth format matches `depthFormat_` (the common case, keeping pre-existing pipeline/cache-hit behavior identical), otherwise falls back to the depth-format-keyed render pass. **A real regression found and fixed while regression-testing this task**: `VulkanMRTProxy` (`SetRenderTargets` with 2–4 targets) previously borrowed `rts[0]->GetDepthView()` directly for its shared depth attachment, an invariant that silently depended on every RT always owning a real depth image regardless of requested `DepthFormat` — broken by this task's own `DepthFormat::None` fix (a `RenderTarget2D` with no depth buffer now has a null `depthView_`), reproducing as a live `vkCreateFramebuffer(): pAttachments[4] is VK_NULL_HANDLE` validation error and process abort on `GraphicsDeviceValidationTest.SetRenderTargets_FourTargets_DoesNotThrow` (which uses the simple no-depth `RenderTarget2D(device, w, h)` overload for all 4 targets). Fixed by giving `VulkanMRTProxy` its own dedicated depth image/view/memory, always in the device-wide `depthFormat_`, never borrowed from any bound RT — also surfaced (and fixed) a second, pre-existing, unrelated leak: `mrtProxy_` was never explicitly reset before `~VulkanGraphicsBackend()` destroyed `device_`, so its own Vulkan objects (previously just `framebuffer_`) always leaked silently; now explicitly `.reset()` early in the destructor while `device_` is still valid. **New discriminating test** `vulkan_rendertarget_depthformat_fidelity_test.cpp` (`Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_DepthFormatFidelity`): draws a near-red-then-far-green depth-tested quad pair into 3 simultaneous `RenderTarget2D`s in one frame (`DepthFormat::None`/`Depth24Stencil8`/`Depth16`) — `None` must show green (no real depth buffer to reject the farther second draw), the other two must show red (a real depth buffer correctly rejects it), proving both genuine per-format fidelity and correct multi-format coexistence within a single frame. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash`**: reverting the Vulkan backend changes reproduces the predicted failure (`DepthFormat::None` incorrectly renders red — a real depth buffer was still silently allocated) and the MRT segfault; restored and reconfirmed both pass. **Full Vulkan regression**: `ctest` 4369/4378 passed (9 failures, identical set to Task 870's own already-documented baseline — 5 `BlendState` + 1 `DepthBias` sub-case + 3 `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults, all independently reconfirmed via the same `git stash` to already exist before this task's changes; zero new failures). 2D sprite pipeline and MRT's own depth handling were explicitly in scope for this task's "push through full scope" mandate (per project owner decision); custom SPIR-V effect pipelines (Task 119, `VulkanEffectBackend`) were NOT touched and may share this same render-pass-compatibility gap for a non-default-depth-format RT — left as a known, separate, not-yet-triaged issue for a future task. |
| 912 | Fix Bgfx: sampling a render target (2D or cube) back out via `EnvironmentMapEffect`/`SpriteBatch` after a real 3D depth-tested draw into it reads back incorrect/black content (found while building Task 877's Bgfx cube depth test) | ✅ | **CLOSED, verify-only — root-caused to an already-documented Bgfx quirk, not a distinct bug.** Bisected exactly as this row's own note suggested, using `rendertarget2d_depth_test.cpp` as the minimal repro: stripped the 2nd (red) quad, `DepthStencilState::Default`, and eventually replaced the entire `BasicEffect`+`DrawUserPrimitives` RT-fill with a plain `SpriteBatch`-based fill (matching every OTHER already-working Bgfx RT-sampling test's pattern) — **still failed identically at every step**, disproving every one of this row's own candidate hypotheses (`DepthStencilState`/`RasterizerState` leakage, `DrawUserPrimitives` vs `SpriteBatch` dispatch). The actual cause: sampling a render target via `SpriteBatch` **immediately** after filling and unbinding it, with no intervening `bgfx::frame()` boundary, can read back stale/black content on the very first `GetBackBufferData()` call — this is the exact same "Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData()` only reliably reflects the first read call per rendered frame" quirk already documented in NEXT.md §5 and already worked around by `bgfx_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp`'s established retry-loop convention (`for (...) { Clear; Draw; Read; if (non-black) break; }`, up to 20 iterations) — `rendertarget2d_depth_test.cpp` simply never needed that convention before because it was never exercised on Bgfx (registered for EasyGL/Vulkan only). Confirmed the fix: adding the identical retry loop to `rendertarget2d_depth_test.cpp`'s Pass 2 makes the exact original test (real depth-tested `BasicEffect` fill, unmodified) pass correctly and consistently on Bgfx. Applied the retry loop directly to the shared file (EasyGL/Vulkan unaffected — both already produce the correct pixel on the very first iteration, so the loop breaks immediately) and registered it for Bgfx for the first time (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer`), closing the coverage gap this row's own investigation surfaced as a side effect. Updated `bgfx_rendertargetcube_depthformat_test.cpp`'s header comment (Task 877) to correct its "tracked separately as Task 912" claim now that this is understood, without changing that test's own already-verified logic. **Full regression, all 3 backends**: `CnaTests` exact baseline match (EasyGL/Vulkan 4262/4264; Bgfx 4266/4268, 2 skipped — 1 fewer skip than the prior baseline, an unrelated `GuideTest` environment non-determinism, still 0 failures); targeted `ctest -R "RenderTarget\|Mip\|Msaa\|MultiSample"` EasyGL/Vulkan unaffected, Bgfx 57/59 (new `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer` passes; the 2 failures are the already-documented `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb limitation and `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`, reconfirmed genuinely flaky under this sandbox — 4/5 direct runs pass, 1/5 fails with a different wrong value each time — pre-existing, unrelated to any change in this task). |
| 878 | Implement `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` mip support on Vulkan and Bgfx (Task 336 finding) | ✅ (Vulkan `RenderTarget2D` only) — Bgfx `RenderTarget2D` split to Task 906 (needs new downsample-shader infra, bigger than originally predicted), `RenderTargetCube` on both backends split to Task 907 | **Vulkan `RenderTarget2D` half CLOSED — real feature implemented and pixel-verified.** `VulkanRenderTargetBackend` gained a real mip chain: `levelCount_` computed the same way as EasyGL's `CalculateRenderTargetMipLevels` (Task 336) when `mipMap=true`; `colorImage_` now created with `mipLevels=levelCount_` and `TRANSFER_SRC_BIT|TRANSFER_DST_BIT` added to its usage (harmless when `levelCount_==1`); a new `colorSampleView_` exposes the *full* mip range for sampling/descriptor purposes while the pre-existing `colorView_` stays mip-0-only for the framebuffer attachment (a render pass can only target one mip level); the RT's one-time construction transition now covers all `levelCount_` levels (not just level 0, unlike the shared single-level `TransitionImageLayout` helper) so `colorSampleView_`'s full range is never sampled while any level sits in `UNDEFINED`. Real mip **generation** is a new `VulkanRTSource::MaybeGenerateMips(VkCommandBuffer)` virtual (default no-op; overridden by `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`), called from `RecordCommandBuffer` immediately after each used RT's `vkCmdEndRenderPass` — a `vkCmdBlitImage` cascade (level 0 → 1 → 2 → ... with `VK_FILTER_LINEAR`, correct per-level `VkImageMemoryBarrier` sequencing so each level is `TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL` only while being read and `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` at rest) — this is Vulkan's equivalent of EasyGL's `glGenerateMipmap`-on-unbind (Task 336) / FNA3D's `OPENGL_ResolveTarget`, and runs whether or not the RT engaged MSAA (the resolve, if any, already lands in `colorImage_` mip 0 via the render pass's `pResolveAttachments` before this runs). `CreateRenderTarget2D` now threads `mipMap` through instead of accepting-and-ignoring it. **Found and fixed a real, independently-necessary prerequisite bug while building the test**: every Vulkan `VkSampler` (`CreateSampler()`'s `defaultSampler_` and every `ApplySamplerState()`-created per-slot variant) had `maxLod=0` (`VkSamplerCreateInfo{}`'s zero-init) — this clamps every sample to mip level 0 regardless of `mipmapMode`/screen-space derivatives, harmless for the single-level images every prior texture/RT used, but it would have made a perfectly-generated mip chain completely unreachable through any sampling path. Fixed both to `VK_LOD_CLAMP_NONE`; safe for every pre-existing single-level resource since the actually-visible LOD range stays bounded by each resource's own `VkImageView`'s `levelCount`, not the sampler alone. New `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` test (`vulkan_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp`): renders a deliberately **asymmetric 7:1** red/blue split (7/8 red rows, 1/8 blue rows — NOT 50/50) into a `mipMap=true` RT via `SpriteBatch`, unbinds (triggering the blit cascade), then reads back two ways — (1) native 1:1 draw (level 0 only, unaffected by mips) must show crisp, unblended red/blue at two points well inside each region; (2) scaled to a 1×1 on-screen destination (a 64:1 minification ratio, `log2(64)=6` exactly) forces the GPU's automatic derivative-based LOD selection to the single coarsest mip level, whose content — per the blit cascade — must be the whole image's true 7:1 weighted average, ~(223,0,32). **Design iteration caught by the project's own discriminating-power discipline before landing**: an earlier version of this test used a 50/50 split, which turned out to be a false positive — a full-quad-to-1-pixel draw always samples the texture's *exact centre* (u=v=0.5, an inherent property of linear UV interpolation collapsed to a single output pixel, confirmed empirically), and a 50/50 split's colour boundary sits exactly there, so an *ordinary bilinear sample at level 0 alone* (mips entirely irrelevant) produces the same ~(128,0,128) blended value regardless of whether the fix exists — `git stash`-reverting the production fix and rebuilding still produced an identical "PASS". Caught precisely because this project's own methodology mandates verifying discriminating power, not just that the test passes once. Re-designed around the 7:1 split (whose centre-sample point, row 32 of 64, sits safely inside the solid red region) and reverified: `git stash`-reverting `VulkanGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp` now correctly fails check 2 (`sample=(255,0,0)`, pure red, exactly the predicted symptom) while check 1 (level-0-only) stays unaffected either way, and restoring the fix returns both checks to PASS with `sample=(224,0,32)` — within 1 unit of the exact predicted (223,0,32). **Full regression**: `CnaTests` (Vulkan, under `Xvfb`+`llvmpipe`) 4262/4264 passed, 2 known skips, 0 failed, excluding one pre-existing `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest`-area segfault confirmed **unrelated** to this task (reproduced identically with this task's `.hpp`/`.cpp` changes fully reverted via `git stash` — a pre-existing Xvfb/llvmpipe full-suite-run flakiness already documented in `NEXT.md` §4, not something this task introduced; the same test passes cleanly in isolation). 16 spot-run pre-existing Vulkan example binaries covering every RT/sampler/texture/viewport path this change could plausibly have regressed (`rendertarget2d_msaa`, `rendertarget2d_depth`, `rendertarget2d_properties`, `rendertargetcube_properties`, `rendertargetcube_sample`, `texture_address_mode` ×3, `texture_filter_point_vs_linear`, `texture_anisotropic_effect`, `sampler_state_effect`, `texture_srgb`, `basiceffect_texture_enabled`, `viewport_subregion`, `viewport_reset_after_resize`, `rendertarget_viewport_scissor_reset`) all pass unchanged — confirms the shared sampler `maxLod` widening and the RT's `GetVkImageView()` switching from `colorView_` to `colorSampleView_` didn't regress any existing sampling or render-target path. **Scope split, found during this task's own research**: Bgfx's `RenderTarget2D` mip half turns out to need substantially more new infrastructure than this row's own original prediction (`hasMips=true` on `bgfx::createTexture2D`) — bgfx's public API has **no** `glGenerateMipmap` equivalent, and its `bgfx::blit()` is a same-size region **copy** only (no resize/filter capability), unlike Vulkan's `vkCmdBlitImage` which natively supports a filtered blit between differently-sized regions (confirmed by reading `bgfx.h`'s `blit()` doc comments directly). Real mip generation on Bgfx therefore needs a genuine render-to-specific-mip-level downsample pass (a new shader program sampling via explicit LOD, fullscreen-quad geometry, and a per-level `bgfx::Attachment(mip=N)` framebuffer for each mip level) — closer in scope to a new small rendering pipeline than a one-line texture-creation-flag change. Split to new **Task 906** rather than rushed into this commit. `RenderTargetCube` mip on both backends (6× the per-face complexity of `RenderTarget2D`, each backend's own dedicated cube-map image/view machinery) split to new **Task 907**. **Original research this task opened from (Task 336's EasyGL fix — retained for historical context; the "Vulkan"-specific claims below are now superseded by the paragraph above):** **EasyGL was fully fixed under Task 336** (real mip pre-allocation + auto-`glGenerateMipmap`-on-unbind, pixel-verified) — Vulkan and Bgfx were deliberately left unfixed, matching the project's own established Task-867-shaped precedent (property correct, GPU support lags per-backend). `LevelCount` is already correct on all 3 backends (shared, backend-agnostic C++ computation in `RenderTarget2D.cpp`/`RenderTargetCube.cpp`), so this task is purely about making Vulkan's/Bgfx's actual GPU resources match what the property already (correctly) claims. Fix shape for Vulkan: `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`/`VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` need to (a) accept and honor the `mipMap` parameter now threaded through `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` (currently accepted-and-ignored), (b) set `VkImageCreateInfo::mipLevels` to the real level count instead of hardcoded `1`, (c) create a mip-aware `VkImageView` (`levelCount` in `subresourceRange`, currently hardcoded to `1` — same shape as Task 867's Vulkan `Texture2D` mip-view finding), and (d) implement the actual mip-chain generation at resolve time — Vulkan has no `glGenerateMipmap` equivalent; the standard approach is a `vkCmdBlitImage` cascade (blit level N to level N+1, one draw-call-free blit per level pair) issued when the RT stops being the active target, mirroring where EasyGL's fix now calls `generate_mipmap()`. Fix shape for Bgfx: `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`/`BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` need to pass `hasMips=true` to `bgfx::createTexture2D`/`createTextureCube`'s attachment creation (currently hardcoded false, same shape as Task 864's `Texture3D`/`TextureCube` finding) — bgfx's own resolve/frame-advance model may auto-generate mips once `hasMips` is set correctly, needs verification against bgfx's actual behavior once wired. Verification: port `easygl_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp`'s `TextureFilter::Anisotropic`-black/blue methodology to Vulkan (Bgfx: same GPU-readback limitation as always — a doesn't-crash smoke test at minimum). Not fixed here — large, backend-internal, needs its own dedicated task and full regression pass per backend, same discipline as every other tracked multi-backend gap. |
| 879 | Implement `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` MSAA support on Vulkan and Bgfx (Task 337 finding) | ✅ (RenderTarget2D only — RenderTargetCube split to Task 903) | **RenderTarget2D MSAA now genuinely implemented and pixel-verified on Vulkan and Bgfx**, real anti-aliasing confirmed via the same diagonal-edge differential methodology as EasyGL's Task 337 fix (`{vulkan,bgfx}_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp`, direct ports of `easygl_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp`). `RenderTargetCube` MSAA is real XNA API surface (confirmed against FNA's actual `RenderTargetCube(GraphicsDevice, size, mipMap, preferredFormat, preferredDepthFormat, preferredMultiSampleCount, usage)` constructor — not moot) but deliberately not attempted here, split out to Task 903. **Vulkan fix shape — a deliberate, documented scope decision, not the fully independent-per-RT-sample-count design FNA's API implies**: `VulkanRenderTargetBackend` piggybacks on the Vulkan backend's own already-picked `sampleCount_` (`VulkanGraphicsBackend::sampleCount_`, chosen once at backend construction from `PresentationParameters.MultiSampleCount`) rather than threading an independent numeric sample count through all ~10 pipeline-cache keys (a much larger, riskier change). A given RT actually engages MSAA only when it requested `multiSampleCount>0` **and** the backend itself was built with `sampleCount_>1`; otherwise it honestly reports `MultiSampleCount=0` (matches the already-established "honest 0" precedent). Implementation: `VulkanRenderTargetBackend` gained an MSAA color image (`msaaColorImage_`, `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, never sampled directly, resolved automatically into the existing single-sample `colorImage_` via the render pass's `pResolveAttachments` at `vkCmdEndRenderPass` — no explicit `vkCmdResolveImage`), a promoted-in-place MSAA depth (`depthImage_`/`depthView_` reused, not duplicated — confirmed `depthView_` is never sampled externally anywhere in this codebase, only ever used as a framebuffer attachment, including the MRT path), and a new per-instance framebuffer against a new lazily-shared `VulkanGraphicsBackend::rtRenderPassMsaa_` (3-attachment: MSAA color/resolve/MSAA depth, DiscardContents-shaped only — PreserveContents+MSAA doesn't get its own LOAD_OP_LOAD variant, an intentionally narrower scope than the non-MSAA `rtRenderPass_`/`rtRenderPassLoad_` split). `draw3DFor`'s `drawMsaa` computation changed from `(targetRT==nullptr) && (sampleCount_>1)` to also check the specific target RT's new `VulkanRTSource::WantsMsaa()` virtual (default `false`), so RT draws reuse the exact same already-lazily-created MSAA pipeline variants the backbuffer path uses (same cache key shape: `colorAttachmentCount<=1`, `msaa=true`) — zero changes needed to any of the ~10 `GetOrCreatePipelineXxx` functions' bodies, since they already select `renderPassMsaa_` vs `renderPass_` based on the `msaa` bool. **A real, empirically-discovered Vulkan validation-layer subtlety**: pipeline/render-pass "compatibility" in this driver (RADV) requires matching subpass **dependency** stage/access masks too, not just attachment descriptions/subpass shape as the base spec text on render-pass compatibility implies — `rtRenderPassMsaa_`'s dependencies had to be made byte-identical to `CreateRenderPassMsaa()`'s (including the transfer-read scope that, semantically, only matters for the backbuffer's `GetBackBufferData` path) before `VUID-vkCmdDraw-renderPass-02684` validation errors stopped appearing; confirmed via direct instrumentation that the RT-pass loop's clear-value/render-pass/framebuffer dispatch (also updated: 3 clear values for the MSAA case, mirroring Phase 2's backbuffer `hasMsaa` handling) was otherwise correct. Fix shape for Bgfx: `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` now passes `BGFX_TEXTURE_RT_MSAA_X{2,4,8,16}` (mutually exclusive with plain `BGFX_TEXTURE_RT` — same 4-bit field, confirmed in `bgfx/defines.h`) instead of the old flag, on both color and depth attachments; the depth attachment must share the color attachment's sample count. `requestedMultiSampleCount` arrives already power-of-two-rounded (or 0) via `RenderTarget2D`'s own `ClosestMSAAPower` step, so the Bgfx-side clamp only needs to pick the matching constant and cap at bgfx's max of 16×. bgfx resolves the MSAA content into the same sampleable texture handle internally — confirmed zero explicit resolve code needed. **Verifying the Bgfx test surfaced two more real, previously-undiscovered, pre-existing bugs, both fixed as hard prerequisites** (invisible until now because no earlier Bgfx test both rendered into an RT smaller than the window AND could pixel-verify the result — blocked by Task 873, fixed alongside this): (1) `BgfxGraphicsBackend::EnsureViewState()` (called from `Clear()`/`SubmitSprite()`) unconditionally reset the current view's rect **and** 2D ortho-projection to the full **window** size on every single call, clobbering `BindAsRenderTarget()`'s correctly RT-sized `bgfx::setViewRect()` the moment `Clear()` was next called on that RT — silently corrupting all rendering (3D and 2D both share `spriteViewId`/`currentViewId_`) into any `RenderTarget2D` smaller than the window; fixed by tracking the currently-bound RT's own size (`currentRtWidth_`/`currentRtHeight_`, set in `SetRenderTarget2D`/`SetRenderTargets`) and only falling back to the full window size when `spriteViewId==0` (backbuffer). (2) A genuinely deep, separate architectural gap, found (not fixed — out of scope) while wiring up the Vulkan test: `GraphicsDeviceManager.PreferMultiSampling`/`ApplyChanges()` **never actually reaches the Vulkan backend at all** — `Game`'s own `GraphicsDevice` member is unconditionally default-constructed (`MultiSampleCount=0`) in `Game::Game()`'s member-initializer list, before any derived `Game` subclass or `GraphicsDeviceManager` preference-setting code can possibly run (fundamental C++ base-before-derived construction ordering, not fixable from a derived constructor); `GraphicsDeviceManager`'s existing apply path (`CreateDevice()`/`ApplyChanges()` → `applyToExistingBackend()` → `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationParameters()`) only patches window size/swap-interval on the already-built backend and explicitly does **not** trigger a full device reset (see that method's own pre-existing doc comment, and the commented-out `Reset()` call in `applyToExistingBackend`) — real device reset/recreation is FNA's `GraphicsDevice.Reset()`, a separate, larger, not-yet-implemented feature. This means `vulkan_msaa_test.cpp` (Task 147) has been a **false positive its entire existence** — its solid-red-quad assertion passes identically whether or not MSAA is actually active, so this gap was invisible until this task's genuinely-discriminating differential test could not pass no matter what `GraphicsDeviceManager` preference was requested. Confirmed the actual Task 878/879 RT-MSAA implementation is correct regardless, by temporarily forcing `VulkanGraphicsBackend::sampleCount_>1` directly — both checks passed cleanly, zero validation errors, on the first attempt. Not generically fixed here (same class of large, architecturally risky, blast-radius-heavy change as the already-tracked `GraphicsDevice::Reset()` TODO and Task 896's RasterizerState-default precedent) — tracked as new Task 902. Instead, added a narrow, `NOXNA`, test-only escape hatch, `GraphicsDevice::RecreateBackendForMultiSampleCount(int)` (tears down and rebuilds `backend_` in place, same window, safe only before any GPU resources exist — used exclusively by the new Vulkan test's `Initialize()`, zero blast radius on any other existing test). **A third, environment-specific finding** (Bgfx only, not fixed, not a code defect): in this project's Linux dev sandbox, bgfx's default OpenGL renderer negotiates only a legacy GL 2.1 context (confirmed via `glxinfo` that the underlying Mesa/RadeonSI driver actually supports OpenGL 4.6 with full hardware acceleration) under which MSAA-flagged framebuffer-attached textures do not actually resolve with real sub-pixel blending, despite `bgfx::getCaps()` reporting `BGFX_CAPS_FORMAT_TEXTURE_FRAMEBUFFER_MSAA`/`BGFX_CAPS_FORMAT_TEXTURE_MSAA` support for RGBA8 — confirmed unrelated to this task's (100% backend-agnostic `bgfx::` API) C++ wiring by forcing bgfx onto its own Vulkan renderer instead (`CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN`, routing through the same real AMD GPU Vulkan uses): both checks pass cleanly with a genuine intermediate pixel value, zero code changes.
