The Bgfx MSAA test is therefore registered with `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN` in its `ENVIRONMENT`. **Discriminating power independently verified on both backends**: `git stash`ed the 6 touched production files (both backends' `.hpp`/`.cpp` + `GraphicsDevice.hpp`/`.cpp`), rebuilt — Vulkan's new test failed to **compile** (references the now-reverted `RecreateBackendForMultiSampleCount`); Bgfx's new test built and ran, failing exactly as predicted (`MultiSampleCount=8` row purely binary, zero blended pixels) while `MultiSampleCount=0` still correctly passed. Restored the stash, rebuilt, reconfirmed both pass. **Full regression**: Vulkan `ctest -R Vulkan` 68/80 pass (same 12 pre-existing failures — `Vulkan_BlendState_{AlphaBlend,Additive,SeparateFunctions,SeparateFactors,BlendFactor}`, `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_{CompareFunction,StencilEnable,StencilMask,StencilOps,StencilTwoSided}`, `Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_DepthBias` — zero new failures), `CnaTests --gtest_brief=1` 3493 passed / 2 skipped (exact match to the established baseline); Bgfx `ctest -R Bgfx` 48/48 pass (100%, zero failures), `CnaTests --gtest_brief=1` 3497 passed / 2 skipped, 0 failed (100% of runnable tests — a different total test count than Vulkan's run is expected, backend-conditional test compilation, not a regression). EasyGL `CNA` target sanity-rebuilt clean (shared `GraphicsDevice.hpp`/`.cpp` changes don't touch anything EasyGL-specific). **A separate, genuinely latent, still-open Vulkan render-pass-compatibility gap noticed but not exercised by this task's own new test** (stride-16 `colored3d` correctly has the `msaa`-aware render-pass ternary; the stride-20/24 `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D` sibling does not — `pci.renderPass = (colorAttachmentCount<=1) ? renderPass_ : GetOrCreateMRTRenderPass(...)` unconditionally, missing the `(msaa && renderPassMsaa_)` check every other pipeline-creation function has): would produce the identical `rasterizationSamples>1`-vs-`renderPass_`-is-1-sample mismatch as the fix above the moment any existing or future test combines backbuffer MSAA with a `textured3d`/`colored_textured3d` draw; no current test exercises this combination. Not fixed here (unrelated to this task's own new test, which uses the already-correct stride-16 `colored3d` pipeline) — tracked as new Task 904. |
| 901 | Fix `BgfxGraphicsBackend::EnsureViewState()` clobbering a bound `RenderTarget2D`'s own viewport/ortho-projection back to the full window size (Task 878/879 finding) | ✅ | **Fixed as a hard prerequisite of Task 878/879's Bgfx MSAA test.** `EnsureViewState()` (called from `Clear()`/`SubmitSprite()`) unconditionally did `bgfx::setViewRect(spriteViewId, 0, 0, cachedWidth, cachedHeight)` and built its 2D ortho-projection matrix from `cachedWidth`/`cachedHeight` (the *window's* size) regardless of whether `spriteViewId` was currently bound to the backbuffer or a `RenderTarget2D` — silently overwriting `BindAsRenderTarget()`'s correctly RT-sized `bgfx::setViewRect()` call the very next time `Clear()` ran on that RT, since the 3D and 2D dispatch paths share `spriteViewId`/`currentViewId_` (per `ApplyViewportOverride()`'s existing comment, which already documented the *intent* for RT passes to keep their own full-RT-size default — the implementation just never actually checked). Concretely: any triangle/quad drawn into an RT smaller than the window rasterized into a viewport far larger than the RT's actual texture, so only a tiny, badly-distorted corner of the intended geometry ever landed inside the RT's real pixel bounds. Invisible until now because no earlier Bgfx test both rendered into a differently-sized RT AND could pixel-verify the result (blocked by Task 873, fixed in the same pass as this). Fixed by adding `currentRtWidth_`/`currentRtHeight_` tracking (set in `SetRenderTarget2D`/`SetRenderTargets` from the bound RT's real size, zeroed when targeting the backbuffer) and using those instead of `cachedWidth`/`cachedHeight` for both the view rect and the ortho matrix whenever `spriteViewId != 0`. Confirmed via direct pixel-value dumps: before the fix, a differential test's expected 15-white/17-black diagonal-edge row came back uniformly all-255 (white) — the RT's actual 32×32 content was a tiny sliver of solid-white triangle interior, not the intended half-white/half-black split; after the fix, the row matches the predicted pattern exactly. |
| 902 | Fix `GraphicsDeviceManager.PreferMultiSampling`/general preference-driven `PresentationParameters` changes never reaching the Vulkan (or any) backend's actual construction — needs a real `GraphicsDevice.Reset()` (Task 878/879 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real `GraphicsDevice::Reset()` now reaches the backend; `vulkan_msaa_test.cpp`'s long-standing false positive fixed and upgraded to a genuinely discriminating test.** Read FNA's actual `GraphicsDevice.Reset()` (`FNA/src/Graphics/GraphicsDevice.cs`) before designing the fix: real FNA does an **in-place** `FNA3D_ResetBackbuffer()` reconfiguration (resolution/MSAA/format/fullscreen/vsync), not a full device/resource teardown — and FNA's own per-resource `GraphicsDeviceResetting()` notification loop is dead code, commented out in FNA's own source (`/* FIXME: Why are we not doing this...? -flibit */`). This is a **narrower** scope than Task 902's original write-up assumed ("needs new DeviceResetting/DeviceReset events on every live IGraphicsResource") — no resource-invalidation system was needed, only an honest backend-level MSAA reconfiguration. **New `IGraphicsBackend` virtuals** (`include/CNA/Internal/Backends/Common/IGraphicsBackend.hpp`): `virtual int ApplyMultiSampleCount(int requested)` (default: echoes back `GetMultiSampleCount()`, i.e. unsupported backends silently report their unchanged real value, never lie) and `virtual int GetMultiSampleCount() const` (default 0). **Vulkan implementation** (`VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplyMultiSampleCount`, `src/CNA/Internal/Backends/Vulkan/VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp`): clamps via the existing `PickSampleCount()`, early-returns if unchanged, `vkDeviceWaitIdle`, destroys `pipeline2DMsaa_`/`renderPassMsaa_`/`rtRenderPassMsaa_` and clears all 9 lazily-created 3D pipeline caches (each `VkPipeline` bakes in `rasterizationSamples` at creation — cheaper to let them lazily rebuild on next use than to thread an msaa-bit-aware partial-clear through 9 different key encodings), updates `sampleCount_`, and — critically, in this order — eagerly recreates `renderPassMsaa_`+`pipeline2DMsaa_` **before** calling `RecreateSwapchain()` when transitioning to MSAA-enabled, since `CreateFramebuffers()` (called internally by `RecreateSwapchain()`) reads `renderPassMsaa_` directly whenever `sampleCount_>1`. Deliberately scoped to backbuffer-only MSAA — already-live `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` instances keep whatever `MultiSampleCount` they engaged at their own construction time (piggybacked on the backend's `sampleCount_` per the Task 878/879 scope decision); a cascading invalidation of live render targets is a separate, not-yet-needed follow-up. **`GraphicsDevice::Reset(pp, adapter)`** (`src/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/GraphicsDevice.cpp`): after `backend_->SetVirtualResolution(...)`, now calls `backend_->ApplyMultiSampleCount(presentationParameters_.getMultiSampleCountProperty())` and writes the real, device-clamped return value back into `presentationParameters_` — mirrors FNA's own `PresentationParameters.MultiSampleCount = FNA3D_GetMaxMultiSampleCount(...)` write-back after `FNA3D_ResetBackbuffer()`. Also made `applyPresentationParametersToWindow()`'s `SDL_SetWindowFullscreen` failure non-fatal (`SDL_ClearError()` instead of throw) to match `GraphicsDeviceManager`'s own already-established Task 224 handling for headless/Xvfb environments, since this method now supersedes that duplicated logic. **`GraphicsDeviceManager::applyToExistingBackend()`** (`src/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/GraphicsDeviceManager.cpp`): replaced the manual `SetPresentationParameters()` + duplicated `SDL_SetWindowFullscreen`/`SDL_SetWindowSize` calls + the long-commented-out `// graphicsDevice_->Reset(...)` with a real call to `graphicsDevice_->Reset(pp, *gdi.getAdapterProperty())`, matching FNA's own `GraphicsDeviceManager.ApplyChanges()` (which calls `graphicsDevice.Reset(...)` directly). `SetPresentationMode()` must run **before** `Reset()`, not after — SDL_Renderer's logical-presentation-size computation depends on which mode is already active, and reordering this (found via a real regression, see below) left a stale logical size computed under the previous mode. Removed the now-dead `makeSdlError()` helper (only caller was the deleted manual fullscreen/resize block). **EasyGL's main `EasyGLGraphicsBackend`** gained a `GetMultiSampleCount()` override (`sampleCount_ > 1 ? sampleCount_ : 0`) — it previously had no override at all (unlike its RT/RT-cube siblings from Task 878/879), silently reporting the interface default of `0` even when it legitimately engaged MSAA at construction; `ApplyMultiSampleCount()` uses the interface default (echo current value) since EasyGL genuinely cannot reconfigure its GL context's MSAA post-construction. **Real regressions found and fixed while verifying (via full 4-backend regression, not just Vulkan)**: (1) `easygl_msaa_change_test.cpp` (Task 229) asserted PP stores the *requested* `MultiSampleCount=8` after `ApplyChanges()` on an already-constructed device with `preferMultiSampling=true` — this is now honestly `0` (EasyGL can't retroactively engage MSAA), so the test's assertion and explanatory comment were updated to match the new, more FNA-faithful "report what's real, not what was asked for" semantics; 9/9 checks pass. (2) SDL_Renderer: reordering `Reset()` before `SetPresentationMode()` (my first attempt) broke `SDL_Renderer_Readback`/`SDL_Renderer_SpriteBatch_Overloads`/`SDL_Renderer_SpriteBatch_Rotation` (all 3 use `PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer`) with `ReadBackbuffer: physical/logical size mismatch` — root-caused to `SetVirtualResolution` computing a logical size while still in the stale default `FixedHeightDynamicWidth` mode; fixed by restoring `SetPresentationMode()` before `Reset()` (see above), confirmed all 3 pass again. **`vulkan_msaa_test.cpp` (Task 147) rewritten**: the original solid-red-fill assertion couldn't discriminate real MSAA from silently-ignored MSAA (matches the exact weakness Task 878/879 already solved for RenderTarget2D via a diagonal-edge differential). Rewritten to draw a diagonal-edged triangle straight to the backbuffer, read back a centre row, and assert a hard binary transition at `MultiSampleCount=0` vs. genuinely blended intermediate pixels at `MultiSampleCount=8` — toggled via the **real** `GraphicsDeviceManager.setPreferMultiSamplingProperty(true)` + `ApplyChanges()` on the already-constructed device (deliberately not using the `RecreateBackendForMultiSampleCount()` NOXNA escape hatch other sibling MSAA tests had to add as a Task-902 workaround), proving the genuine end-to-end runtime path. **Discriminating power confirmed via `git stash`**: reverting all 6 production files (keeping only the new test) reproduced the exact predicted failure (`MultiSampleCount=8` row purely binary, exit code 1); restoring the fix passes both checks again (`[Vulkan] MultiSampleCount reset to 4×` — llvmpipe's software Vulkan clamped 8→4 in this sandbox, irrelevant to the test's any-blending assertion). **Full 4-backend regression, each independently `git stash`-verified against its own confirmed pre-existing baseline**: Vulkan `ctest` 4362/4377 (15 pre-existing failures: the already-documented 12 state-test failures + 3 newly-confirmed-pre-existing `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest` segfaults, all reproduced identically with changes stashed), `CnaTests` clean; EasyGL `ctest` 4430/4433 (3 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests` — all reproduced identically stashed), `CnaTests` 4281/4283 passed, 2 skipped; SDL_Renderer `ctest` 4275/4288 (13 pre-existing gtest failures, all reproduced identically stashed); Bgfx `ctest` 4337/4339 (2 pre-existing — `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`/`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`, both this sandbox's already-documented GL2.1-vs-Vulkan DRI3 negotiation flakiness, confirmed by forcing `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN` manually and observing a clean pass). Zero new failures introduced on any backend. `GraphicsDevice::RecreateBackendForMultiSampleCount()` (the NOXNA test-only escape hatch Task 878/879 added) is left in place — still used by `vulkan_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp`/`vulkan_rendertargetcube_msaa_test.cpp`/`vulkan_basiceffect_textured_msaa_test.cpp`, which test per-RT MSAA independent of backbuffer MSAA and don't need this task's fix. |
| 903 | Implement `RenderTargetCube` MSAA support on Vulkan and Bgfx (split out of Task 879) | ✅ | **Closed on both backends, each mirroring its own `RenderTarget2D` sibling's exact technique (Task 878/879), applied per cube face via a shared MSAA color image (same "share one resource across all 6 faces" pattern this class already uses for its depth buffer).** **Vulkan**: `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` gained a `requestedMultiSampleCount` constructor param, a `GetMultiSampleCount()` override, and — when engaged — promotes the shared depth image to `owner_->sampleCount_` samples, allocates one shared `msaaColorImage_`/`msaaColorView_` (TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT, reused across all 6 faces the same way `depthImage_` already is, since only one face is ever bound at a time), and builds each face's framebuffer against `rtRenderPassMsaa_` (att0=MSAA color, att1=resolve into that face's own `faceViews_[face]`, att2=MSAA depth) instead of `rtRenderPass_`, mirroring `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`'s mutually-exclusive `framebuffer_`/`msaaFramebuffer_` shape exactly. Each `FaceProxy` gained `msaaFramebuffer`/`msaaRenderPass` fields plus a `WantsMsaa()` override — no changes needed to `RecordCommandBuffer` itself, since it already dispatches purely through the generic `VulkanRTSource` interface. **Bgfx**: `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` gained a `requestedMultiSampleCount` param and a `GetMultiSampleCount()` override; `cubeTex`/`depthTex`'s creation flags changed from plain `BGFX_TEXTURE_RT` to `BgfxMsaaRtFlag(...)`'s result (the exact same helper `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` already uses) — bgfx resolves the multisampled cube content internally into the same texture handle, no separate resolve texture needed. **Found and fixed a real, previously-undiscovered Vulkan bug while writing the verification test**: the 2D sprite pipeline's MSAA-variant selection (`drawSpritesFor`'s `useMsaaPipe`) only ever checked `targetRT == nullptr && sampleCount_ > 1` — i.e. the *backbuffer's* own MSAA state — never an RT's, meaning a `SpriteBatch` fill into ANY MSAA-enabled `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` face (bound the non-MSAA 2D pipeline against the RT's real 3-attachment MSAA render pass) produced live `VUID-vkCmdDraw-multisampledRenderToSingleSampled-07284`/`VUID-vkCmdDraw-renderPass-02684` validation errors — invisible until now because every prior MSAA RT test (Task 878/879) filled via a 3D `BasicEffect` draw instead, whose own pipeline selection already checked `rt->WantsMsaa()` correctly; this task's own cube-face fills use `SpriteBatch`, matching Task 907's established face-filling convention, and were the first to exercise this path. Fixed by checking `targetRT->WantsMsaa()` when targeting an RT (mirrors the 3D path's existing check exactly) instead of only ever checking the backbuffer's state — this same fix also silently corrects the identical latent gap for `RenderTarget2D` MSAA + `SpriteBatch` fills, not just the new `RenderTargetCube` case. **Test design, deliberately scoped down from a genuine sub-pixel AA differential** (unlike `{vulkan,bgfx}_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp`'s diagonal-edge methodology): Task 907's own mip-chain test already discovered and documented that `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s reflection-vector sampling of a flat, fixed-normal quad can't be used to sweep across a specific cube face's UV space the way direct 2D texture sampling can (no equivalent of "shrink the destination rectangle to force a coarser LOD/specific UV point"); independently re-derived and empirically re-confirmed here (with `World=View=Projection=Identity` and vertex normal fixed at `(0,0,1)`, the reflection direction algebraically reduces to `normalize(vertexPosition.xy, 0)` — it has a zero Z component almost everywhere on the quad, meaning this technique overwhelmingly samples the cube's four side faces, not whichever face a diagonal split would need to sweep across; confirmed via a probe that filled `+Z` with a distinguishing pattern and read a 9×5 grid of backbuffer points — every point read the *other 5* faces' solid colour, never the distinguishing one, even at the exact centre). New tests `{vulkan,bgfx}_rendertargetcube_msaa_test.cpp` therefore check: (1) `GetMultiSampleCount()` property fidelity (0 stays 0; 8 reports a real nonzero device-clamped value — this is the actual, provable bug this task's title describes: both backends' `RenderTargetCube` backend previously never overrode this at all, always returning the base class's default of 0 regardless of what was requested) and (2) a no-corruption/no-crash sanity check (fill all 6 faces solid blue, sample the centre back via the established Task 334/907 technique, confirm real content came back), matching Task 907's own identical scope-limitation precedent. **A second real, already-documented issue was hit while writing the Bgfx test, not a new bug**: without a `bgfx::frame()` boundary forced between each face's fill, the centre sample read back a wrong, non-blue value — this is Task 910's already-tracked "all 6 faces share one hardcoded bgfx view id, only the last-bound face actually renders" limitation; fixed by applying the same established dummy-backbuffer-read-between-faces workaround `bgfx_rendertargetcube_mip_test.cpp` already uses. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverting all 4 Vulkan+Bgfx backend files reproduced the exact predicted failures on both (`MultiSampleCount request 8 -> applied 0` on both backends; Vulkan's validation-error-producing pipeline mismatch also reproduced before the sprite-pipeline fix was applied); restored and reconfirmed all 3 checks PASS on both, zero validation errors. Full regression: `CnaTests` Vulkan/Bgfx exact baseline match (Vulkan 4272/4274, Bgfx 4276/4278), 0 failed; targeted `ctest -R "RenderTarget\|Mip\|Msaa\|MultiSample"` Vulkan 67/67 (100%), Bgfx 59/60 (1 failure is the already-documented, pre-existing `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb/DRI3 environment limitation, unrelated). |
| 904 | Fix `VulkanGraphicsBackend::GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`'s missing `msaa`-aware render-pass selection (`textured3d`/`colored_textured3d`, stride 20/24) — found while implementing Task 878/879, not exercised by its own test | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, confirmed, latent bug fixed exactly per this row's own predicted fix shape.** Added the missing `(msaa && renderPassMsaa_) ? renderPassMsaa_ : renderPass_` ternary to `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`'s `pci.renderPass` assignment (previously unconditionally `renderPass_` when `colorAttachmentCount<=1`), mirroring every sibling pipeline-creation function's already-correct pattern (e.g. `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`, right above it in the same file) exactly. New `Vulkan_BasicEffect_TexturedMsaa` test (direct port of Task 366's `vulkan_basiceffect_texture_enabled_test.cpp` with backbuffer MSAA forced via the `GraphicsDevice::RecreateBackendForMultiSampleCount(8)` NOXNA hook, since `GraphicsDeviceManager.PreferMultiSampling` never reaches the Vulkan backend — Task 902): stride-20 `BasicEffect`+`Texture2D` draw with `MultiSampleCount=8`, pixel result `(160,40,30)` matches the un-MSAA'd baseline exactly. **Discriminating power confirmed via the Vulkan validation layer, not the pixel assertion alone** (matches this row's own prediction, and Task 905's identical precedent that this class of render-pass-compatibility mismatch doesn't necessarily corrupt pixel output on a permissive driver): `git stash`-reverting the one-line fix and rebuilding reproduced the exact predicted validation errors — `VUID-VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo-multisampledRenderToSingleSampled-06853` (`rasterizationSamples (4) does not match the number of samples of the RenderPass... (1)`) at pipeline creation, plus `VUID-vkCmdDraw-renderPass-02684` (render-pass incompatibility) at draw time — while the test's own pixel assertion still passed either way, confirming this bug is real regardless of whether it happens to corrupt output on any given driver. Full regression: `CnaTests` (Vulkan, filtered) 4262/4264 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); spot-run `Vulkan_BasicEffect_TextureEnabled`/`Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` unchanged. Historical analysis retained below: **Confirmed real, latent, not yet exercised by any existing or new test.** Every other Vulkan 3D pipeline-creation function (`GetOrCreatePipeline3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineAlphaTest3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineEnvMap3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineSkinned3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineLitTextured3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D`) selects `pci.renderPass` via `(colorAttachmentCount>1) ? GetOrCreateMRTRenderPass(...) : (msaa && renderPassMsaa_) ? renderPassMsaa_ : renderPass_` (or the equivalent `if`-shaped form) — but `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D` (the stride-20 `textured3d`/stride-24 `colored_textured3d` pipeline, Task 899's fog-bundle sibling of the already-correct `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`) instead does `pci.renderPass = (colorAttachmentCount<=1) ? renderPass_ : GetOrCreateMRTRenderPass(...)` unconditionally — `ms.rasterizationSamples` (set correctly a few lines earlier via the standard `(msaa && colorAttachmentCount<=1) ? sampleCount_ : VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT` formula) can therefore end up `>1` while `pci.renderPass` is `renderPass_` (a 1-sample render pass) — a genuine `VkPipelineMultisampleStateCreateInfo`/render-pass sample-count mismatch, the same class of validation failure Task 878/879 had to fix for the new `rtRenderPassMsaa_` render pass. Not exercised by Task 878/879's own new test (uses `BasicEffect.VertexColorEnabled` with no texture, stride 16, routing to the already-correct `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`) nor by any existing test (none combine backbuffer MSAA with a `textured3d`/`colored_textured3d` draw). Fix shape: add the same `(msaa && renderPassMsaa_) ? renderPassMsaa_ : ...` check this function's `colorAttachmentCount<=1` branch is missing, mirroring every sibling pipeline-creation function's already-correct pattern exactly. Not fixed here — needs its own new discriminating test (backbuffer MSAA + a `DualTextureEffect`/textured `BasicEffect` draw) to verify, deliberately out of this task's own scope. |
| 905 | Fix `VulkanGraphicsBackend::CreateRTRenderPass()`'s subpass dependencies not actually matching `CreateRenderPass()`'s despite a comment claiming they did — found during independent review/verification of Task 878/879's diff | ✅ | **Found and fixed during my own independent review pass of the (uncommitted, background-agent-authored) Task 878/879 diff — not part of that diff itself.** Running the new `vulkan_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp` produced correct pixel results but also 5 live `[Vulkan Validation]` `VUID-vkCmdDraw-renderPass-02684` errors ("pDependencies[0].srcStageMask is incompatible ... FRAGMENT_SHADER_BIT != FRAGMENT_SHADER_BIT\|EARLY_FRAGMENT_TESTS_BIT\|LATE_FRAGMENT_TESTS_BIT" and similar for access masks) — a real spec violation the test's own binary exit code doesn't catch. Root cause, confirmed 100% pre-existing (outside every hunk of the Task 878/879 diff, per `git diff` hunk boundaries): `CreateRenderPass()` (the backbuffer's non-MSAA `renderPass_`) has a wide `deps[0].srcStageMask = FRAGMENT_SHADER_BIT\|EARLY_FRAGMENT_TESTS_BIT\|LATE_FRAGMENT_TESTS_BIT` (plus matching depth-scoped access masks and a `deps[1]` `TRANSFER_BIT`/`TRANSFER_READ_BIT` scope) — and its own comment claims "Use the same two subpass dependencies as rtRenderPass_ so that pipelines created against renderPass_ are also compatible with rtRenderPass_." But `CreateRTRenderPass()`'s actual `deps[]` was narrower (`srcStageMask = FRAGMENT_SHADER_BIT` only, no depth scope, no transfer scope) — the comment's stated intent was never actually implemented, so `renderPass_` and `rtRenderPass_`/`rtRenderPassLoad_` were never truly render-pass-compatible. Invisible until now because `GetOrCreatePipeline3D`'s `msaa=false` variant (created against `renderPass_`) is only reused against `rtRenderPass_` when a depth-tested 3D primitive is drawn into a plain (non-MSAA) `RenderTarget2D` — Task 878/879's new test is the first one to do exactly that (its `MultiSampleCount=0` comparison call). Fix: widened `CreateRTRenderPass()`'s `deps[0]`/`deps[1]` to be byte-identical to `CreateRenderPass()`'s (both feed the same `VkRenderPassCreateInfo`, so this also fixes `rtRenderPassLoad_`, its `LOAD_OP_LOAD` sibling created from the same `deps[]` array). Verified: validation errors gone (confirmed via a clean rerun with zero `[Vulkan Validation]` output), both `vulkan_rendertarget2d_msaa_test` checks still PASS, full `CnaTests --gtest_brief=1` still 3493 passed/2 skipped (exact baseline match), `ctest -R Vulkan` still 68/80 (identical 12 pre-existing failures, zero new ones, including the new `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` passing). |
| 906 | Implement `RenderTarget2D` mip support on Bgfx (split out of Task 878) | ✅ | **CLOSED — real feature implemented and pixel-verified, and it turns out to be a ONE-LINE fix, correcting Task 878's own research finding.** Task 878's row claimed "bgfx has no `glGenerateMipmap` equivalent... Real mip generation on Bgfx therefore needs a genuine render-to-mip-level downsample pass" (a new shader/geometry/per-level-framebuffer undertaking). That claim is **wrong** — it was only true of `bgfx::blit()` (confirmed still a same-size copy, no resize/filter). bgfx has a real, built-in, automatic mip-generation mechanism, just gated behind a *framebuffer attachment* resolve flag rather than exposed on the texture-copy API surface: `bgfx::Attachment::init()`'s `_resolve` parameter defaults to `BGFX_RESOLVE_AUTO_GEN_MIPS` (confirmed in `bgfx/bgfx.h`), and the simple `bgfx::createFrameBuffer(numAttachments, TextureHandle*, destroyTextures)` overload **already sets this flag automatically for any attachment whose texture has mips** (confirmed directly in `bgfx.cpp`: `at.init(..., !ref.hasMips() || ref.isDepth() ? BGFX_RESOLVE_NONE : BGFX_RESOLVE_AUTO_GEN_MIPS)`) — exactly the overload `BgfxRenderTargetBackend::CreateResources()` already uses, unmodified. bgfx triggers the actual resolve (`TextureGL::resolve()` → `glGenerateMipmap`, confirmed in `renderer_gl.cpp`, with equivalent code paths in every other bgfx renderer backend — `renderer_vk.cpp`, `renderer_d3d11.cpp`, `renderer_d3d12.cpp`, `renderer_mtl.cpp`, `renderer_webgpu.cpp`) automatically inside `setFrameBuffer()` every time bgfx switches away from a framebuffer — precisely the moment `RenderTarget2D`'s own `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)` reaches. **The entire fix**: `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`'s constructor gained a `bool mipMap` parameter, threaded through from `CreateRenderTarget2D` (previously accepted-and-ignored), and `colorTex`'s `bgfx::createTexture2D(...)` call's `hasMips` argument changed from hardcoded `false` to `mipMap`. No shader, no geometry, no per-level framebuffers, no C++ resolve-triggering code at all — a spike-and-revert false start (2 new `.sc` shader files, a `compile_shaders.py` entry, bgfx's `shaderc` tool built from scratch) was cleanly reverted once this was discovered, before landing the real 2-line fix. New `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` test (direct port of `vulkan_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp`'s asymmetric-7:1-split/forced-centre-sample methodology, Task 878): both checks pass, `sample=(224,0,32)` matching the predicted `(223,0,32)` weighted average. **A separate, real Bgfx test-harness finding surfaced while writing this test, unrelated to the mip fix itself** (reproduces identically with `mipMap=false`): sampling the *same* already-rendered `RenderTarget2D`/`Texture2D` object via `SpriteBatch` across more than one independent `Clear`+`Draw`+`GetBackBufferData` cycle only reliably produces fresh data on the *first* such cycle — every later cycle reads back garbage/black no matter how many retries. Every other multi-checkpoint Bgfx pixel test in this project already avoids this by constructing a **fresh** `RenderTarget2D` per checkpoint (e.g. `bgfx_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp`'s `RenderAndReadRow`) and doing exactly **one** `GetBackBufferData` call per independent render — this test's first draft violated both conventions (reused one persistent RT across 3 checkpoints, read 3 separate single-pixel rects from it) and was rewritten to match the established pattern (fresh RT per checkpoint; check 1's two sample points read via one single-column `GetBackBufferData` call instead of two separate point reads) before it produced reliable results. Not investigated further as its own bug (matches this project's "no opportunistic fixes" discipline) — logged here as a reusable diagnosis for the next person who hits it. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted `BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`, rebuilt, reran — check 1 (level-0-only) still passed unaffected, check 2 failed with `sample=(176,93,114)` (a corrupted/edge-clamped value, not pure red — bgfx's own minification behavior on a genuinely single-level texture differs slightly from Vulkan's pre-fix symptom, but is clearly wrong either way); restored and reconfirmed both checks pass. **Full regression**: `CnaTests` (Bgfx) 4275/4277 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); spot-run `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` (unaffected, same pre-existing Xvfb/DRI3-environment-only failure when run outside `ctest`'s `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN` override), `Bgfx_Viewport_Subregion`, `Bgfx_DualTextureEffect_Blend` all pass unchanged. |
| 907 | Implement `RenderTargetCube` mip support on Vulkan and Bgfx (split out of Task 878's original "RenderTarget2D/RenderTargetCube" scope) | ✅ | **CLOSED on both backends — real feature implemented, each mirroring its own `RenderTarget2D` sibling's exact technique (Task 878 Vulkan / Task 906 Bgfx), applied per cube face/array-layer instead of a single 2D image.** **Vulkan**: `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` gained a `bool mipMap` constructor param; `image_` (the shared 6-layer cube-compatible image) now allocates `levelCount_` mips instead of hardcoded `1`; `cubeView_` (the full-cube sampling view) now spans the full mip range instead of level 0 only (mirrors `VulkanRenderTargetBackend::colorSampleView_`'s identical Task 878 pattern); each `FaceProxy` (the per-face `VulkanRTSource` used by `RecordCommandBuffer`'s existing generic per-RT `MaybeGenerateMips` hook, unchanged from Task 878) gained a new `MaybeGenerateMips(VkCommandBuffer)` override doing a `vkCmdBlitImage` cascade scoped to `baseArrayLayer=faceIndex` — otherwise byte-for-byte the same barrier/blit sequence as the 2D case. **Found and fixed a real, confirmed prerequisite bug while building the mip test** (the identical class of gap Task 878 fixed for the 2D case, just never previously needed since this class's mip levels didn't exist before): unlike `RenderTarget2D`'s level 0 (which every real render pass transitions to `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` via its own `finalLayout`), a `RenderTargetCube` face's mip levels 1..levelCount-1 are *only* ever touched by the new blit cascade, whose own first barrier for each destination level assumed it already started in `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` — never true the very first time, since nothing else ever transitions those subresources. Produced live `VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-09600` validation errors (correct pixel output regardless) until fixed with a construction-time full-range (`levelCount_` levels × all 6 layers) initial `UNDEFINED`→`SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` transition, mirroring `VulkanRenderTargetBackend`'s identical Task 878 fix. New `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_MipChain` test (port of Task 334's sample test with `mipMap=true`): all 6 faces filled solid blue, sampled back via `EnvironmentMapEffect`, centre reads `(0,0,255)`. **Deliberate scope decision, tried harder first**: unlike `RenderTarget2D`'s asymmetric-7:1-split/forced-1x1-destination differential (Task 878), this test does NOT assert on coarser mip *levels'* content — an attempt at exactly that (an asymmetric split within one face + a shrunk backbuffer to force a coarse LOD via `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s reflection vector) was tried and abandoned after `git stash`-reverting the fix still passed identically, proving non-discrimination (no equivalent of "shrink the destination rectangle" exists for a reflection-vector-driven sample, and cube-map mips never blend across faces so a uniform-per-face pattern can't discriminate at any LOD either). The test's real value ended up being exactly what it caught: the validation-layer bug above, independently confirmed via `git stash` (reverting just the initial-transition addition reproduces the exact validation errors). **Bgfx**: `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` gained a `bool mipMap` constructor param threaded into `bgfx::createTextureCube`'s `hasMips` argument (previously hardcoded `false`) — the identical one-line mechanism as Task 906's `RenderTarget2D` fix; `BindAsRenderTargetFace`'s existing `bgfx::Attachment::init()` call already defaults `_resolve` to `BGFX_RESOLVE_AUTO_GEN_MIPS` unconditionally (confirmed in `bgfx.h`), so no other change was needed for the mip mechanism itself. **This session's first-ever exercise of Bgfx's `RenderTargetCube`-via-`EnvironmentMapEffect` path surfaced two more real, previously-unreported, unrelated bugs, both found and fixed as hard prerequisites** (this test literally could not pass without them): (1) **closes Task 874** — `BgfxGraphicsBackend`'s `EnvironmentMapEffect` dispatch did an unsafe `static_cast<const BgfxTextureCubeBackend&>` on whatever `ITextureCubeBackend` it was handed, reading `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend::fbo` (a framebuffer-pool handle) where `BgfxTextureCubeBackend::handle` (a texture-pool handle) was expected whenever the argument was actually a `RenderTargetCube` — the identical bug shape Task 873 already fixed for `RenderTarget2D`. Fixed via a new `IBgfxCubeSamplable` interface (mirroring `IBgfxSamplable`) implemented by both concrete classes, dispatched through a safe `dynamic_cast`. (2) **A second, previously-unreported gap**: the shared `IGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargetCubeFace` default only calls `BindAsRenderTargetFace` — it never updates `currentRtWidth_`/`currentRtHeight_` (the state `EnsureViewState()` uses to size the 2D ortho/viewport for `SpriteBatch` draws), the identical bug shape Task 901 already fixed for 2D `RenderTarget2D` — meaning every `SpriteBatch` draw into a cube face was rasterizing into a viewport sized to the full window instead of the face's own size. Fixed by overriding `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargetCubeFace` to also set these, mirroring `SetRenderTarget2D`'s own pattern. **A third, genuinely architectural bug was found — root cause fully isolated this time, unlike Task 876's still-open Vulkan analogue, but NOT fixed here, split to new Task 910**: rendering into all 6 cube faces within a single un-advanced bgfx frame (exactly how this test's own fill loop — and any real game refreshing a reflection probe once per game-frame — would naturally do it) only actually renders into whichever face's framebuffer was bound *last*, because `bgfx::setViewFrameBuffer(viewId, fbo)` is a per-view, per-*frame* setting, not a per-submit-call one, and all 6 faces share the same hardcoded view id (1) in `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend::BindAsRenderTargetFace`. Confirmed directly: forcing a `bgfx::frame()` boundary between each face's fill (a throwaway `GetBackBufferData` call) makes every face render correctly; removing it reproduces the original "5 of 6 faces are garbage" symptom exactly. New `Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_MipChain` test (same structure as the Vulkan one) uses this per-face frame-boundary workaround to get real content into all 6 faces at all, and documents the finding in its own header comment. **Full regression, both backends**: Vulkan `CnaTests` (filtered) 4262/4264 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); spot-run `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_{Sample,Properties}` unchanged. Bgfx `CnaTests` 4275/4277 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match); spot-run `cna_test_bgfx_env_map` (regular `TextureCube`, unaffected) still passes. `git stash` revert-and-refail independently confirmed discriminating power on both backends. |
| 910 | Fix Bgfx: rendering into all 6 `RenderTargetCube` faces within one un-advanced bgfx frame only actually renders into the last-bound face (found while building Task 907's Bgfx mip test) | ✅ | **CLOSED — every render target (2D, cube, MRT) now gets its own distinct, stable bgfx view id instead of sharing one hardcoded id 1.** Root cause (already isolated by Task 907's own investigation): `bgfx::setViewFrameBuffer(viewId, fbo)` is a per-view-per-*frame* setting, resolved once at `bgfx::frame()` — not per `bgfx::submit()`/`bgfx::touch()` call — so whichever value was set *last* for a shared view id before the frame flushes is what every draw submitted to that view that frame actually renders into, regardless of call order. **Fix**: new `Detail::AllocateRtViewId()`/`ReleaseRtViewId()` free-list-backed pool (`BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp`) — view id 0 stays permanently reserved for the backbuffer; ids `[1, 256)` are allocated on demand (mirrors bgfx's own internal `BGFX_CONFIG_MAX_VIEWS` default, not part of the public `bgfx.h` API so hardcoded with a comment rather than `#include`d). `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`/`BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` each gained a `viewId_` member, allocated in their constructor (stable for the RT's whole lifetime, survives across frames) and released in their destructor — free-list reuse means long-running games creating/destroying render targets don't exhaust the ~255 available ids. `BindAsRenderTarget()`/`UnbindAsRenderTarget()`/`BindAsRenderTargetFace()` now use `viewId_` instead of the old hardcoded `1`. `SetRenderTarget2D()`/`SetRenderTargetCubeFace()` set `currentViewId_`/`spriteViewId` to the bound RT's own `viewId_` (cast to the concrete Bgfx type, matching this file's existing `SetRenderTargets()` cast convention — no shared `IRenderTargetBackend`/`IRenderTargetCubeBackend` interface change needed, this is Bgfx-internal plumbing). `SetRenderTargets()` (MRT, count>1) allocates its own fresh `mrtViewId_` each call (released whenever `mrtFbo_` is torn down, mirroring that member's own existing lifecycle) so two *different* MRT setups bound within one frame don't clobber each other either. `EnsureViewState()`/`ApplyViewportOverride()` needed zero changes — both already branch on `spriteViewId != 0`/`currentViewId_ == 0` ("is this the backbuffer"), not on the specific value 1, so any non-zero per-RT id works unmodified. **New `bgfx_concurrent_rendertargets_test.cpp`** (`RenderTarget2D`, not cube — the more general case the old row's own write-up predicted would be affected too): primes `RT_A` to a known green baseline in an isolated frame, then — with NO `bgfx::frame()` boundary in between — binds+clears `RT_A` to red, binds+clears `RT_B` to blue, unbinds; reads back each RT in its own later frame (avoiding the unrelated, already-documented "`GetBackBufferData` only reflects the first read per frame" Bgfx quirk). **Discriminating power confirmed via `git stash`**: reverting just the 2 production Bgfx backend files reproduced the exact predicted failure — `RT_A` stayed at its primed green baseline `(0,255,0)` instead of turning red (the clear-to-red was silently redirected into `RT_B`'s framebuffer once the frame flushed), while `RT_B` still correctly read blue (matches "only the last-bound one wins"); restored and reconfirmed all 3 checks pass. Existing Task 907/903 cube tests' own "dummy-backbuffer-read-between-faces" Task-910 workaround is left in place (harmless now that it's no longer strictly necessary — not removed, to keep this change minimal and because those tests still pass unmodified). Full regression: Bgfx `ctest` 4338/4340 (2 pre-existing, already-documented `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_{MsaaResolve,MipChain}` environment flakiness, zero new failures), `CnaTests` 4285/4287 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed (exact baseline match). EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer `cmake -S . -B` reconfigured cleanly (CMakeLists.txt's new test registration is correctly scoped inside the existing Bgfx-only test block — no repeat of Task 915's mis-scoping mistake). |
| 908 | Fix 4 `RasterizerState::CullNone`-missing example files Task 896's own 119-file audit failed to catch (found while running §8 item 0's full `ctest` suite for the first time since Task 896) | ✅ | **Real, confirmed regression found and fixed — a genuine gap in Task 896's own "upfront audit" methodology, not a new bug this session introduced.** Running the full EasyGL `ctest` suite end-to-end for the first time since Task 896 (unblocked by this session's `CNA_TEST_DISPLAY` fix) surfaced 4 previously-undetected failures beyond the already-documented pre-existing list: `EasyGL_AvatarRenderer_RealRender`, `EasyGL_AvatarRenderer_AttachPart`, `EasyGL_AvatarRenderer_TintRouting` (all reading background-only, no rendered content at all — `centre=(0,255,0)`/`(0,0,0)` instead of the expected rendered colour), and `EasyGL_ModelDraw_RedQuad` (`centre=(0,255,0)` instead of red). All 4 source files (`avatar_real_render_integration_test.cpp`, `avatar_attach_part_integration_test.cpp`, `avatar_tint_routing_integration_test.cpp`, `easygl_model_draw_test.cpp`) predate Task 896 by a wide margin (Phase 10/Tasks 126–150 respectively — confirmed via `git log --diff-filter=A`), so they were in-scope for that task's audit and should have been caught, but weren't — genuinely missed, not newly broken by the 2026-07-07 merge or by Task 878. Root cause confirmed identical to every other Task 896 fix: each file draws a quad using raw NDC-space vertex positions with a CCW winding under CNA's real default `RasterizerState.CullMode = CullCounterClockwiseFace` (independently verified via the same 2D cross-product formula Task 896's own audit used, e.g. the `AvatarRealRender` quad's first triangle `(-1,1)→(-1,-1)→(0,-1)` has cross product `(0,-2)×(1,-2) = +2`, i.e. CCW), so every triangle in all 4 files was being silently culled — no fragments at all reached the framebuffer, leaving only the clear colour. Fixed identically to Task 896's own established pattern: added `#include ".../RasterizerState.hpp"` plus `device.setRasterizerStateProperty(RasterizerState::CullNone);` immediately after each file's existing `BlendState::Opaque` setup, before its first draw call. 3 of the 4 files (`avatar_real_render_integration_test.cpp`/`avatar_attach_part_integration_test.cpp`/`avatar_tint_routing_integration_test.cpp`) are shared source reused by both EasyGL's and Vulkan's `ctest` registrations (confirmed via `grep` on `CMakeLists.txt`) — rebuilt and reran all 3 on Vulkan too, all pass identically (previously also silently broken on Vulkan, just never caught since no prior full-suite run existed there either). `easygl_model_draw_test.cpp` is EasyGL-only. **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted `avatar_real_render_integration_test.cpp` alone, rebuilt, reran — reproduced the exact original failure (`centre=(0,255,0)` instead of `(255,0,0)`); restored and reconfirmed PASS. The other 3 files share the identical mechanism/fix shape (verified individually, not just by analogy) so a second isolated revert-and-refail pass was not repeated for each. |
| 909 | Fix 2 Vulkan-specific `BasicEffect` pixel tests actually broken by Task 896 itself (not merely missed by its audit, unlike Task 908) — found while running §8 item 0's full `ctest` suite for Vulkan | ✅ | **Real, confirmed regression — Task 896's own audit affirmatively examined these 2 files and reached the wrong conclusion, unlike Task 908's files which were simply never looked at.** Running the full Vulkan `ctest` suite end-to-end for the first time since Task 896 surfaced `Vulkan_BasicEffect_Specular` (1/5 → all of checks (a)/(b)/(c) reading pure black `(0,0,0)` instead of their expected non-zero values) and `Vulkan_BasicEffect_MultiLightEmissive` (0/3, same all-black symptom) — beyond the already-documented list of 12 pre-existing Vulkan failures (5× `BlendState`/Task 868, 5× `DepthStencilState`/Task 870, `ReferenceStencil`/Task 872, one `DepthBias` sub-case). Root cause: both test files (`vulkan_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp` — Task 886/898, `vulkan_basiceffect_multilight_emissive_test.cpp` — Task 897) carried an explicit comment, written when they were originally authored (both *before* Task 896 existed): "No `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround needed here (unlike Bgfx) — Vulkan's default cull state is effectively `CullNone`, matching EasyGL (Task 896 finding)." That claim was true *at the time* (Vulkan had no real default-`RasterizerState` GPU wiring at all yet) but Task 896 later made it false by pushing the real default (`CullCounterClockwiseFace`) to Vulkan's actual GPU state — silently culling both tests' CCW-wound quads ever since. Task 896's own 119-file audit evidently re-examined these 2 files (the stale comment shows deliberate reasoning, not silence) and incorrectly concluded no fix was needed, rather than simply missing them outright (Task 908's shape). Fixed identically to every other Task 896 row: added `#include ".../RasterizerState.hpp"` plus `dev.setRasterizerStateProperty(RasterizerState::CullNone);` right before each test's `fx.Apply()`/draw call, and rewrote the now-inaccurate comment to record what actually happened. **Discriminating power independently verified** on `vulkan_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`: `git stash`-reverted, rebuilt, reran — reproduced the exact original failure (1/5 PASS, checks (a)/(b)/(c) all `(0,0,0)`); restored and reconfirmed 5/5 PASS. `vulkan_basiceffect_multilight_emissive_test.cpp` shares the identical mechanism (verified directly by running it, not just by analogy) — now 3/3 PASS. |
| 880 | Wire `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` to a real GPU viewport on all 3 backends (Task 338 finding) | ✅ | **Fixed on all 3 backends, mirroring `ScissorRectangle`'s already-wired pattern exactly.** Added `virtual void SetViewport(int x, int y, int w, int h, float minDepth, float maxDepth) {}` to `IGraphicsBackend`; `GraphicsDevice::setViewportProperty()` now forwards to `backend_->SetViewport(...)` (`viewport_ = value; if (backend_) backend_->SetViewport(...)`, identical shape to `setScissorRectangleProperty()`). Also fixed the separate `UpdateViewportFromWindow()` path (window construction/resize), which mutates `viewport_`'s fields directly rather than through `setViewportProperty()` (a deliberate Task 349 precedent so it doesn't stomp a legitimately-custom sub-region `Viewport` on every resize-check) — added an explicit `backend_->SetViewport(0,0,width,height,0,1)` call there too, so a resize actually updates the GPU-side viewport, not just the C++ property. `ResetViewportAndScissorForRenderTarget` needed no separate fix — it already calls `setViewportProperty()`, so the backend-forwarding fix flows through automatically. **EasyGL**: added `SetViewport()` (Y-flip mirroring `SetScissorRect`) and removed `Clear()`/`ClearColorAndDepth()`'s hardcoded `device.set_viewport(0,0,windowW,windowH)` reset (glClear is viewport-independent — only the scissor rect, if enabled, narrows it — so it's safe to just stop resetting the viewport there, letting GL's own persistent viewport state survive across `Clear()`). **Found and fixed a real regression this surfaced immediately**: the naive Y-flip (`physH - y - h` using the *window's* physical height via `getPhysicalSize()`) is wrong whenever a `RenderTarget2D` is bound — it produced a GL viewport whose y-offset fell entirely outside the RT's actual pixel range (e.g. window 600px tall, RT 64px tall → viewport y-offset 536, but the FBO only has 64 real rows), discarding every fragment. This broke `EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer`/`EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` (2 new regressions caught by the full-suite rerun). Root cause: `SetScissorRect` had the *identical* latent bug (same `getPhysicalSize()`-based flip), just never manifesting since `ScissorTestEnable` is opt-in and no existing test combined a custom scissor with a bound RT. Fixed both `SetViewport` and `SetScissorRect` to use `currentRtHeight_` for the Y-flip when an RT is bound, falling back to the window's physical height otherwise — mirroring `ReadBackbuffer`'s already-established identical `fbH` pattern (`currentRtHeight_ == 0` ⇒ default framebuffer). **Vulkan**: added `viewportX_/Y_/W_/H_/MinDepth_/MaxDepth_` storage members (mirroring `scissorX_/Y_/W_/H_`'s pattern) and a storage-only `SetViewport()`; wired the **backbuffer pass** in `RecordCommandBuffer` to read the stored state (falling back to full swapchain extent when unset). **Deliberately left the RT pass hardcoded to each RT's own full size** — the deferred, potentially-multi-RT-per-frame command-buffer-recording architecture means a single frame-global stored viewport value cannot be correctly attributed to a *specific* RT's draws if the app used more than one differently-sized RT (or a custom sub-region Viewport followed by a different RT bind) within the same frame; this is the exact same limitation already accepted for `SetScissorRect`'s RT-pass hardcoding (a separately-tracked, pre-existing gap). **Bgfx**: added `viewportX_/Y_/W_/H_`/`viewportSet_` storage members and a storage-only `SetViewport()` (depth range intentionally ignored — Bgfx has no per-view depth-range knob, an acceptable deviation mirroring `SetVirtualResolution`'s documented no-op precedent); added a new private `ApplyViewportOverride()` helper called at the top of all 4 real 3D draw-dispatch entry points (`DrawColoredPrimitives`, `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`, `DrawPrimitivesEx`, `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`) that calls `bgfx::setViewRect(currentViewId_, ...)` right before the pipeline's state/submit calls, overriding whatever `EnsureViewState()` last set for that view — but **only when `currentViewId_ == 0`** (backbuffer), leaving RT passes at their `EnsureViewState()`/`BindAsRenderTarget()`-established full-RT-size default, for the identical reasoning as Vulkan's RT-pass scoping decision above (`currentViewId_`/`spriteViewId` are shared between the deferred 2D `SpriteBatch` path and this 3D path, so a per-RT custom Viewport can't be recovered from one frame-global value). Deliberately did **not** touch `EnsureViewState()` or the 2D `SpriteBatch` path on any backend — EasyGL's `SpriteBatch::FlushBatch()` already self-resets its own viewport to full window before every 2D flush (unaffected either way); Vulkan/Bgfx's 2D dispatch wasn't touched, so a custom 3D sub-region `Viewport` has no effect on 2D sprite rendering on any backend (matches `ScissorRectangle`'s pre-existing, real, identical 2D/3D asymmetry on Bgfx). **New tests, one per backend** (`easygl_viewport_subregion_test.cpp`, `vulkan_viewport_subregion_test.cpp`, `bgfx_viewport_subregion_test.cpp`): set a left-half sub-region `Viewport`, draw a full-NDC-range quad, confirm the left-half pixel is drawn and the right-half pixel stays background/black (not drawn outside the `Viewport`); then restore the full `Viewport` and confirm both halves draw; then a getter/setter round-trip check. **Bgfx-specific test-harness fix required**: the first version of the Bgfx test read 2 sample points per render pass and intermittently failed (`right-half restored` showing stale black) — root cause traced to `ReadBackbuffer`'s `bgfx::requestScreenShot()`+`bgfx::frame()` call sequence: each `GetBackBufferData` call itself advances/presents a bgfx frame, so a *second* read within the same logical Clear+Draw cycle observes an extra, empty `bgfx::frame()` with no new `submit()` queued (the already-documented "Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData()` only reliably reflects the first read per rendered frame" quirk, NEXT.md §5) — rewrote to do one full Clear+Draw+single-read pass per sample point (4 total passes instead of 2), matching every other multi-point Bgfx pixel test's established convention. **Discriminating power independently verified on all 3 backends**: `git stash`-reverted all 8 production files together (`IGraphicsBackend.hpp`, `GraphicsDevice.cpp`, both `.hpp`/`.cpp` for EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx), rebuilt each backend, reran each new test — all 3 failed exactly as predicted (EasyGL/Vulkan: right-half incorrectly drawn, 4/5; Bgfx: right-half-restored regressed to black, 4/5); restored and reconfirmed all 3 pass 5/5. **Full regression, all 3 backends**: EasyGL `ctest -j1` full suite 3636/3639 (exactly the 3 pre-existing, already-documented failures: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, zero new regressions after the RT-Y-flip fix above). Vulkan `Vulkan`-filtered suite 61/73 (exactly the 12 pre-existing, already-documented failures: 5× `Vulkan_BlendState_*`/Task 868, 5× `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_*`/Task 870, `Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`/Task 872, `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s one sub-case; `CnaTests` gtest suite 3493 passed/2 skipped). Bgfx full suite 3540/3540 (100%, zero failures at all — no pre-existing Bgfx baseline failures in this run). |
| 881 | Cap `SetRenderTargets` at FNA's real `MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS = 4` limit, consistently across all 3 backends (Task 339 finding) | ✅ | **Fixed exactly per this row's own predicted fix shape**: added an explicit check at the very top of `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTargets` (shared C++, before delegating to any backend, before `ResetViewportAndScissorForRenderTarget`/backend access) that throws `std::invalid_argument` when `renderTargets.size() > MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS` (a new file-local `constexpr std::size_t MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS = 4`, mirroring FNA's own `internal const int MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS = 4`). Used `std::invalid_argument` rather than the row's own tentative `System::ArgumentOutOfRangeException` guess, matching this project's already-established closer precedent: `SkinnedEffect::SetBoneTransforms` throws the identical `std::invalid_argument("... exceeds MaxBones.")` for the same "count exceeds a fixed max" shape (Task 402). Each backend's own pre-existing ad-hoc cap (`EasyGL`/`Bgfx`'s hardcoded 8, Vulkan's no-cap-at-all) is now unreachable in practice since the shared layer rejects anything over 4 before any backend ever sees it — left in place as defense-in-depth rather than removed, since removing them wasn't part of this row's own stated fix shape and they cause no harm now that they're unreachable. **New unit tests** (`GraphicsDeviceValidationTests.cpp`, `GraphicsDeviceValidationTest.SetRenderTargets_{FiveTargets_Throws,FourTargets_DoesNotThrow,OneTarget_DoesNotThrow,Empty_DoesNotThrow}`) using **real `RenderTarget2D` instances**, not default-constructed `RenderTargetBinding` — discovered while writing this test that CNA's `RenderTargetBinding` (unlike FNA's, whose only constructors throw `ArgumentNullException` on a null target) has a default constructor wrapping a null `Texture*`, and passing several null-target bindings through `SetRenderTargets` **segfaults** deeper in the function (a real, separate, unreported robustness gap — not fixed here, since a real game can never actually construct a null-target `RenderTargetBinding` the way FNA's API is shaped, so this is an unreachable-in-practice CNA-only deviation, not this task's concern). **Discriminating power independently verified**: `git stash`-reverted the one production file, rebuilt, reran — `SetRenderTargets_FiveTargets_Throws` failed exactly as predicted ("throws nothing"), the other 3 (all ≤4 targets) correctly kept passing; restored and reconfirmed all 4 pass. **Full regression**: EasyGL serial `-j1` 3635/3638 pass (same 3 pre-existing failures: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`); Vulkan/Bgfx sanity-rebuilt clean (shared, backend-agnostic file, no backend-specific regression risk). Closes the divergence on all 3 backends simultaneously via the single shared C++ gate. |
| 883 | Implement `Effect::Clone()` (Task 351 finding) | ✅ | **Closed — with a real, pre-existing surprise: 5 of the 8 concrete `Effect` subclasses (`AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `EnvironmentMapEffect`, `SkinnedEffect`, `SpriteEffect`) already had a working `Clone()` from an earlier, undocumented pass (this row was never updated to ✅ at the time) — this task's real remaining scope was making `Effect::Clone()` a genuine polymorphic contract and filling in the last 3 missing overrides.** User's scoping decision (already recorded, see `project_task883_896_decisions.md`): clone shares the same GPU shader/program (free by construction for CNA's 6 stock effects, since backend pipelines are cached globally by state, not per-`Effect`-instance — there is nothing per-instance to share/duplicate in the first place), gets its own independent `Parameters`/`Techniques`/`CurrentTechnique`, `owner_` back-pointers re-pointed at the clone. **The aliasing hazard this row originally worried about (`EffectPass::owner_`/`techniqueId_` pointing at the wrong `Effect`) turns out not to materialize with the established implementation pattern**: every concrete `Clone()` constructs the clone via `Effect(*src.device_)` (the base class's normal, non-clone constructor), which always builds a **fresh** "Default" `EffectTechnique`+`EffectPass` pair correctly owned by the new object (fresh `EffectTechnique::id_` too, via its own `NextId()` counter) — not a copy of the original's technique/pass at all. Confirmed by a new dedicated test (`EffectTest.CloneGetsIndependentTechniqueNotAliasedToOriginal`) that the clone's and original's passes are distinct objects and applying one never touches the other's `applyCount`. Made `Effect::Clone()` a real pure-virtual base contract (`[[nodiscard]] virtual Effect* Clone() = 0;`), added `override` to the 5 already-shipped concrete `Clone()` declarations (previously non-virtual — silently *not* polymorphic despite the class hierarchy, confirmed by a new `EffectTest.CloneThroughBasePointerDispatchesToDerivedOverride` base-pointer-dispatch test), and implemented the 3 remaining concrete subclasses discovered by the resulting build break: `BasicEffect` (the actual "stock effect" gap this row's title meant — private copy ctor + field-for-field copy of all 22 properties, new `BasicEffectDefaultsTest.CloneCopiesAllProperties` test covering every property plus independence-after-mutation both directions), `EffectMaterial` (the XNA/FNA `EffectMaterial(Effect cloneSource) : base(cloneSource)` construct-from-any-effect wrapper — `Clone()` just calls its own existing clone-shaped constructor on itself; needed an explicit `static_cast<Effect&>(*this)` since the compiler's overload resolution otherwise prefers the (deleted, since the base `Effect` copy ctor is `= delete`) implicit `EffectMaterial(const EffectMaterial&)` over the real `EffectMaterial(Effect&)` constructor — a real, non-obvious C++ overload-resolution gotcha, not a design choice), and `ShaderEffect` (a NOXNA CNA extension, not counted in "7 concrete stock-effect subclasses" — the pure-virtual base broke its `std::make_shared<ShaderEffect>()` call site in `ContentManager.cpp` too). **`ShaderEffect`'s `Clone()` is a deliberate, documented deviation from the shared-GPU-shader decision**: it uniquely owns a real per-instance compiled backend program (`std::unique_ptr<IEffectBackend>`, compiled from GLSL source at construction) rather than using CNA's global state-keyed pipeline cache like the 7 XNA-surface effects, so genuinely *sharing* the compiled program would need a reference-counted backend-ownership model; recompiling fresh from the same cached `vertSrc_`/`fragSrc_` strings instead is simple, correct, and out of this task's real scope to generalize further. Added `Clone()` unit-test coverage for the 3 effects that had none before this task (`BasicEffect`, `EffectMaterial`, `SpriteEffect` — the last already had a working `Clone()` but zero tests) plus a new `ShaderEffectTests.cpp` (the class had **no** test file at all before this task, for any method). **Verification**: full builds clean on all 3 backends (EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx, including every example executable — the compiler itself is the strongest check here, since a missed `override` on any 9th subclass would be a hard "abstract class" build error, not a silent gap); `CnaTests` EasyGL 3501/2 skipped (0 failed) and Bgfx 3505/2 skipped (0 failed), both exact-clean; Vulkan run under this session's new `Xvfb :99` + software (`llvmpipe`) fallback (adopted mid-session after real-display test runs disrupted the user's desktop — see `NEXT.md` §4) shows environment-flakiness unrelated to this task: 3 separate full-suite runs each failed a *different*, small (1–7), always Clone-unrelated set of tests (e.g. `DrawUserIndexedPrimitivesArgumentGuardTest`, unrelated `AlphaTestEffectDefaultsTest`/`BasicEffectDefaultsTest` property-default checks) — not reproducible, not tied to this diff, consistent with resource contention under rapid SDL-window creation on a software rasterizer; every Clone-specific test individually passed cleanly and reliably across all 3 runs. The pre-existing `easygl_effect_clone_test.cpp` (Task 184, `AlphaTestEffect`-based) and `easygl_effect_current_technique_test.cpp` continue to pass unchanged. |

No call-site changes needed anywhere in the codebase — every existing usage went through `operator[]`, `GetParameterBySemantic`, or range-`for` (never named the `iterator`/`const_iterator` type directly), confirmed by grep before editing. **New discriminating tests added to `EffectCollectionTests.cpp`**: `EffectParameterCollectionTest.PointerStableAcrossReallocatingAdd` and `EffectPassCollectionTest.PointerStableAcrossReallocatingAdd`, each taking `&col[0]` after one `Add()`, then forcing 64 more `Add()` calls (guaranteed to exceed any reasonable initial `vector` capacity and force reallocation), then asserting the original pointer/address is unchanged and still reads the original element's data. **Discriminating power independently verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild** (production files only, keeping the new tests): pre-fix, `EffectParameterCollectionTest.PointerStableAcrossReallocatingAdd` failed cleanly with a pointer-address mismatch (`first` vs `&col[0]` differed after reallocation, exactly as predicted); `EffectPassCollectionTest.PointerStableAcrossReallocatingAdd` **segfaulted** (a genuine use-after-free dereferencing `first->getNameProperty()` through a freed/moved-from address) — both confirming the hazard was real, not theoretical, for the exact mechanism the fix addresses. Restored the fix (`git stash pop`), rebuilt, reconfirmed both pass.
