| 440 | Document model-loading and content pipeline limitations                                | ✅      | **CLOSED — pure documentation, the final task in Phase 49 (mirrors Task 430's `docs/spritefont-support.md` and Task 731's `docs/sdl-renderer-2d-completeness.md` as the closing doc task for its own phase).** Wrote `docs/model-content-pipeline-support.md`. Summarizes Tasks 431-439's own runtime-API audit findings up front (all confirmed FNA-faithful, 1 documented safe deviation, Task 916 still open), then focuses on the piece those tasks never touched: **content loading**. Read FNA's real `ModelReader.cs` line-by-line and compared it against CNA's `ModelTypeReader` (`ContentManager.cpp`). **Key finding: CNA's Model content format is not FNA's binary `.xnb` format at all** — it's an original, NOXNA `.model.json` + raw-binary-sidecar scheme (no real XNA/FNA-produced `.xnb` model asset can ever be loaded). **Real, previously-undocumented gaps found in the loader itself** (documented, not fixed — pure docs task): the loader always synthesizes exactly ONE `ModelBone` regardless of how many the JSON's `"bones"` array actually lists (only the first entry's `"name"` is ever read) — no bone hierarchy support at all, even though the runtime API fully supports it after Tasks 431-439; every mesh is built via the OLD 3-arg constructor path, so `ModelMesh::ParentBone` is never assigned — notably, this is a gap even the just-shipped Task 439 fix doesn't close, since the loader doesn't call the new 4-arg `Model` constructor; `ModelMesh::BoundingSphere` is never populated (stays a degenerate zero-radius default for every loaded mesh); neither `Model::Tag` nor any `ModelMesh::Tag` is ever set; no `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer`/`Effect` resource sharing across meshes (FNA's real `ReadSharedResource` can dedupe, CNA always allocates fresh); no `existingInstance` hot-reload support (acceptable, FNA's own use of it is pipeline-tooling-only). **Confirmed via repo-wide search: zero test or example coverage of `ModelTypeReader`/`ContentManager::Load<Model>()` anywhere** — every one of Tasks 431-439's extensive tests hand-builds `Model`/`ModelMesh`/`ModelBone` directly in C++, never through the content pipeline, which is exactly how Task 439's dead-`ParentBone` gap went unnoticed as long as it did. Cross-referenced `SkinnedModelEXT`/`docs/avatar-real-rendering-ext.md` as a deliberately separate, fully-covered system, to avoid confusing readers into thinking the Avatar pipeline's own test coverage extends to plain `Model` loading too. **This closes Phase 49 in full (Tasks 431-440)**: `Model`'s runtime API is now thoroughly audited and FNA-faithful; its content-loading path has a clearly-documented, honestly-scoped set of gaps versus both FNA and CNA's own now-more-capable runtime API, left as open follow-up work rather than silently glossed over. Docs-only, no code changed. |
| 916 | `Model`'s only non-default constructor auto-defaults `Root` to `bones[0]`, with no way to specify a different root bone index (Task 431 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — the last of the 5 confirmed bugs from Task 500's own ~90% milestone declaration to close this session (921, 922, 918, 916 all now fixed; only Task 868, Vulkan `BlendState`, remains open — confirmed by checking its real row, not assumed).** Confirmed FNA's real `Model` constructor (`src/Graphics/Model.cs`) never sets `Root` at all — `ModelReader` (`src/Content/ContentReaders/ModelReader.cs:178-180`) assigns it externally afterward via `model.Root = bones[rootBoneIndex]`, where `rootBoneIndex` can name any bone. Added an optional 5th `rootBoneIndex` parameter (default `0`, preserving the existing default) to the 4-arg `Model` constructor (Task 439's own constructor, since that's the "full-featured" hand-built-model overload already used for per-mesh `ParentBone` assignment) — additive-only, no existing call site's behavior changes: `Model::Model(GraphicsDevice*, vector<ModelBone*> bones, vector<ModelMesh*> meshes, vector<ModelBone*> meshParentBones, std::size_t rootBoneIndex = 0)`. Throws `std::out_of_range` for an out-of-bounds index; matches the 3-arg constructor's own existing empty-bones leniency (an empty `bones` vector leaves `Root` as `nullptr` regardless of the requested index, rather than throwing on the harmless default `0`) — confirmed this edge case explicitly rather than assuming it, since a naive `if (index >= size) throw` would have wrongly thrown on the common `bones={}` construction path. **New tests** (`tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/ModelTests.cpp`): default-index-matches-prior-behavior, non-zero-index genuinely honored (3-bone hierarchy, index 2 selects the 3rd bone, confirmed both by equality to the expected bone AND inequality to bone 0), out-of-range throws, and the empty-bones-leaves-null-regardless-of-index edge case. **Discriminating power verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily made the constructor ignore `rootBoneIndex` entirely (kept the delegating 3-arg constructor's own `bones[0]` default) — the 2 tests that actually depend on honoring a non-default index (`FiveArgConstructorHonorsNonZeroRootBoneIndex`, `FiveArgConstructorThrowsWhenRootBoneIndexOutOfRange`) failed exactly as predicted, while the other 24 `ModelTest.*` tests were unaffected; restored and reconfirmed all 26 pass. **Full regression**: 4535/4538 passed — exactly the 3 already-documented pre-existing EasyGL failures (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`), zero new. Cross-backend compile sanity check: Vulkan/Bgfx/SDL_Renderer all build the `CNA` target clean (`Model` is shared, backend-agnostic code; full test suites not run on those 3, out of scope). Updated `docs/model-content-pipeline-support.md` (Task 440's own doc) to reflect this is now fixed — the runtime API section's own "Still open: Task 916" note is gone; the content-pipeline-loader table's own separate, still-genuinely-open row (`ModelTypeReader` never calls this new parameter, moot regardless since the JSON loader only ever creates 1 bone) was clarified, not removed, since that's a distinct, still-real gap. |
| 917 | Bgfx occlusion queries can't measure true scene-depth visibility — the query-attached submit() shares the SAME view (and depth buffer) as any other same-frame geometry, unlike bgfx's own official "26-occlusion" example, which attaches its occlusion-measurement submit() to a SEPARATE, dedicated view (Task 448 finding) | ⬜ | Task 448 fixed `BgfxOcclusionQueryBackend::Begin()`/`End()` to genuinely attach to a real `bgfx::submit(id, program, occlusionQuery)` call (previously literal no-ops) — a real, verified improvement matching bgfx's own documented API contract exactly. But bgfx's own official example additionally submits its occlusion-measurement pass to a dedicated view (view 1), separate from the "real" visible-scene view (view 0), specifically so the query's own sample count isn't polluted by other geometry drawn earlier in the same view/depth buffer. CNA's fix currently attaches the query to whichever view the game's normal 3D draw already targets (`currentViewId_`), shared with everything else drawn there that frame. Reproducing bgfx's dedicated-view pattern (likely reusing the existing `Detail::AllocateRtViewId()`/`ReleaseRtViewId()` free-list infrastructure already used for render targets, Task 910) would let a query's result reflect ONLY its own geometry's true visibility against the real scene's already-drawn depth, rather than being potentially contaminated by unrelated same-view draws. Not attempted here — a real further architecture addition, and this sandbox's software Mesa GL 2.1 (llvmpipe) renderer couldn't even discriminate whether the CURRENT (shared-view) fix changes observable behavior at all (see Task 448's own write-up), so verifying a fix for this specific gap would need a real GPU-backed environment to test against meaningfully, not this sandbox. |
| 918 | EasyGL has NO real anisotropic filtering support at all — `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` silently falls back to plain trilinear filtering (`LinearMipmapLinear`/`Linear`), with no `GL_EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic`/`glSamplerParameterf(GL_TEXTURE_MAX_ANISOTROPY_EXT, ...)` call anywhere in this backend or the underlying `easy-gl` library (Task 456 finding) | ✅ | **CLOSED — a genuine cross-repo fix, the first of this project's session.** Confirmed the row's own diagnosis directly: `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::ApplySamplerState`'s `case 2: // Anisotropic` mapped to plain trilinear with `maxAnisotropy` unused. Traced the root cause one layer deeper than the row anticipated: this backend has zero raw GL calls anywhere — every GL operation goes through the vendored `meta-gl`/`easy-gl` type-safe wrapper libraries (separate sibling git repositories, not part of `cna_graphics`), and `metagl::SamplerParameter` had no `MaxAnisotropy` entry, `metagl::GetParameter` had no `MaxTextureMaxAnisotropy` entry — both needed adding to `meta-gl` before any CNA-side fix was possible at all. `metagl::HasExtension("GL_EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic")` already existed as the exact real extension-check API needed (its own doc comment even used this exact extension as its usage example), and the underlying `GL_TEXTURE_MAX_ANISOTROPY_EXT`/`GL_MAX_TEXTURE_MAX_ANISOTROPY_EXT` constants (`0x84FE`/`0x84FF`) were confirmed against the vendored Khronos `gl2ext.h`, but not wired into any enum. **Real permission/process note**: a forked sub-agent was denied the cross-repo `meta-gl` edit twice by the sandbox's own permission classifier — first as an unauthorized cross-repo action even after a conversational authorization, then again citing that a coordinator-relayed claim of user authorization is not sufficient grounds (an agent cannot vouch for a human's consent to another agent). The project owner was asked directly and the edit was completed by the coordinator itself after two more denials (the classifier explicitly required this to run outside "auto mode," meaning it needed the project owner's own real-time approval at the point of the actual tool call, not just prior conversational agreement) — a real, worth-remembering precedent for any future cross-repo dependency fix in this project. **`meta-gl` change** (committed there separately, not pushed — `fc1289e` "Add GL_EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic support to SamplerParameter/GetParameter"): added `SamplerParameter::MaxAnisotropy` and `GetParameter::MaxTextureMaxAnisotropy` enum entries (guarded `#ifndef`/`#define` for the 2 raw GL constants, since they're not part of core GLES/GL and `<GLES3/gl32.h>` doesn't define them) plus their `to_string()` mappings in `EnumNames.hpp` — both existing `glSamplerParameterf`/`glGetFloatv` dispatch paths already handle float-valued parameters, so no new functions were needed. **CNA-side fix** (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`): `ApplySamplerState`'s Anisotropic branch now genuinely calls `s.set_parameter(SamplerParameter::MaxAnisotropy, clamped)` gated behind `HasExtension`, clamping the real requested `SamplerState.MaxAnisotropy` against a live `glGetFloatv(GetParameter::MaxTextureMaxAnisotropy, ...)` query — mirroring the existing MSAA-clamp-to-`GL_MAX_SAMPLES` idiom in the same file, exactly as this row's own prior analysis suggested. Also updated Task 456's own startup capability-dump log to report the real, runtime-detected status instead of the previous hardcoded "NOT supported" string. **Confirmed the extension is genuinely present in this sandbox**: `cna_demo_2d`'s real console output now reads `anisotropic filtering: supported (Task 918, up to 16x)` — this sandbox's Mesa/llvmpipe software renderer does support `GL_EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic` up to 16x, contrary to what might be assumed of a software rasterizer. **New test** (`examples/easygl_anisotropic_gl_state_test.cpp`, `EasyGL_Anisotropic_GlState`): rather than attempting a fragile true visual anisotropic-quality pixel comparison (inherently driver-dependent, and genuinely hard to set up reliably — needs oblique viewing angles plus a complete mip chain), talks directly to `easygl::Sampler`'s own public API (bypassing CNA's `GraphicsDevice` entirely): sets `SamplerParameter::MaxAnisotropy` to a real sub-cap value (4.0) and reads it straight back via `get_parameter_fv`, confirming the driver genuinely stored it; also exercises Task 299's own extreme `9999` value directly against the raw `Sampler` API, confirming the driver clamps it to its real cap rather than storing it verbatim. **Discriminating power independently verified via sabotage-and-revert**: commented out the `set_parameter` call for the sub-cap case — the round-trip check correctly failed (readback dropped to the driver's own default, not `4.0`), while the other 2 checks were unaffected; restored and reconfirmed all 3 pass. The pre-existing Task 299 test (`EasyGL_TextureAnisotropic_DualTextureEffect`, extreme-value-must-not-crash) also still passes, now genuinely exercising the real code path for the first time rather than a no-op. **Full regression**: 4531/4534 passed — exactly the 3 already-documented pre-existing failures (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`), zero new. Cross-backend compile sanity check (Vulkan/Bgfx/SDL_Renderer, `CNA` target only, no test suites run) all clean — this is an EasyGL-only fix, those backends' own code is untouched. Closes one of the 5 confirmed bugs gating Task 500's ~90% Graphics compatibility milestone; part of Task 738's real remaining scope. |

| 454 | Ensure Vulkan unsupported features throw or document fallback behavior           | ✅      | **CLOSED — no bug found; the underlying `VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures` gating was already correctly implemented, just not previously documented anywhere — documented here per this task's own "throw or document fallback behavior" framing.** Investigated `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s device-creation code directly, since Vulkan's own device-capability model is genuinely stricter than GL/bgfx's — requesting an unsupported optional feature makes `vkCreateDevice` fail outright, not just silently degrade, so this needed real verification rather than assuming graceful-by-default behavior. Confirmed the ENTIRE optional-feature request surface is exactly 2 features (`fillModeNonSolid` for `FillMode::WireFrame`, `samplerAnisotropy` for anisotropic texture filtering) — no other `VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures` are ever requested at all (no `geometryShader`/`tessellationShader`/`wideLines`/etc.), and both of these 2 are correctly gated behind a real `vkGetPhysicalDeviceFeatures(physicalDevice_, &supported)` query first, each behind its own `if (supported.X)` check before being added to the enabled-feature struct passed to `vkCreateDevice`. Confirmed downstream usage sites correctly respect the resulting support flags rather than assuming the feature is always available: `fillModeWireframe_ = (fillMode == 1) && fillModeNonSolidSupported_` (a device without `fillModeNonSolid` silently falls back to solid fill instead of requesting an invalid pipeline state that would otherwise validation-error or crash); `maxSamplerAnisotropy_` is read from the device's real `VkPhysicalDeviceLimits.maxSamplerAnisotropy` and used to clamp any requested anisotropy level rather than blindly passing through an arbitrary request. Also spot-checked MSAA sample-count selection (`PickSampleCount`) since it's the same class of "device may not support the exact requested value" concern — confirmed it queries the device's real `framebufferColorSampleCounts` limit and picks the best available count ≤ the requested one, not assuming an arbitrary count is always legal. **No gap found across any of these 3 checks** — added a new documented section to Task 451's own `docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md` (not a new separate doc, since this is a small, single-backend addendum to an already-comprehensive matrix) recording this finding, since "already correctly implemented but never written down anywhere" is itself worth fixing even when no code changes. Zero code changes — pure investigation + documentation. |

The capability itself was only ever manually spot-checked ONCE during Task 914's own development (per that task's own code comment: "confirmed BGFX_CAPS_TEXTURE_BLIT/READ_BACK are both supported in this project's Xvfb/llvmpipe/OpenGL sandbox via a throwaway caps-log check before implementing"), never given a persistent runtime diagnostic for other renderers/hardware that might lack it. Fixed by adding a clear `std::cerr` log message to both failure branches, matching this file's own already-established `"CNA: bgfx ... not supported on ..."` diagnostic convention (used elsewhere for shader-compilation fallbacks), naming the actual active renderer via `bgfx::getRendererName(bgfx::getRendererType())` so a real user hitting this on unusual hardware gets an actionable message instead of silent, unexplained data loss. **Discriminating power independently verified**: temporarily forced the `TextureCube::GetData` failure branch unconditionally — rebuilt and reran, confirmed the new log message fires with the correct renderer name AND the existing `Bgfx_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip` test genuinely fails as predicted (proving the existing test suite would already catch a real regression here, not just that the log line appears); restored (confirmed clean `git diff` — net +21/-2 lines, both `GetData` methods' new log blocks, zero sabotage residue) and reconfirmed the test passes again. **Separately investigated and confirmed already-adequate, left alone**: `BgfxEffectBackend` (backing the `NOXNA` `ShaderEffect` extension)'s own known limitation — `CompileProgram` always returns `false`, `IsValid()` correctly reports `false`, `GetCompileError()` gives a clear, specific message ("bgfx backend requires pre-compiled binary shaders (not GLSL source)") — is already non-silent: `ShaderEffect`'s own constructor already logs the compile error to `std::cerr` and exposes a public `IsEffectValid()` query for game code to check; `OnApply()` degrades gracefully (a debug log, not a crash) rather than doing anything harmful with an invalid effect. This matches (rather than needs to newly adopt) the idiomatic "queryable compile-status flag" pattern most graphics APIs use for shader compilation, not a bug needing a fix. **Newly-discovered, unrelated flaky test found while closing this task**: the full regression's first run showed an unexpected 2nd failure, `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` (a completely different code path from anything this task touched — `RenderTarget2D`'s own mip-chain auto-generation via `BGFX_RESOLVE_AUTO_GEN_MIPS`, Task 906's own established mechanism, not `Texture3D`/`TextureCube::GetData` at all). Investigated via 8 isolated runs of the UNMODIFIED code (`git stash`) before assuming anything — confirmed it fails there too, ~1-in-8 times, with the IDENTICAL failure signature both with and without this task's change (`sample=(255,0,0)` instead of the correct blended `(224,0,32)` — the coarsest mip level evidently hadn't finished auto-generating/blending yet at readback time, a timing race, not a deterministic bug). Documented as a new, genuinely pre-existing, environment-dependent flaky test in `NEXT.md`'s own §5 known-bugs table, matching the already-established `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`/`Vulkan_RenderTargetUsage` flaky-test precedent exactly — not investigated further (out of this task's own scope), not a regression from this task's real fix. Full Bgfx regression (2 runs, since the 1st happened to hit the newly-discovered flaky test): 1st run 4412/4414 (the 1 known `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` baseline failure plus the newly-discovered flaky `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain`); 2nd run (immediately after, no code changes in between) **4413/4414 passed (99%)**, exactly the 1 known pre-existing failure, zero new failures — confirming the 1st run's 2nd failure was indeed the flaky test, not a real regression. `Bgfx_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip` (the 4 tests actually exercising this task's changed code paths) all passed cleanly under `ctest` in both runs. |

That falls squarely under this project's own "modifying CI/CD pipelines" category that warrants the project owner's explicit sign-off rather than autonomous action, even under this session's otherwise-broad "commit and push per finished task" authorization (which was granted for ordinary source-code changes, not for authoring new recurring CI automation). Rather than either silently creating the workflow or silently dropping the "CI-friendly" half of this task's own framing, split the work: **done here** — (1) added `LABELS GraphicsSmoke` to all 4 existing `set_tests_properties` blocks (`CMakeLists.txt`, 4 one-line additions, zero behavior change to the tests themselves); (2) added `scripts/run-all-backend-smoke-tests.sh`, a new local orchestrator that iterates the 4 backends against this project's own persistent per-backend build directories (`cmake-build-debug`=EASYGL, `cmake-build-vulkan`, `cmake-build-bgfx`, `cmake-build-sdl`), configuring fresh only if a build dir doesn't already exist, building with the project's own established `-j4 -- -k` convention (tolerating the one already-known, unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy build error rather than treating it as a false "backend unavailable" signal — an early draft of this script wrongly gated on the aggregate build exit code and reported all 4 backends as unavailable purely because of that one irrelevant target; desktop: 1 synchronous immediate context recreate) — real, thought-through code, never exercised against an actual browser. Documented anticipated (not verified) WebGL2/GLES3 capability gaps versus desktop GL (no geometry/tessellation shaders; anisotropic filtering needs `EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic`, currently moot per Task 918's own finding that EasyGL has no real anisotropic support on any platform yet; narrower baseline texture-format support, currently moot per Task 176's `SurfaceFormat::Color`-only constraint; zero WebGL-1 fallback by design). | 479 | Add script to compare CNA JSON results against FNA JSON results                             | ✅      | **CLOSED — built the missing other half of the comparison (a CNA-side JSON exporter), not just the diff script, since a "compare CNA vs FNA JSON" script is unverifiable with no real CNA JSON to feed it.** Investigated first: confirmed CNA's C++ side had zero general JSON export mechanism (the only "json" hits anywhere in `include/`/`src/` were an unrelated ad-hoc `ExtractJsonStringField` helper in `ContentManager.cpp` for reading `.model.json` content, and zero vendored JSON libraries anywhere); confirmed CNA's own `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/PackedVector/PackedVectorTests.cpp` already has hardcoded `EXPECT_EQ(..., 0x...)` assertions (e.g. `Alpha8Test.GoldenHalfAlpha` → `0x80`, `HalfVector2`'s `(1,0)` → `0x00003c00`) matching `tests/PackedVectorGolden.md`'s own values, which Task 473 already confirmed match real FNA output — a valid but narrower, PackedVector-only, already-existing cross-check. **Scope decision**: chose to build both the generic Python comparison script AND a minimal new CNA-side C++ JSON exporter mirroring Tasks 472/473/476's own 4 categories (enums, state presets, PackedVector, Viewport — all confirmed `GraphicsDevice`-free), rather than script-only infrastructure with nothing real to run it against. New `tools/cna-reference/JsonWriter.hpp` (a tiny hand-rolled JSON writer mirroring `tools/fna-reference/JsonWriter.cs`'s own `Add()`-overload shape) and `tools/cna-reference/CnaReferenceDump.cpp` (new `cna_reference_dump` CMake target, gated `CNA_BUILD_EXAMPLES AND NOT EMSCRIPTEN AND NOT ANDROID`, not registered as a `ctest` since the full comparison additionally needs `mono`/`xbuild` + a locally-built `FNA.dll`, not guaranteed on every machine that builds CNA). New `scripts/compare-fna-reference.py`: recursively diffs two JSON trees by key path, one-directional (every FNA-side key must exist and match on the CNA side; CNA-only keys, e.g. NOXNA `PrimitiveType.PointListEXT`, are not reported as mismatches), exact match for ints/bools/strings, `--tolerance`-bounded (default `1e-4`) for floats. **Running the real comparison for the first time found 3 categories of genuine, real bugs — exactly the point of building this tooling**: (1) `JsonWriter.hpp`'s `Add(double)` used `ostringstream`'s default 6-significant-digit precision, silently truncating large packed-value integers (e.g. `Byte4`'s `4278190335` printed as `4278190000`) and sub-millimeter `Viewport.Project` float differences alike — fixed via `std::setprecision(std::numeric_limits<double>::max_digits10)`; (2) the first draft of `DumpBlendState`/`DumpDepthStencilState`/`DumpRasterizerState`/`DumpSamplerState` omitted `BlendState`'s own `BlendFactor` property and all 4 state classes' inherited `GraphicsResource::Name`/`IsDisposed` properties (confirmed CNA's own state classes already expose `getNameProperty()`/`getIsDisposedProperty()`, and that the exact same preset `Name` strings FNA's dump reported, e.g. `"BlendState.Additive"`, are already set in CNA's own `BlendState.cpp`) — fixed by adding them; (3) the first draft of `DumpEnums`'s `SurfaceFormat` block only listed the 20 members shared with older FNA, omitting 7 `*EXT`-suffixed members (`ColorBgraEXT`/`ColorSrgbEXT`/`Dxt5SrgbEXT`/`Bc7EXT`/`Bc7SrgbEXT`/`ByteEXT`/`UShortEXT`) — confirmed via the real FNA dump (this sandbox's locally-built `FNA.dll` is version 26.5.0.0) that these are genuine, current upstream FNA enum members with the exact same names, not CNA-only NOXNA additions despite the `EXT` naming convention CNA otherwise reserves for non-XNA additions — fixed by adding them too. **After fixing all 3 tooling bugs, the comparison found exactly ONE real, substantive CNA-vs-FNA divergence, not a tooling artifact**: `IndexElementSize`'s numeric values do not match FNA (`SixteenBits=0`/`ThirtyTwoBits=1` in the real FNA source, confirmed by reading `src/Graphics/Vertices/IndexElementSize.cs` directly — a plain, implicitly-sequential enum with no explicit values — vs. CNA's own `SixteenBits=16`/`ThirtyTwoBits=32`, apparently an earlier, incorrect assumption that the enum's numeric value represented a literal bit-width; CNA even has an existing, confidently-wrong test asserting this, `OcclusionQueryDynamicBufferTests.cpp`'s `IndexElementSizeTest.SixteenBitsIssixteen`/`ThirtyTwoBitsIsThirtyTwo`). **Deliberately NOT fixed as part of this task** — changing a public XNA API enum's underlying values, plus correcting its own existing (now-confirmed-wrong) test coverage, plus auditing the 2 real (symbolic, `==`-only, not value-dependent) internal usages in `IndexBuffer.cpp`, is a genuinely separate, scoped piece of work from "build the comparison tooling," matching this session's own "one task = one commit" discipline; tracked as new Task 921 instead. **Discriminating-power independently verified 3 ways**: (a) temporarily set `Alpha8`'s `0.50` case to `129` in a copy of the real CNA JSON output — script correctly reported exactly that 1 mismatch (`FNA=128 CNA=129`), nothing else; (b) confirmed the `--tolerance` flag genuinely gates float comparisons — a `+0.00001` perturbation to `Viewport.camera_offAxis.Project_X` passed at the default `1e-4` tolerance, a `+0.01` perturbation correctly failed; (c) the 3 real tooling bugs above were themselves caught BY this exact mechanism during normal development, not manufactured after the fact. Full regression: rebuilt cleanly (`cmake --build cmake-build-debug`, only the already-known, unrelated `cna_demo_xact` Content-copy error, matching this session's established baseline) and ran the complete EasyGL `ctest` suite (4525 tests) — 99% passed; all 8 failures (`ENetDiscoveryServiceTest`×3, `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection`, `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`) are pre-existing/environmental (network-discovery and Mesa/llvmpipe rendering-driver limitations in this sandbox), confirmed unrelated since this task's own `CMakeLists.txt` change is purely additive (14 insertions, 0 deletions, one new independent target) and touches nothing any of those 8 tests exercise. |

This closes one of the 5 confirmed bugs gating Task 500's ~90% Graphics compatibility milestone — 918/922/916/868 remain open. |
| 922 | `SpriteBatch::Draw` is missing one of FNA's real 7 overloads as a faithful public XNA API member — `Draw(Texture2D, Rectangle destination, Rectangle? source, Color, rotation, origin, SpriteEffects, layerDepth)` only exists as a `NOXNA`-marked variant with a **required** `Rectangle source` param instead of FNA's `Rectangle?` (optional) (Task 487 finding, confirmed via `grep -c "public void Draw(" src/Graphics/SpriteBatch.cs` = 7 in real FNA vs. 6 non-`NOXNA` overloads in `include/.../SpriteBatch.hpp`) | ✅ | **CLOSED — added the real 7th `SpriteBatch::Draw` overload as a faithful, additive-only public XNA API member.** Read FNA's real overload (`src/Graphics/SpriteBatch.cs:593`) directly to confirm its `Rectangle? sourceRectangle` null-handling (resolves to the whole texture, `sourceX/Y=0, sourceW/H=1`, exactly matching this project's own already-established `std::optional<Rectangle>` sibling-overload pattern at `SpriteBatch.hpp:273`/`.cpp:332`). New public overload `void Draw(const Texture2D&, const Rectangle& destinationRectangle, std::optional<Rectangle> sourceRectangle, Color, float rotation_rad, Vector2 origin, SpriteEffects effect, float layerDepth)` (naming matches the existing `NOXNA` sibling's own `rotation_rad`/`effect` convention for this same Rectangle-destination overload family, since the `Vector2`-position family uses `rotation`/`effects` instead — an intentional per-family match, not an inconsistency). Implementation resolves `sourceRectangle` to `Rectangle(0, 0, w, h)` when empty, mirroring the exact resolution logic already used by every other `std::optional<Rectangle>`-taking overload in this file, then forwards to the same private `pushSprite()` helper the pre-existing `NOXNA` overload already uses — no new resolution logic invented. **Kept the existing `NOXNA` overload as-is** (lowest-risk, additive-only choice per this row's own guidance) — confirmed real existing callers depend on its exact required-`Rectangle` signature (e.g. `examples/easygl_sprite_effects_test.cpp`), so removing it would have been a breaking change with no benefit. New tests in `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/SpriteBatchTests.cpp`: a guard-throw test (`DrawRectOptSrcColorRotOriEffLayerBeforeBeginThrows`, mirroring the existing `DrawRectOptSrcColorBeforeBeginThrows` naming convention) plus two `RecordingSpriteBatchBackend`-based value-correctness tests modeled directly on the existing `DrawIsRecordedWithExactParameters` test — one with a populated `sourceRectangle` confirming it's forwarded byte-for-byte, one with `std::nullopt` confirming it resolves to `Rectangle(0, 0, texWidth, texHeight)` (the "whole texture" case). **Discriminating power independently verified via sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily hardcoded the new overload's `src` to always be the whole-texture rectangle regardless of `sourceRectangle` — the populated-source test failed exactly as predicted (`call.sourceRectangle` showed the whole-texture rect instead of the requested crop), while the nullopt test still (correctly, coincidentally) passed since both branches produce the same value for that specific case; restored the real resolution logic and reconfirmed both tests pass, confirmed a clean `git diff` afterward. **Full regression** (`ctest --test-dir cmake-build-debug --output-on-failure`, 4533 tests): 4529/4533 passed — the 3 already-known EasyGL baseline failures (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`) plus 1 already-documented flaky audio test (`CueTest.PlayCalledTwiceWhileAlreadyPlayingIsANoOpAndDoesNotDuplicateInstances`, explicitly called out in `NEXT.md` §5 as a known pre-existing flake unrelated to Graphics) — reconfirmed genuinely flaky and unrelated via 3 isolated reruns, all 3 passing cleanly. **Cross-backend compile sanity check**: `SpriteBatch.hpp`/`.cpp` is shared, backend-agnostic code, so confirmed clean `CNA` target builds on Vulkan, Bgfx, and SDL_Renderer too (compile-only, no full test suites run on those 3, per scope) — zero overload-ambiguity errors from adding a new `std::optional<Rectangle>`-taking overload alongside the pre-existing required-`Rectangle` one (confirmed via a full `cmake-build-debug` rebuild of every example target, not just the library, since an implicit `Rectangle` → `std::optional<Rectangle>` conversion could in principle have created an ambiguous call site anywhere an existing caller passes a raw `Rectangle` literal to the 8-arg family — none did). |
| 923 | Fix Bgfx's narrower `BlendState` gap (split out of Task 868): `alphaSrcBlend`/`alphaDstBlend` unused (commented out of `BgfxGraphicsBackend::ApplyBlendState`'s signature, alpha channel always reuses the color channel's factors), `colorBlendFunc`/`alphaBlendFunc` entirely ignored (always implicitly `BlendFunction::Add`) | ✅ | **CLOSED — production fix done and confirmed correct; the BlendFunction half is pixel-verified, the alpha-factor half could not be independently pixel-verified in this sandbox (see below, matches this project's established precedent for a sandbox/renderer limitation, not a CNA defect).** Confirmed no existing `Bgfx_BlendState_*` tests existed at all (zero registrations in `CMakeLists.txt`, unlike EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer which all have full sets) — this task had to build its own verification from scratch, not just "strengthen" pre-existing ones as originally scoped. **Fix**: added `XnaBlendFunctionToBgfxEquation()` (mirrors Task 868's `ToVkBlendOp` exactly: `Add=0→BGFX_STATE_BLEND_EQUATION_ADD`, `Subtract=1→SUB`, `ReverseSubtract=2→REVSUB`, `Max=3→MAX`, `Min=4→MIN`) and rewrote `ApplyBlendState` to use `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_FUNC_SEPARATE(colorSrc,colorDst,alphaSrc,alphaDst)` `\|` `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_EQUATION_SEPARATE(colorFunc,alphaFunc)` instead of the old `BGFX_STATE_BLEND_FUNC(colorSrc,colorDst)`-only call. Also widened the existing "Opaque fast path" short-circuit (`blendFlags_=0` when blend is a no-op) to check all 4 factors (`colorSrc==One && colorDst==Zero && alphaSrc==One && alphaDst==Zero`), matching Task 868's own Vulkan fix — previously it only checked the 2 color factors, silently treating some real custom `BlendState`s as Opaque regardless of their alpha factors. No pipeline-cache-key complication needed (unlike Vulkan): Bgfx's `blendFlags_` is a single mutable member applied fresh via `bgfx::setState()` on every draw, immediate/synchronous, not baked into a cached pipeline object. **Verified the bit-packing is genuinely correct** by reading bgfx's own vendored `renderer_gl.cpp` (`cmake-build-bgfx/_deps/bgfx_cmake-src/bgfx/src/renderer_gl.cpp` — decodes `srcRGB/dstRGB/srcA/dstA` from the exact same nibble positions this fix packs into, then genuinely calls `glBlendFuncSeparate`/`glBlendEquationSeparate`) — confirms this is the real, correct, documented bgfx API usage, not a guess. **New `Bgfx_BlendState_SeparateFunctions` test** (Bgfx-specific adaptation of `examples/easygl_blendstate_separate_functions_test.cpp`/Task 307 — could NOT reuse that file verbatim like Vulkan's Task 868 registration does, because it calls the low-level `GraphicsDevice.SetDepthTestEnabled(false)`, an unconditional `ThrowNo3DState()` on Bgfx, a separate pre-existing documented gap, not this task's to fix; safely omitted in the Bgfx copy since each `DrawAndSample` call clears+draws exactly one full-screen quad, so depth-test state has no effect on the result either way): Check A (`ColorBlendFunction=Subtract`) directly discriminates the "always implicitly Add" bug. **Verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverted just the 2 production files, rebuilt, reran — Check A failed exactly as predicted (`(250,250,0)` instead of `(150,0,0)`, i.e. it silently computed Add's result instead of Subtract's), Check B coincidentally still passed (matches the same pattern Task 868's own Vulkan test comments document); restored and reconfirmed both pass. **The alpha-factor-independence half could NOT be pixel-verified here, after substantial genuine effort, not a shortcut**: built a 2-draw indirect-observation test (draw 1 sets up a known alpha value via specific alpha factors; draw 2 uses `Blend::DestinationAlpha` as its own COLOR factor to turn that otherwise-invisible alpha value into an observable RGB difference, since this project has no established, verified way to read the alpha channel back from the backbuffer directly) — empirically, this **reported the exact same result whether the fix was applied or reverted** (3 independent probes with different factor/value combinations all showed the framebuffer's alpha ending up effectively pinned at 255/1.0 whenever a draw's own colour and alpha blend factors genuinely differ, regardless of which side — buggy reused-color-factors or the fix's real separate factors — was active). Cross-checked bgfx's own `renderer_gl.cpp`: it unconditionally calls the real `glBlendFuncSeparate`/`glBlendEquationSeparate` GL entry points with the correctly-decoded values whenever `numRt<=1` (our case, single backbuffer target) — so this isn't a dispatch-logic bug in bgfx's own C++ layer either; the discrepancy manifests below that, in this sandbox's Mesa llvmpipe/GL2.1 software rasterizer itself (or some deeper interaction this session didn't fully bottom out). Root-caused as far as reasonably possible within this task's own scope (which is CNA's `ApplyBlendState` mapping, not bgfx's vendored renderer internals or the sandbox's software GL driver) and NOT investigated further, matching this project's own established precedent for a sandbox/renderer limitation rather than forcing an unreliable test to "pass" (Task 448's occlusion query, Task 879/Task 903's `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` DRI3 finding) — the test was deleted rather than shipped, since a test that reports identically regardless of the underlying fix is worse than no test (a false sense of coverage). **Full Bgfx regression** (`ctest`, all 4415 tests): 4412 passed, 3 failed — all 3 pre-existing and already documented (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve` Xvfb/DRI3 limitation, `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` ~1-in-8 timing flake, one unrelated `AudioCategoryTest` flake) — zero new failures. Cross-backend compile check clean (EasyGL/Vulkan/SDL_Renderer untouched, this fix touches only `BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`). This closes the last of the 2 backends split out of Task 868's original diagnosis (Vulkan/868 done, Bgfx/923 done) — Task 500's confirmed-bugs list is now fully closed on both backends. |

| 732 | Implement real `SurfaceFormat` GPU forwarding on EasyGL | ⛔ | **Real architecture decision needed — see question below.** Investigated the actual current state before writing any code, per this project's own blast-radius-check discipline. Confirmed the task's own premise (`CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` hardcode `::metagl::InternalFormat::Rgba8` regardless of the `surfaceFormat` parameter — `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`'s `EasyGLTexture3DBackend::EasyGLTexture3DBackend(int, int, int, bool, int /*surfaceFormat*/)` and `EasyGLTextureCubeBackend::EasyGLTextureCubeBackend(int, bool, int /*surfaceFormat*/)` both literally comment out the parameter name) — but this dead code is currently **unreachable from any live call path**: the shared, cross-backend `Texture::ValidateFormat(SurfaceFormat)` (`Texture.cpp:109`), called unconditionally by every format-taking `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructor on ALL FOUR backends, already throws `std::runtime_error` for every `SurfaceFormat` value except `Color` — so none of the 14 formats this task names can ever reach the EasyGL backend's `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` today, regardless of backend. This is not an oversight: it is an existing, ALREADY-SHIPPED, ALREADY-TESTED contract from Task 176 (`examples/easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp`), which explicitly asserts `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` constructed with `Bgr565`/`HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable`/`Vector4`/etc. (multiple of the EXACT formats this task names) **must throw** — and is separately, explicitly documented as an accepted, by-design, cross-backend limitation in `NEXT.md` §5 ("`SurfaceFormat` support is Color-only for real GPU formats"). Implementing this task as literally scoped would directly reverse that shipped decision (a regression against Task 176's own test) via a change to SHARED code (`Texture::ValidateFormat`) affecting ALL FOUR backends, not an EasyGL-only fix, despite being filed under an EasyGL-only phase. **Deeper blast radius, found while investigating further**: even if `ValidateFormat` were loosened for these 14 formats, CNA's ENTIRE public `SetData`/`GetData` API surface for `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` is exclusively `Color*`-based (4 bytes/pixel RGBA) — there is no way for any caller to supply correctly-packed non-RGBA8 data (e.g. a 2-byte `Bgr565` texel, a 16-byte float `Vector4` texel) at all today. `Texture3D` alone has one `NOXNA SetDataPointerEXT(..., const void* data, int dataLength)` raw-bytes escape hatch; `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` have none (`Texture2D::SetDataRGBA` is RGBA8-only, not format-agnostic). Genuinely implementing "real GPU forwarding" for these 14 formats therefore needs, at minimum: (a) loosening the shared `ValidateFormat` for exactly these 14 values (reversing Task 176's contract, cross-backend); (b) adding/extending raw-or-typed `SetData`/`GetData` overloads for `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` (mirroring `Texture3D::SetDataPointerEXT`'s precedent) so a caller can actually supply/read non-RGBA8 pixel data; (c) correct bytes-per-pixel math throughout mip-level-size/`GetData`-buffer-size calculations (currently hardcoded to 4 everywhere); (d) the EasyGL-side format-mapping table this task's own note focuses on. (a)-(c) are cross-cutting API/architecture decisions well beyond "map an enum to GL internal formats in one backend file." **Question for the project owner:** (a) do the full job — extend the public `SetData`/`GetData` API surface (a real, generic-or-typed data path, not just `Color*`), loosen `ValidateFormat` for these 14 formats across all 4 backends, then implement EasyGL's GLES 3.2 format table (large, multi-file, cross-backend architecture change, arguably deserving its own dedicated multi-task plan rather than one line item); or (b) implement GPU-allocation-only forwarding — have `CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` (and `CreateTexture`, if given format info) allocate the GPU texture with the correct internal format for these 14 values, verified via a backend-level test that bypasses the public `Texture2D`/`Texture3D`/`TextureCube` API (since the public API still can't supply/read the data) — narrower, but produces plumbing the public API can't use yet, and still requires deciding whether `ValidateFormat` moves at all; or (c) leave the current, already-documented, already-tested "Color-only" limitation as-is, and treat this task as superseded/closed-won't-do unless a future, properly-scoped architecture task (generic `SetData<T>`/raw-bytes API design, spanning all 4 backends) is planned separately. Not guessing on this per project's own risky-decision policy — leaving BLOCKED until the project owner picks a direction. Zero code changes made — this was investigation only. |
| 733 | Implement `FillMode::WireFrame` emulation on EasyGL via barycentric-coordinate geometry shader + `fwidth()` edge detection | ✅ | **CLOSED — already implemented, already tested; the task's own premise was stale.** Investigated before writing any code (per this project's own verify-first discipline). Confirmed `FillMode::WireFrame` is ALREADY emulated on EasyGL — not via a geometry shader, but via a simpler, already-shipped technique: `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::ApplyRasterizerState` sets a `wireframe_` flag when `fillMode==1`, and every 3D draw entry point (`DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawTexturedPrimitives`/etc.) calls `DrawWireframe()` first when that flag is set, which re-expands the triangle list/strip's index stream into a scratch `GL_LINES` element buffer (one edge-pair per triangle side) and issues a `GL_LINES` draw instead of `GL_TRIANGLES` — correctly handling both `TriangleList`/`TriangleStrip`, indexed and non-indexed, 16-bit and 32-bit indices, and `baseVertex`. This is real, complete, working code (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`), not a stub — the task's own "N/A on GLES" framing being corrected was already out of date. **Also already has test coverage**: `EasyGL_FillMode_Solid` (registered in `CMakeLists.txt`, reusing `vulkan_fill_mode_test.cpp` verbatim per this project's own established backend-agnostic-test-reuse convention, e.g. Task 326) already exercises exactly this — a real triangle drawn Solid (centre must be RED), then WireFrame (centre must be BLACK, interior not rasterized), then reset to Solid (centre RED again) — and already passes 3/3 on a fresh run this session (confirmed directly, not assumed from a stale log). The test's own name is a slight misnomer (it validates all 3 sub-tests, not just Solid) but its actual assertions are exactly what this task needs. **Discriminating power independently verified**: sabotaged `ApplyRasterizerState` to force `wireframe_ = false` unconditionally — rebuilt and reran, and exactly the WireFrame sub-test failed as predicted (centre read RED instead of BLACK, 2/3 pass) while the two Solid sub-tests stayed correct; restored (confirmed clean `git diff`) and reconfirmed 3/3 pass. **Why the proposed barycentric-coordinate/geometry-shader technique was NOT implemented instead**: replacing a working, correct, already-tested implementation with a fundamentally different, more complex one for no behavioral gain would violate this project's own "don't add complexity beyond what's required" and "match XNA/FNA behavior over personal C++ preference" conventions. Real hardware `glPolygonMode(GL_LINE)` (what FNA/XNA's `FillMode::WireFrame` actually models) produces thin, non-anti-aliased edge lines — exactly what the existing `GL_LINES` re-expansion already produces — whereas a `fwidth()`-based barycentric-distance technique is the standard idiom for a THICK, anti-aliased stylized wireframe-overlay effect, a deliberately different, non-XNA-faithful visual. That technique remains a viable FUTURE option if a stylized overlay effect is ever explicitly wanted, but implementing it now would be speculative, unrequested scope, not a fix for any real gap. Wrote and then deleted a duplicate EasyGL-specific test file after discovering the existing reused-Vulkan-source test already covers this fully (per this project's reuse-over-duplication convention) — zero net files added. No code changes shipped (the sabotage-and-revert touched already-shipped code with zero net diff); no separate `ctest` regression needed since no production code changed net. |
| 734 | Add a non-geometry-shader barycentric fallback for GLES 3.2 drivers with unreliable geometry-shader support | ✅ | **CLOSED — moot, depended entirely on Task 733's premise, which Task 733 itself found to be stale.** This task exists solely as a fallback path for the geometry-shader-based `FillMode::WireFrame` implementation Task 733 was supposed to add. Task 733 investigated first and found `FillMode::WireFrame` is ALREADY correctly emulated on EasyGL via a completely different, simpler, already-shipped technique (`DrawWireframe`'s `GL_LINES` index re-expansion) that involves no geometry shader at all — and, per Task 733's own write-up, implementing the geometry-shader/barycentric approach was explicitly declined as unrequested scope replacing a working, XNA-faithful implementation. With no geometry-shader-based WireFrame implementation in existence (by design, not by omission), there is nothing for this task's "non-geometry-shader fallback" to fall back FROM — the existing `GL_LINES` technique already IS the universally-portable, driver-independent, no-geometry-shader-required implementation this task was trying to provide as a fallback. No code changes needed; no `docs/easygl_bugs.md` note needed since there is no unreliable-geometry-shader-driver risk to document (no geometry shader is used for wireframe at all). |
| 735 | Implement `Texture3D` mip-chain generation on EasyGL | ✅ | **CLOSED — already implemented; the task's own premise ("`mipMap` constructor parameter is currently unused") was stale before any code was written.** Investigated `EasyGLTexture3DBackend::EasyGLTexture3DBackend` (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:79-106`) directly: the `mipMap` parameter IS used — `levelCount = mipMap ? CalculateTexture3DMipLevels(w, h) : 1;`, followed by a per-level `set_image_3d(...)` loop that pre-allocates real GPU storage for every mip level, halving width/height/depth each level (standard volume-mip behavior; depth doesn't participate in the level *count*, matching FNA's `Texture3D` constructor and `Texture3D.cpp`'s own `CalculateMipLevels(w,h)`, but each level's own storage still shrinks in all 3 dimensions). This was already fixed by Task 862/864 (commit `6787a06d`, "pre-allocate Texture3D/TextureCube mip levels on EasyGL and Vulkan" — same bug shape as Task 276's already-fixed EasyGL `TextureCube` mip bug), which predates this task in the numbered list only by virtue of manual task-file authoring order, not actual chronology. Confirmed existing test coverage is real and passing, not just present: `examples/easygl_texture3d_mip_test.cpp`, registered as `EasyGL_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip` (also reused verbatim for `Vulkan_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip`/`Bgfx_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip` per this project's backend-agnostic-test-reuse convention). Rebuilt `cna_test_easygl_texture3d_mip` fresh and reran directly via `ctest -R EasyGL_Texture3D_Mip_RoundTrip` on this session's own EasyGL build (`cmake-build-debug`, confirmed `CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND=EASYGL`/`CNA_TEST_DISPLAY=:99`): 1/1 passes. No code changes needed — this task is superseded by Task 862/864, which already shipped the fix and its test. |

CalculateCubeMipLevels(size) : 1;`, followed by a nested per-face/per-level `set_image_2d(...)` loop that pre-allocates real GPU storage for every mip level of all 6 cube faces, halving `levelSize` each level (cube faces are square, matching `TextureCube.cpp`'s own `CalculateMipLevels(size,size)`). Already fixed by the same Task 862/864 commit (`6787a06d`) that fixed Task 735's `Texture3D` case (both backends were fixed together in one commit, since they share the identical bug shape originally found and fixed once already for `TextureCube` alone by Task 276). | 737 | Add explicit `TextureAddressMode::Mirror` regression test for the Task 269 SpriteBatch SamplerState fix | ✅ | **CLOSED — real, previously-unverified gap in test coverage closed; no production bug found (the underlying fix already covers Mirror correctly).** Task 269 fixed `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s `SamplerState` having no effect on EasyGL by adding `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` overrides, and its own test (`easygl_texture_address_mode_test.cpp`) independently pixel-verified `Wrap` and `Clamp` at `U=1.25` — but never `Mirror`, even though `Mirror` is threaded through the exact same `pendingAddressU_`/`pendingAddressV_` fields and the exact same `SetSamplerAddressMode` call. Distinct from Task 296's existing `easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_effect_test.cpp`, which verifies `Mirror` via `DrawUserPrimitives`+`DualTextureEffect` and `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]` directly — a completely different code path that never touches `ISpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` at all. FNA has no `PointMirror` static preset (only `*Clamp`/`*Wrap` for `Point`/`Linear`/`Anisotropic`), so the new test builds a custom `SamplerState` (`Filter=Point`, `AddressU=AddressV=Mirror`), mirroring Task 296's own approach. Reused Task 269's exact methodology (2×1 Red\|Blue texture, `SpriteBatch::Draw` with a `sourceRectangle` 2× the texture width so sampled `U` spans `[0,2]`) but sampled at `U=1.6` instead of Task 269's `U=1.25` — deliberately, since `Mirror` and `Clamp` coincidentally agree at `U=1.25` (both land on the texture's last texel), which would make the check non-discriminating; at `U=1.6`, `Wrap`→fract(1.6)=0.6→Blue, `Clamp`→clamps to last texel→Blue, `Mirror`→reflects 2.0-1.6=0.4→Red — all three modes cleanly separated (matches Task 296's own `U=1.6` choice for the identical reason). New `examples/easygl_texture_address_mode_mirror_test.cpp`, registered as `EasyGL_TextureAddressMode_Mirror`: asserts `Mirror`→Red as the actual subject, and additionally asserts both `Wrap`/`Clamp`→Blue at the same sample point purely to confirm the point genuinely discriminates (not part of the pass/fail gate's headline claim, but still checked, unlike Task 685's SDL_Renderer test which only prints its context checks). Passes on the first attempt with the exact predicted RGB values. **Discriminating power independently verified via targeted sabotage-and-revert**: temporarily reduced `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::SetSamplerAddressMode` to a no-op (simulating Task 269's pre-fix silent-no-op state) — rebuilt and reran, and the test failed exactly as predicted (`Mirror` sample read Blue instead of Red, the same wrong answer a broken/reverted fix would produce); restored the two-line assignment (confirmed clean `git diff` — zero net production-code change from this task) and reconfirmed pass. ## Phase 74 — XNA compiled effect bytecode (`.fx`) support via MojoShader

> **Superseded/re-audited by [`plan_fx.md`](plan_fx.md).** The original rows below are retained as
> historical evidence and contain assumptions now known to be false (MojoShader was already pinned
> through FNA3D, genuine fixtures already existed, and direct SPIR-V is available). The production
> FNA3D vertical slice now implements the byte-array constructor, reflection/parameters,
> techniques/passes, state and sampler application, clone/lifecycle behavior, 3D and SpriteBatch
> draws, the XNB `EffectReader`, format diagnostics, and `CompiledEffects` capability. Incomplete
> renderers remain explicitly false; their rollout is tracked only in `plan_fx.md`.
>
> Row-by-row disposition of the original plan: 10200 is **obsolete** (MojoShader arrives with the
> FNA3D pin, so no separate submodule is vendored); 10201, 10206, 10207 and 10208 are **delivered**
> by `plan_fx.md` FX-011..FX-024 and FX-030..FX-038, against the six provenance-tracked `.fxb`
> fixtures plus an in-tree synthetic conformance builder; 10202 is **re-scoped** to FX-062 and
> 10203/10204 are **obsolete** as written (the pinned MojoShader emits SPIR-V directly, so the
> glslang hop FX-064 replaces them); 10205 is **carried forward** unchanged as FX-068; 10209 is
> **delivered** as `docs/fx-compiled-effects.md`. No row below should be picked up from this table.

Opened by Task 352's full-support decision. Full research/reasoning behind this phase lives in
`docs/fx-bytecode-support-plan.md` — read that first. Dedicated task-number block (`10200`+),
mirroring the WebGPU backend's `10000`+ convention (Phases 56–69), since this is comparable in
scope: a new vendored native dependency, two independent per-backend GPU-shader-translation
paths, an unknown-feasibility third backend, and real test-fixture sourcing with no XNA Content
Pipeline tooling available in this project.
