## Phase 75 — Real CNA bugs found while porting `cna-samples` (2026-07-10)

> Source: `../cna-samples`' own `DEFERRED.md` (a running log of gaps found while porting the
> official XNA 4.0 sample collection to CNA — 88 samples ported, 24 still placeholder). Every
> item below was independently re-confirmed against this branch's own current source (not just
> copied from `cna-samples`' findings against `../cna`'s `develop` branch) before being added —
> see each row's own "confirmed via" note. All are real, narrow, already-diagnosed bugs (small to
> medium effort) discovered by porting real sample games, not speculative work. Task numbers
> reference the originating `DEFERRED.md` item in parentheses for cross-referencing back to the
> full investigation writeup (`../cna-samples/DEFERRED.md`, `../cna-samples/samples/<Name>/
> missing.md`).

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 927 | Fix `ModelTypeReader::Read()`'s vertex-stride/vtable-size mismatch — corrupts vertex data for every stride-32 `.model.json` (DEFERRED.md item #26) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10, high-priority fix landed and verified on all 3 hardware-rendering backends.** Confirmed the exact bug DEFERRED.md item #26 describes: `ModelTypeReader::Read()` (`ContentManager.cpp`, mesh-parsing loop) compared the `.model.json`-declared `"vertexStride"` (a clean XNA size — 16/20/24/32) against `sizeof(Graphics::VertexPositionXxx)` directly, then `reinterpret_cast` the tightly-packed file bytes as an array of those (`IVertexType`-vtable-inflated) structs — silently reading every vertex field from the wrong byte offset for every stride-32 model in the ecosystem (confirmed via a live `sizeof()` probe matching DEFERRED.md's own numbers exactly). **Fix**: read each vertex's fields explicitly from the file's own known-clean byte offsets (`memcpy`-based `readF`/`readVec3`/`readVec2`/`readColor` helpers) and construct real, normally-initialized C++ objects (`std::vector<T>` built via `reserve()`+`emplace_back()`, not a sized constructor — `VertexPositionColorTexture`'s own declared `= default` default constructor turned out to already be silently deleted, since its `Color Color` member has no zero-arg constructor; a sized `std::vector<T>(count)` constructor would try to default-construct and fail to compile), then upload through the existing typed `VertexBuffer::SetData` overload — which already correctly packs into the compact GPU layout (confirmed by reading `VertexBuffer.cpp`'s own `SetData(const VertexPositionNormalTexture*, int)`: it does real field access through the compiler-resolved struct layout, packs into a separate tightly-packed `GpuVertex`, and was never the actual bug). All 4 vertex-format branches (16/20/24/32) fixed identically; only stride-32 (`VertexPositionNormalTexture`) has a real, exercised `.model.json` fixture anywhere in this project to test against (confirmed via `grep`: zero pre-existing tests used `Content.Load<Model>()` at all before this task). New `examples/easygl_model_json_reader_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_ModelJsonReader_Quad`) writes a real `.model.json` + binary vertex/index fixture (a flat quad, NDC x/y ∈ [-0.5, 0.5], stride-32) and renders it via `Content.Load<Model>()` + `Model::Draw()`: 2/2 PASS (quad renders White exactly where expected, Blue background elsewhere) — reused verbatim on Vulkan (`Vulkan_ModelJsonReader_Quad`, 2/2 PASS) since the fix lives entirely in shared `ContentManager.cpp`. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced exactly the predicted failure (1/2 — the quad's corrupted position landed completely outside the view frustum, matching DEFERRED.md's own "full invisibility" symptom description); restored, reconfirmed 2/2 on both backends. Full regression (EasyGL 4541 tests, Vulkan 4474 tests): zero new failures beyond each backend's own established baseline. **Bgfx needed a restructured adaptation** (`bgfx_model_json_reader_test.cpp`, per-check `RunCheck()` passes for Task 406's "first read per frame" quirk) and **surfaced a second, real, separate, pre-existing bug while verifying**: the quad renders at the exact correct position (proving this task's own fix works there too) but solid black instead of white — root-caused to `BgfxGraphicsBackend` never overriding `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` (confirmed via `grep`: only the base `IGraphicsBackend` declares it), so this indexed, Effect-bound draw silently falls back to `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`, dropping `GpuDrawParams` (BasicEffect's real `DiffuseColor`) and rendering via the `colored3D` pipeline, which expects a bound `a_color0` attribute `VertexPositionNormalTexture` never provides (GL defaults an unbound attribute to `(0,0,0,1)`, exactly matching the observed black). This is the concrete rendering impact of an already-known, already-flagged Task 766 "adjacent, out-of-scope discovery" — split to new **Task 948** rather than fixed here (unrelated in scope to this task's own vertex-stride bug). `Bgfx_ModelJsonReader_Quad` is registered and expected to show this ONE new, documented, pre-existing-class failure (1/2) until Task 948 lands — added to the established Bgfx failure baseline (`docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md`), not a surprise regression. |
| 948 | `BgfxGraphicsBackend` never overrides `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` — any indexed, Effect-bound draw silently loses all `GpuDrawParams` (diffuse color, texture, lighting, fog) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Added a real `BgfxGraphicsBackend::DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` override (`BgfxGraphicsBackend.hpp`/`.cpp`), mirroring `DrawPrimitivesEx`'s own complete `GpuDrawParams` dispatch (dualTexture/skinned/envMapping/alphaTest+vertexColor/alphaTest/lightingEnabled/textureEnabled+vertexColor/textureEnabled/no-texture-fallback branches, uniform-for-uniform) with an index buffer bound instead of a plain vertex draw — index/wireframe handling copied from the existing `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`'s own established pattern (`ExpandWireframeIndices(&ib, ...)`, `bgfx::setIndexBuffer(ib.handle)`). Previously the base `IGraphicsBackend`'s default implementation silently discarded the entire `GpuDrawParams` argument and fell back to `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` (the no-Effect legacy path), so any indexed, Effect-bound draw with a vertex format lacking a `Color` attribute rendered via the fallback `colored3D` pipeline, reading an unbound `a_color0` attribute (GL default `(0,0,0,1)` = black) regardless of the mesh's real `BasicEffect.DiffuseColor`/texture/lighting state. **Test:** the already-registered `Bgfx_ModelJsonReader_Quad` (Task 927's own test, previously a documented 1/2 known failure specifically because of this gap) now passes 2/2 — the quad's centre reads White (`BasicEffect`'s real default diffuse) instead of black. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced exactly the previously-documented failure (1/2, centre `(0,0,0)` instead of White); restored, reconfirmed 2/2. Full regression: `CnaTests` gtest suite 4361/4363 passed twice in a row (2 pre-existing hardware-dependent skips, zero failures both runs — an initial single backgrounded run showed 21 unrelated failures in `SkinnedEffect`/`SpriteEffect`/`Texture3D`/PNG-JPEG-saving tests that don't touch indexed 3D drawing at all; 2 immediate clean re-runs confirmed this was transient environmental flakiness, not a regression). Full `ctest -R "^Bgfx_"` (98 cases): 92/98 passed — the only 6 failures are the already-documented pre-existing sandbox `glReadPixels`/RenderTarget-readback crashes (`Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer`/`MsaaResolve`/`MipChain`, `Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_MipChain`/`MsaaResolve`/`DepthFormat`), unrelated to indexed drawing; `docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md`'s Bgfx known-failure count drops from 7 back to 6 now that this fix has landed. |
| 949 | Vulkan: `VulkanGraphicsBackend::GetViewportSize()` mixes logical (DPI-scaled) and physical pixel units, computing a too-small Viewport on any HiDPI/scaled display | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10, reported directly by the project owner (relayed from `mobile-eggbert`, a real consumer of this repo).** The report's own stated root cause (`viewportSet_` allegedly staying `true` forever after `RecreateSwapchain()`, pinning a stale viewport) was investigated and found **not to hold up**: both referenced tests (`Vulkan_ViewportResetAfterResize`, `Vulkan_ViewportSubregion`) already passed 8/8 and 5/5 on the *unmodified* code, because `GraphicsDevice::Present()` already calls `UpdateViewportFromWindow()` on every single frame, which re-derives the viewport from a live size query and re-invokes `backend_->SetViewport()` (refreshing `viewportW_`/`viewportH_`/`viewportSet_` every frame regardless of prior state) — this self-correcting path already covers every code path that triggers `RecreateSwapchain()` (lazy present-time recreation, `SetVirtualResolution()`, `Reset()`/`ApplyMultiSampleCount()`), confirmed by tracing each call site. **The real, verified bug found instead:** `VulkanGraphicsBackend::GetViewportSize()` called `SDL_GetWindowSize()` (logical/DPI-scaled "points"), while the swapchain itself is *always* sized in physical pixels (`CreateSwapchain()` uses `caps.currentExtent` or `SDL_GetWindowSizeInPixels()` — both real device pixels, confirmed via direct source read). On any display where the OS DPI scale ≠ 1.0 (mobile devices, Retina, Wayland fractional scaling — exactly `mobile-eggbert`'s own target), `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` would compute a Viewport smaller than the real framebuffer, rendering into only a corner of the screen. **Checked EasyGL and Bgfx too (as requested)**: both are internally consistent — neither ever calls `SDL_GetWindowSizeInPixels()` anywhere (confirmed via `grep`); both `GetViewportSize()` and their own backbuffer-resize paths (`getLogicalSize()`/`getPhysicalSize()` for EasyGL, `cachedWidth`/`cachedHeight` for Bgfx) exclusively use `SDL_GetWindowSize()` — no equivalent mismatch exists on those 2 backends. **Fix:** `GetViewportSize()` now calls `SDL_GetWindowSizeInPixels()` instead of `SDL_GetWindowSize()` — a live query in the correct (physical) units, deliberately *not* a read of the cached `swapchainExtent_` member. **A first attempt using `swapchainExtent_` directly was tried and reverted** after it regressed up to 51 gtest cases (`TextureCubeTest.*`, `AlphaTestEffectDefaultsTest.*`, etc. — confirmed via `git stash` to be clean on the true pre-fix baseline): those tests construct many short-lived `GraphicsDevice`/window instances in quick succession, and a cached `swapchainExtent_` only reflects whatever the window's real size was at the moment `CreateSwapchain()` last happened to run — wrong/stale if the window wasn't fully realized by the windowing system yet at that exact instant, with nothing to refresh it until a future resize event; a live `SDL_GetWindowSizeInPixels()` call self-corrects on every call, exactly like the original `SDL_GetWindowSize()` it replaces, just in the correct units. **Also added the project owner's own requested defensive measure**: `CleanupSwapchain()` now resets `viewportSet_ = false` — confirmed functionally inert given the self-correcting path above (every caller of `CleanupSwapchain()`/`RecreateSwapchain()` is always immediately followed, same call stack or next frame, by an `UpdateViewportFromWindow()` refresh), kept as a harmless belt-and-braces guard against a future code path that might not have that follow-up. **Cannot be pixel-verified as a discriminating test in this sandbox**: empirically confirmed via a throwaway SDL3 probe that `SDL_GetWindowPixelDensity()` is always exactly `1.0` under Xvfb/X11 regardless of the `SDL_WINDOW_HIGH_PIXEL_DENSITY` flag, so `SDL_GetWindowSize()` and `SDL_GetWindowSizeInPixels()` are numerically identical here — matches this project's own established "behaviorally inert in this sandbox, semantically correct, not fabricating fake verification" precedent (Task 928). **Verification:** both explicitly-requested tests reconfirmed 8/8 and 5/5 after the fix. Full regression revealed pre-existing, environment-driven test flakiness *unrelated to this fix*, independently confirmed identical on the true pre-fix baseline via repeated `git stash` runs: `CnaTests` (minus the already-known-flaky `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest.*` suite) showed between 0 and 43 randomly-varying failures (different, unrelated test classes each run — `EnvironmentMapEffectDefaultsTest`, `AlphaTestEffectDefaultsTest`, `TextureCubeTest`, etc.) on *both* the pre-fix and post-fix builds across repeated runs, confirming this is pre-existing Vulkan/Xvfb/llvmpipe environmental flakiness (rapid GPU-context churn under many short-lived test-fixture windows), not a regression introduced by this fix. `ctest -R "^Vulkan_"` (118 cases): 117/118 passed — only the already-documented pre-existing `Vulkan_DepthBias` failure. |
| 928 | `GraphicsDevice::Clear(const Color&)` (single-arg overload) never clears the depth/stencil buffer (DEFERRED.md item #24) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Confirmed the exact gap: `Clear(const Color&)` (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:234`) only ever forwarded to the color-only `Clear(r,g,b,a)` overload. **Fix** (matching real FNA's own `Clear(Color)` implementation exactly, confirmed by reading `FNA/src/Graphics/GraphicsDevice.cs:791-799`): forward to `Clear(ClearOptions::Target | ClearOptions::DepthBuffer | ClearOptions::Stencil, color, getViewportProperty().getMaxDepthProperty(), 0)` — FNA's own reference uses `Viewport.MaxDepth` (the device's own **current** viewport's `MaxDepth` property, not a hardcoded `1.0f` constant — a `GraphicsDevice` property read, confirmed via the FNA source), so the fix reads the real current value rather than assuming a fixed default. **No pixel test added — deliberately, not an oversight**: investigated both hardware-rendering backends directly and found this fix is currently **behaviorally inert on both**: `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::Clear(r,g,b,a)` already unconditionally calls `device.clear(ClearFlags::Color | ClearFlags::Depth)` regardless of what's requested (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:1494`), and `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own render-pass setup hardcodes `depthAtt.loadOp = VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_CLEAR` unconditionally too (confirmed via `grep`, multiple render-pass-creation sites) — both backends already clear depth on every `Clear()`/render-pass-begin as an existing implementation detail, independent of the `ClearOptions` `GraphicsDevice` computes. This matches `DEFERRED.md`'s own honest framing ("Blocked samples: none outright... worth fixing on general principle" — the cna-samples authors also found this via source reading, not an empirically-reproduced rendering bug). The fix is still correct and worth having (matches FNA's real, documented API contract exactly, and would matter for any future backend or if either existing backend's own unconditional-clear behavior ever changes) — verified via full regression instead of a dedicated pixel test, since `Clear()` is exercised pervasively by nearly every other test in the suite: EasyGL 4541 tests (zero new failures beyond the established 3-item baseline), Vulkan 4474 tests (zero new failures beyond the established 4-item baseline). `ClearOptions::Stencil` is correctly requested by this fix too, but stencil-clearing itself remains unimplemented on any backend (a separate, already-tracked gap, Task 871) — requesting it now is forward-compatible with that future fix, not a regression. |
| 929 | `Game::DoInitialize()` subscribes `Components_.ComponentAdded`/`ComponentRemoved` *after* calling the user's `Initialize()` override, unlike real XNA/FNA (DEFERRED.md item #23) | ✅ | **CLOSED, 2026-07-10 — no bug found; DEFERRED.md item #23's own root-cause claim ("real XNA/FNA's `Game` constructor subscribes immediately") is factually wrong, confirmed by reading FNA's actual source directly rather than trusting the cited claim.** Read `FNA/src/Game.cs`'s real constructor (lines 241-281) in full: it does **not** subscribe `Components.ComponentAdded` anywhere. The subscription lives in FNA's own `private void DoInitialize()` (`Game.cs:760-793`) — with the **exact same ordering CNA already has**: `Initialize()` is called first (line 777), and `Components.ComponentAdded += OnComponentAdded;`/`ComponentRemoved += ...` are subscribed *after* (lines 791-792), with FNA's own explicit code comment justifying it: `"We need to do this after virtual Initialize(...) is called. 1. Categorize components... 2. Subscribe to Added/Removed events to keep the categorized lists synced and to Initialize future components as they are added."` This is intentional, documented real FNA design, not an oversight this task should "fix." **Why the common "add a `DrawableGameComponent` from inside `Initialize()`" pattern still works in real FNA despite this ordering**: FNA's own base `protected virtual void Initialize()` (`Game.cs:623-663`) loops with `for (int i = 0; i < Components.Count; i += 1)` — deliberately **re-evaluating `Components.Count` every iteration** (confirmed by FNA's own comment: `"it's actually possible to add something during initialization, and those must also be initialized... let's worry about initializing everything we get"`), so any component present in (or added *during* this same loop, via another component's own `Initialize()`) gets its own `Initialize()`/`LoadContent()` called regardless of `ComponentAdded` subscription timing. **Confirmed CNA's own base `Game::Initialize()` (`Game.cpp:513-522`) already uses the identical re-evaluating-`Components_.getCountProperty()` loop pattern** — not cached into a local variable, matching FNA's own mechanic exactly. **Root cause of the original Graphics3D/PickingSample segfault this row is really about**: adding a component from *inside* a user's `Initialize()` override **after** that override's own call to `base.Initialize()` (i.e., `base.Initialize()`'s own re-evaluating loop has already finished running by the time the late-added component exists) is a genuine, well-known XNA/FNA gotcha in **both** engines equally — FNA's own constructor-area comment elsewhere warns exactly this ("if this is late, you can still create it yourself... but Begin/EndDraw will not get called... make the service before calling Run()"). The `AddComponent()` workaround `../cna-samples` applied (`Components.Add(component)` + an explicit `component->Initialize()`) is the objectively correct, XNA-idiomatic fix for **this specific ordering pitfall in real game code**, not a workaround for a CNA framework bug — CNA's own `Game`/`DoInitialize()`/base-`Initialize()` mechanics already faithfully match FNA in every respect checked here. **No code changed** — changing `DoInitialize()`'s subscription order as originally proposed would have introduced a **real, new deviation from FNA**, not fixed one. |
| 930 | `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` have no `GetData<T>()` — no way to read back GPU buffer contents (DEFERRED.md item #25) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Added `GetData(T*, int count)` + `GetData(T*, int startIndex, int elementCount)` to `VertexBuffer` (5 vertex types: `VertexPositionColor`, `VertexPositionColorTexture`, `VertexPositionNormalTexture`, `VertexPositionTexture`, NOXNA `VertexPositionNormalTextureSkinned`) and to `IndexBuffer` (`std::uint16_t`, `std::uint32_t`) — 14 new overloads total, all documented with Doxygen. **Implementation approach — CPU-side shadow buffer, not a per-backend GPU-readback virtual (the alternative DEFERRED.md item #25 itself suggested):** every existing `SetData` overload on both classes now additionally caches its just-uploaded bytes into a new private `std::vector<std::uint8_t> cpuShadow_` member (the compact packed GPU layout for `VertexBuffer`, the raw typed array for `IndexBuffer`); `GetData` reads back from this cache instead of round-tripping through the GPU. Justified because nothing in the XNA 4.0 pipeline ever writes back into a `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` from the GPU side — a CPU shadow is fully faithful, not an approximation — and it works identically across all backends without adding a new virtual method to `IVertexBufferBackend`/`IIndexBufferBackend` (mirrors the already-established `Texture2D::SetData`/`GetData` shadow-buffer precedent, and bgfx's own pre-existing `cpuData` cache on its dynamic buffers, which exists for the same underlying reason — bgfx's dynamic buffers have no GPU readback API at all). Guards added matching `SetData`'s existing pattern: `ObjectDisposedException` if disposed, `NotSupportedException` if `BufferUsage::WriteOnly`, `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` if the requested range exceeds what has actually been uploaded so far. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_vertexbuffer_indexbuffer_getdata_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_VertexBufferIndexBufferGetData`) constructs a real `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` against a live `GraphicsDevice`, uploads known distinct per-vertex/per-index values via `SetData`, and asserts `GetData` (both the full and the `startIndex`/`elementCount` slice overload) round-trips them exactly via each type's own `operator==` — 20/20 PASS, covering all 5 vertex types + both index widths + the `WriteOnly`/out-of-range guard paths. Reused verbatim on Vulkan (`Vulkan_VertexBufferIndexBufferGetData`, 20/20 PASS) since the fix lives entirely in shared `VertexBuffer.cpp`/`IndexBuffer.cpp`. **Discriminating power verified via targeted mutation, not `git stash`** (this is new functionality, not a fix to existing behavior — there is no prior implementation to revert to): temporarily removed the `cpuShadow_`-caching lines from one `VertexBuffer::SetData` overload and one `IndexBuffer::SetData` overload, rebuilt, and confirmed the test immediately fails (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, empty shadow buffer) rather than silently passing; reverted the mutation, rebuilt, reconfirmed 20/20 PASS. Full regression: EasyGL 4359 gtest cases (0 new failures; 2 pre-existing hardware-dependent skips unrelated to this task) + full `ctest -R "^EasyGL_"` (178 tests, only the 2 already-documented pre-existing failures `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments` and `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` — independently reconfirmed via `git stash` that both fail identically with this task's changes fully removed, i.e. pre-existing, not a regression); Vulkan `ctest -R "^Vulkan_"` (114 tests, only the already-documented pre-existing `Vulkan_DepthBias` failure, Task 868). |
| 931 | `ModelTypeReader::Read()` hardcodes 16-bit indices — no way to `Content.Load<Model>()` a mesh with more than 65535 vertices (DEFERRED.md item #6 addendum) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Confirmed the exact gap: `ContentManager.cpp`'s mesh-parsing loop unconditionally divided the index file's byte length by `sizeof(std::uint16_t)` and always built a `SixteenBits` `IndexBuffer` via the NOXNA convenience constructor, regardless of vertex count. **Fix:** auto-select `IndexElementSize::ThirtyTwoBits` whenever `numVertices > 65535` (mirroring real XNA's stock `ModelProcessor`, which makes this same selection automatically — no explicit per-mesh JSON field needed, since a mesh that large could not have been produced with 16-bit indices in the first place), branching both the index-count byte-stride divisor and the `IndexBuffer` constructor/`SetData` overload (`std::uint16_t*` vs `std::uint32_t*`) accordingly; the full 4-arg `IndexBuffer(device, IndexElementSize, count, BufferUsage)` constructor is now used explicitly instead of the always-16-bit convenience overload. Confirmed both `IndexBuffer`/`IIndexBufferBackend` (EasyGL and Vulkan) already fully implemented 32-bit indices end-to-end beforehand (`CreateIndexBuffer32`, a real `GL_UNSIGNED_INT` draw path) — the gap was specifically `ModelTypeReader`'s hardcoded assumption, not the underlying buffer classes, so no backend code needed to change. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_model_json_reader_32bit_indices_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_ModelJsonReader_32BitIndices`) writes a real `.model.json` + binary fixture with 65540 `VertexPositionNormalTexture` vertices (the first 65536 inert zeroed filler, purely to push the mesh past the 65535-vertex ceiling) and an index buffer whose 6 index values (65536-65539) all *require* the full 32 bits to represent — proving the fix reads real ≥65536 index values correctly, not just a vertex-count threshold check. Renders the same NDC `[-0.5, 0.5]` quad pattern as the Task 927 test: 2/2 PASS (centre White, outside Blue background). Reused verbatim on Vulkan (`Vulkan_ModelJsonReader_32BitIndices`, 2/2 PASS) since the fix lives entirely in shared `ContentManager.cpp`. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced exactly the predicted failure (1/2 — centre stayed the Blue background colour, since the pre-fix code misread the ≥65536 index values against a 16-bit assumption and never actually drew the quad's real geometry); restored, reconfirmed 2/2 on both backends. Full regression (EasyGL 4359 gtest + 179 `ctest` cases, Vulkan 115 `ctest` cases): zero new failures beyond each backend's own already-documented pre-existing baseline (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_DepthBias`). |
| 932 | `.model.json`/`ModelTypeReader::Read()` has no per-mesh `"texture"` field — every `Content.Load<Model>`-loaded mesh renders untextured (DEFERRED.md item #6 addendum) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Confirmed the exact gap: the mesh-parsing loop in `ContentManager.cpp` extracted `"name"`/`"vertices"`/`"indices"`/`"vertexStride"`/`"effect"` per mesh but no `"texture"` field, so every `Content.Load<Model>`-based mesh rendered with `BasicEffect.TextureEnabled == false` and the class-default plain-white `DiffuseColor` regardless of the source asset's own real material texture. **Fix:** added a `"texture"` field to the mesh schema (root-relative, same convention as `"vertices"`/`"indices"`); when present and the mesh's effect resolves to a `BasicEffect` (via `dynamic_cast`, since a custom `"effect"`-loaded shader has no standard texture slot to bind through here), loads it via the existing `cm.Load<Graphics::Texture2D>()` path and calls `setTextureProperty()`/`setTextureEnabledProperty(true)`. Added a new `ModelResources::textureOwners` (`std::vector<std::unique_ptr<Texture2D>>`) to keep each loaded texture alive for the Model's lifetime — deliberately heap-indirected like the existing `vbs`/`ibs` pattern (not a `std::vector<Texture2D>` of values), since `BasicEffect::setTextureProperty()` takes a raw pointer that must stay stable across any later `push_back`-triggered reallocation of the owning vector. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_model_json_reader_texture_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_ModelJsonReader_Texture`) writes the same NDC `[-0.5, 0.5]` quad fixture as the Task 927 test plus a tiny hand-encoded 2×2 solid-Red QOI texture (a minimal from-scratch QOI encoder emitting every pixel as a literal `QOI_OP_RGBA` chunk — always spec-valid, no run/diff/index optimization needed for a fixed-color fixture) and a `"texture"` field naming it: 2/2 PASS (centre Red — texture correctly sampled and multiplied by the untouched white `DiffuseColor`; outside Blue background). Reused verbatim on Vulkan (`Vulkan_ModelJsonReader_Texture`, 2/2 PASS) since the fix lives entirely in shared `ContentManager.cpp`. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced exactly the predicted failure (1/2 — centre rendered plain White, `BasicEffect`'s untextured default, instead of Red). Full regression (EasyGL 4359 gtest + 180 `ctest` cases, Vulkan 116 `ctest` cases): zero new failures beyond each backend's own already-documented pre-existing baseline (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_DepthBias`). **Note:** the severe "solid white silhouette" visual consequence this row originally described (untextured mesh combined with `EnsureLit3DProgram()`'s no-op white fallback texture and XNA's bright default 3-point lighting rig clamping the lit RGB product to `(1,1,1)`) is now avoided for any mesh that supplies a real `"texture"` field; a mesh that genuinely has no texture asset still renders the same way as before (untouched, out of scope for this task). |
| 933 | EasyGL: a full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch` draw before any 3D draw call in the same frame breaks that frame's 3D rendering entirely (DEFERRED.md item #28) | ✅ | **INVESTIGATED, 2026-07-10 — NOT reproduced in isolation across 4 targeted variants; remains open, not closed.** Read `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend`'s full flush path (`FlushBatch()`) and the 3D draw path (`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`/`DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`) side by side first: SpriteBatch owns its own dedicated VAO (`vao_`, attribute locations 0/1/2), unbound (`glBindVertexArray(0)`) at the end of every `FlushBatch()`; every 3D `VertexBuffer` likewise owns its own dedicated, persistent VAO (`EasyGLVertexBufferBackend::vao`, configured once via `ApplyLayout()`), bound fresh before every draw and unbound after — these are two independent GL objects, so no VAO/attribute-binding bleed is possible by construction. `SpriteBatch::Begin()` (the high-level XNA class, not the backend) already explicitly resets `DepthStencilState` to `DepthStencilState::None` on every call (with its own code comment citing a prior, already-fixed version of exactly this class of bug), matching real FNA's contract — so the 4 states `../cna-samples`' own report says were tried-and-failed (`BlendState`/`RasterizerState`/`DepthStencilState`/`SamplerState`) were, in fact, already being correctly reset even before any manual reset attempt. Built 4 minimal, isolated repro variants (a temporary local test executable, `examples/easygl_spritebatch_then_3d_repro_test.cpp`, deleted after this investigation once its findings were captured here — not kept as a permanent regression test, since all 4 currently pass and it asserts no CNA behavioral contract) to test each remaining plausible distinguishing factor one at a time, always preceded by the identical full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch.Begin()/Draw()/End()` call plus the same 4-state reset `../cna-samples` already tried: **(A)** `GraphicsDevice::DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` (temporary/no persistent GPU buffer) with `BasicEffect` + identity World/View/Projection — PASSED (3D quad rendered correctly). **(B)** a real, persistent `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` + `SetVertexBuffer`/`setIndicesProperty` + `DrawIndexedPrimitives` + `EffectPass::Apply()`, matching `ModelMesh::Draw()`'s own exact call shape (the actual code path EnvmapDemo's saucer model — loaded via item #26's RawMesh-style bypass — would have gone through, as opposed to variant A's very different temporary-buffer path) — PASSED. **(C)** variant B repeated with a real, non-identity perspective camera (`Matrix::CreateLookAt`/`CreatePerspectiveFieldOfView`) across 3 real iterations (ruling out both "needs a real camera" and "only manifests from the 2nd frame onward due to some lazy-init/cache effect") — PASSED. **(D)** `EnvironmentMapEffect` + a real 6-face `TextureCube` (the *actual* effect EnvmapDemo used, not `BasicEffect` — `../cna-samples`' own report only tested swapping *away* from it, never tested it in a from-scratch minimal repro) + the same real perspective camera across 3 iterations — PASSED (a non-white pixel confirmed the env-mapped quad genuinely drew over the SpriteBatch's own white full-screen sprite). **None of the 4 variants reproduce the reported "3D content renders nothing" symptom** — this rules out, with actual working test code rather than reasoning alone: draw-call shape (user-array vs. persistent-buffer), effect type (`BasicEffect` vs. `EnvironmentMapEffect`), camera matrices (identity vs. real perspective), and single-shot vs. repeated-frame execution as the differentiating factor. **One genuinely real, separate, independently-confirmed latent bug found while reading the flush path (not yet connected to this task's own reported symptom, and NOT fixed here — out of this task's scope, a separate finding)**: `EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::FlushBatch()` unconditionally calls `device_.set_viewport(0, 0, physW, physH)` (the backend's own *physical* window/framebuffer size) on every single flush, regardless of what `GraphicsDevice`'s own currently-active logical `Viewport` actually is, and **never restores the prior GL viewport afterward** — confirmed via direct code read (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp` `FlushBatch()`, ~line 1074). This cannot explain the reported symptom on its own (it fires identically regardless of sprite size, contradicting the report's own "a small `DrawTitle()` draw does NOT trigger it" observation — ruled out via that same reasoning, not empirically retested), but IS a real bug in its own right, and remains a live suspect if the true EnvmapDemo repro turns out to involve a bound `RenderTarget2D` (whose own viewport can legitimately differ from the physical window size) at some point earlier in the frame — a factor none of the 4 variants above tested. **Remaining untested variables, in descending order of suspected relevance:** (1) a bound `RenderTarget2D` active earlier in the same frame (ties directly to the viewport-reset finding above); (2) a texture loaded via real file decode (`Content.Load<Texture2D>`, going through `Texture2DTypeReader`/`stb_image`) rather than an in-memory `SetData` call, in case a `GL_UNPACK_ALIGNMENT`/pixel-store side effect from decoding a real, non-power-of-two-dimensioned image (EnvmapDemo's real backbuffer is 480×800) is involved; (3) genuine separate `Present()`-separated frames via the real SDL game loop (`Update()`/`Draw()` cycling), as opposed to this investigation's own 3 same-`Draw()`-call iterations. **Where to look next:** either instrument the actual `../cna-samples` `ReachGraphicsDemo` `EnvmapDemo` scene directly (`glGetError`/state-debugger instrumentation mid-scene, as originally proposed), since 4 good-faith isolated attempts in `cna_graphics` alone did not reproduce it; or have the project owner confirm which of the 3 remaining untested variables (RenderTarget, real file texture, real multi-frame `Present()`) to try next. Effort M investigation spent; root cause still not found — leaving open rather than fabricating a fix for a symptom that could not be reproduced.

**Follow-up investigation, 2026-07-16 — all 3 previously "remaining untested variables" now tried (RenderTarget2D skipped, confirmed inapplicable), STILL not reproduced; remains open, not closed.** Confirmed variable (1) doesn't apply here first: `EnvmapDemo` never binds a `RenderTarget2D` anywhere in its scene (`../cna-samples/samples/ReachGraphicsDemo/src/EnvmapDemo.hpp`, read directly), so the viewport-reset bug the 2026-07-10 pass found can't be the trigger for *this* item regardless of its own validity elsewhere — deprioritized in favor of the 2 variables that do apply. Built a new, single temporary repro executable (`examples/easygl_task933_investigation_test.cpp`, same disposable-investigation convention as the original 4 variants — deleted after this pass, `CMakeLists.txt` reverted) combining variables (2) and (3) together with every other real-fidelity detail `DEFERRED.md` item #28's own account describes, verified against `EnvmapDemo.hpp`'s actual source line-by-line rather than reasoning from the summary alone:
- Real window size **480×800** (matching `EnvmapDemo`'s own backbuffer exactly, not an arbitrary test-default size).
- A real **3-frame `Game::Run()` loop** with genuine `GraphicsDevice::Present()` calls between frames (`Game::EndDraw()`'s normal cycle), not same-`Draw()`-call iterations with no `Present()` — the readback only happens on frame 3.
- The `SpriteBatch`'s background texture, a `RawMesh`-style base texture, and all 6 `TextureCube` faces **all real-file-decoded** from temp `.qoi` fixtures on disk through the exact same `Texture2D(path, device)` → `ImageLoader::Load()` → `IMG_Load` (SDL_image) path `ContentManager::Load<Texture2D>()` itself uses — not `SetData()` shortcuts.
- **Two** `SpriteBatch.Begin()/Draw()/End()` calls before the 3D draw (a full-backbuffer background sprite **and** a small corner "title" sprite), matching every one of `EnvmapDemo`'s 5 sibling scenes' real per-frame structure — not just the single full-backbuffer draw in isolation.
- 3D geometry uploaded into a real, persistent `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer`, drawn via `SetVertexBuffer`/`setIndicesProperty` + a `pass.Apply()`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives` loop — `RawMesh::Draw()`'s exact call shape (`../cna-samples/samples/ReachGraphicsDemo/src/RawMesh.hpp`), not `DrawUserPrimitives`.
- The real camera **verbatim**: `CreateLookAt((4500,-400,0), (0,-400,0), Up)`, `CreatePerspectiveFieldOfView(PiOver4, aspect, 10, 10000)` — not a simplified near camera (quad scaled/translated to the view target so it's still visible and lands on the exact screen centre at that distance, geometry re-oriented to face the camera head-on at that position).
- `EnvironmentMapEffect` + a real `TextureCube`, `DepthStencilState::Default`, and the 2-arg `Clear(color, 1.0f)` matching `EnvmapDemo`'s own DEFERRED #24 workaround.
- 3D content reused Task 399/469's already-proven combined-scene formula (`easygl_environmentmapeffect_golden_test.cpp`) so a correct render has a known expected centre pixel, (151,101,76) ±20, to check against instead of just "non-background ⇒ pass".

Built incrementally (each addition re-run before the next) rather than all at once, and an explicit `CNA_TEST_SKIP_SPRITEBATCH=1` A/B toggle was checked at every stage: **every single stage — the minimal version and the final, maximally-faithful combined version — rendered the expected (151,101,76) centre pixel correctly, both with and without the `SpriteBatch` calls.** The bug did not reproduce at any point in this build-up. This now rules out, on top of the 2026-07-10 pass's own 4 ruled-out factors, all 3 originally-flagged "remaining untested variables" (RenderTarget2D binding — confirmed inapplicable to this scene; real file-decoded textures; real `Present()`-separated multi-frame execution) plus 2 additional structural details the 2026-07-10 pass didn't cover (a second small `SpriteBatch` call before the 3D draw, matching the real per-scene structure; the exact real long-distance camera). Two independent, good-faith investigation passes (2026-07-10 and 2026-07-16) totaling well over Effort M have now failed to reproduce this symptom outside the real `../cna-samples` `ReachGraphicsDemo` binary itself. **Recommendation, not acted on here:** the only remaining productive next step is instrumenting the actual `EnvmapDemo` scene live (temporarily re-adding its own original `SpriteBatch`-based background draw in place of the current `DrawBackgroundQuad()` workaround, then running the real `ReachGraphicsDemo` binary itself under `glGetError`/state-debugger instrumentation) rather than further from-scratch synthetic repro attempts in `cna_graphics` — every synthetic angle this project's own two investigations could think of has now been tried and passed. Left open (⬜), not closed — a real report with a detailed, credible isolation account exists and should not be dismissed as invalid just because it hasn't been reproduced synthetically twice. ReachGraphicsDemo's existing `DrawBackgroundQuad()` workaround should remain in place regardless. **Resolution, 2026-08-23 (`SAMPLE-005`):** the fresh line-by-line port removed both `DrawBackgroundQuad()` and the `RawMesh` family, restored the original full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch` draw plus official XNA-pipeline Model/TextureCube content, and rendered EnvmapDemo correctly on OPENGLES3 and real Chrome WEBGL2. The browser pass did expose and fix two concrete EasyGL defects in the declaration-bearing official-model path: semantic remapping unbound its VAO before `glDraw*`, and GLES/WebGL attempted base-vertex entry points outside their guaranteed API floor. Those findings do not retrospectively prove which change caused the 2026-07 report, but the exact actual consumer and source shape now pass with no workaround, so the current symptom is closed rather than preserving obsolete sample guidance. |
| 934 | Add `TextureCubeTypeReader` for `Content.Load<TextureCube>()` (DEFERRED.md item #14) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Added `TextureCubeTypeReader : ContentTypeReader<Graphics::TextureCube>` (`.dds` extension) to `ContentManager.cpp`'s built-in type readers, delegating to the already-existing, already-proven `TextureCube::DDSFromStreamEXT(GraphicsDevice&, System::IO::Stream&)` decoder (used directly by every pre-existing `examples/easygl_texturecube_*` test) via a `System::IO::FileStream` opened on the resolved path — mirrors `Texture2DTypeReader`'s own shape exactly, as the row originally specified. **Real architectural snag found and fixed along the way (not anticipated by the row's own "Effort S" estimate, but small once found):** `TextureCube`'s copy constructor is deleted (`NOXNA TextureCube(const TextureCube&) = delete`, move-only), so the generic `ContentManager::Load<T>()` template's strong `std::any`-based cache (`loadedAssets_[key] = result`) fails to compile for it — the exact same problem `SoundEffect` (also move-only) already solved via its own dedicated, not-cached `template<> Load<Audio::SoundEffect>()` specialisation. Added an identical `template<> Graphics::TextureCube ContentManager::Load<Graphics::TextureCube>(const std::string&)` specialisation (declared in `ContentManager.hpp`, defined in `ContentManager.cpp`, plus a new `class TextureCube;` forward declaration in the header) that skips caching entirely — each call does a fresh decode, exactly matching what a cache MISS already did before this specialisation existed. Deliberately did **not** attempt `Texture2D`'s own weak-cache-with-GPU-backend-reuse mechanism for `TextureCube` — that's substantial new infrastructure genuinely out of this task's narrow, already-small scope; a future task can add cube-map instance sharing separately if it ever becomes a real need. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_texturecube_content_load_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_TextureCube_ContentLoad`) hand-encodes a real, minimal, single-mip DXT1-compressed `.dds` cubemap from scratch (128-byte header matching `DDSFromStreamEXT`'s own exact byte offsets, 4×4 texels/face — the smallest valid square DXT1 size, one full compression block per face — each face a distinct solid color via a same-endpoint DXT1 block, i.e. `color0 == color1`, all 16 pixel indices `0b00`, valid regardless of DXT1's two interpolation-mode branches), writes it to a temp file, loads it via `Content.Load<TextureCube>()`, and reads back every face via `TextureCube::GetData()`: 7/7 PASS (correct face size plus all 6 faces decoding to their own distinct, correctly-ordered color, confirming `DDSFromStreamEXT`'s own `static_cast<CubeMapFace>(face)` loop order). Reused verbatim on Vulkan (`Vulkan_TextureCube_ContentLoad`, 7/7 PASS) since the fix lives entirely in shared `ContentManager.cpp`/`.hpp`. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced a hard compile failure (the exact `std::any`/deleted-copy-constructor error this fix solves), an even stronger confirmation than a runtime-only revert. Full regression (EasyGL 4359 gtest + 181 `ctest` cases, Vulkan 117 `ctest` cases): zero new failures beyond each backend's own already-documented pre-existing baseline (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_DepthBias`). |

> **SAMPLE-014 correction — 2026-08-24.** Spacewar's original Evolved renderer calls
> `Content.Load<TextureCube>()` while drawing and depends on XNA's strong asset cache. The
> move-only/uncached exception recorded by Task 934 was a CNA implementation limitation, not XNA
> behavior: it decoded and uploaded the cubemap XNB every frame and reduced the browser build to
> about 1 FPS. TextureCube now uses the same copyable shared-resource wrapper convention as
> Texture2D, the dedicated uncached specialization is gone, and generic `ContentManager::Load<T>`
> retains the cube until `Unload()`. Copy-sharing and strong-cache/unload tests cover the correction;
> the unchanged faithful Spacewar port runs its Evolved scene continuously in hardware WebGL2,
> with steady-state draw diagnostics reduced from tens of milliseconds to roughly 1--2 ms after
> the one-time content load.

| 935 | Add a texture-less `VertexPositionNormal` vertex type + wire it into the existing lit shader path (DEFERRED.md item #5, Primitives3D-specific) | ⛔ | **CLOSED, NOT IMPLEMENTED (project-owner decision, 2026-07-10) — after 2 rounds of deeper investigation revealed this is a much bigger job than "Effort S" for a consumer that turns out not to actually need it.** **Investigation finding #1** (byte-stride collision): a packed CNA-internal GPU vertex for Position+Normal only is exactly 24 bytes — the SAME stride CNA already uses for `VertexPositionColorTexture` (Position+Color+TexCoord) on both EasyGL and Vulkan, both of which dispatch vertex-attribute layout AND shader selection purely by raw byte stride (confirmed via `grep`: `case 24:` already means `VertexPositionColorTexture` in both `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp` and `VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp`). Adding a same-stride `VertexPositionNormal` would make the two formats indistinguishable to any backend — a real risk of silent data corruption if a game ever used both types in the same process. Project owner chose "pad the internal packed layout to a disambiguating 28 bytes" to resolve this (a real, viable fix, never actually implemented — investigation continued into finding #2 before writing any code). **Investigation finding #2** (per-backend pipeline rigidity, the actual reason this closed): EasyGL's vertex layout is genuinely flexible at runtime (a GL VAO can bind any attribute set via `ApplyLayout()`, so adding a `case 28:` there — 2 attributes instead of 3, no new shader needed, the fragment shader's existing 1×1-white-texture fallback already handles "no real UV" gracefully) — straightforward. **Vulkan is not**: `GetOrCreatePipelineLitTextured3D` (`VulkanGraphicsBackend.cpp:4284`) hardcodes `constexpr std::size_t kLitStride = 32` and a fixed 3-attribute (`pos`+`normal`+`UV`) `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription` layout baked directly into the compiled pipeline+shader pair — Vulkan pipelines require an exact attribute-for-attribute match between the shader's declared inputs and the bound vertex layout (unlike GL's flexible/gracefully-defaulted disabled-attribute behavior), so a 28-byte, 2-attribute buffer cannot be fed through this pipeline at all; a real, new, separate shader pair (SPIR-V) and pipeline variant would be required just for Vulkan, with Bgfx's own similarly rigid `bgfx::VertexLayout`-per-shader mechanism not even investigated yet. This is a materially larger, multi-backend shader-authoring task, not a "just add a switch case" one. **Why closed rather than scoped down to EasyGL-only:** checked the actual downstream consumer directly — `../cna-samples`' own `samples/Primitives3D/missing.md` (2026-07-06 update) already concludes, independently and for its own reasons, that **no CNA engine change is wanted here at all**: *"The pragmatic fix no longer needs a CNA engine change: assign a dummy/unused texcoord (e.g. (0,0)) to every generated vertex and switch this port to `VertexPositionNormalTexture` + the already-working lit shader, rather than waiting for CNA to add a texture-less variant."* Primitives3D is already shipped (`PLAN.md`: "✅ Done") using that exact workaround. Presented all of this to the project owner via `AskUserQuestion` (byte-collision fix vs. full 4-backend build-out vs. EasyGL-only vs. stop entirely); project owner chose to stop, matching the downstream sample's own independently-reached preference. **No source code changed** — this is a pure scope/decision closure, not a partial implementation. |

---

## Phase 76 — Multi-bone rigid-part model hierarchy (DEFERRED.md item #6, main)

> `Content.Load<Model>()` already works end-to-end for **single-bone (fully static or
> single-rigid-body) models** — proven by `examples/easygl_model_draw_test.cpp` and dozens of
> shipped `cna-samples` ports. The gap here is specifically **multi-bone rigid-part hierarchies**:
> a model whose different meshes need to move/rotate independently of each other (e.g. a tank's
> wheels/turret/cannon/hatch, each animated separately but never *skinned* — no smooth per-vertex
> blending, just rigid per-mesh transforms), which today collapses to a single synthetic "Root"
> bone with every mesh's parent-bone left null. This is a real, moderate-scope, well-diagnosed CNA
> code gap (confirmed still present, see Task 936/937 below) — approved by the project owner
> 2026-07-10 after review of `../cna-samples`' own `DEFERRED.md` item #6 and `SplitScreen`'s
> `missing.md` writeup. Distinct from, and a prerequisite for reasoning about, Phase 77's much
> larger skeletal-*animation* gap below (a rigid multi-bone hierarchy has no smooth per-vertex
> blending or keyframe playback at all — see Phase 77's own intro for exactly where the two
> diverge).

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 936 | Add `ModelMesh::setParentBoneProperty(ModelBone*)` | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Confirmed real FNA's `ModelMesh.ParentBone` is `get; internal set;` (`FNA/src/Graphics/ModelMesh.cs`); mirrored the exact same "internal set → plain public C++ setter" pattern already established for `ModelMeshPart::setEffectProperty` (also `internal set` in real FNA) rather than inventing a new visibility mechanism. Added `setParentBoneProperty(ModelBone* value)` to `ModelMesh.hpp`/`.cpp`, a one-line field assignment. 2 new gtest cases (`ModelMeshTests.cpp`): stores a given pointer, and a subsequent `nullptr` call clears it back. Discriminating power verified via `git stash`: reverting reproduces a hard compile failure (no such member). Full regression: 4359/4361 passed (2 pre-existing hardware skips), zero new failures. Small, mechanical addition — the real work is Task 937 (actually calling it with a real per-mesh bone). |
| 937 | `ModelTypeReader::Read()` builds one real `ModelBone` per mesh instead of a single synthetic "Root" (or parses an explicit bone hierarchy from `.model.json`) | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Implemented option (a) from this row's own note: for each parsed mesh, `ModelTypeReader::Read()` now constructs a real child `ModelBone` (named after the mesh, indexed sequentially after Root) via `ModelBone::AddChild()` (which correctly sets both the child's `Parent` pointer and Root's own `Children` collection), then calls Task 936's new `mesh->setParentBoneProperty(meshBone.get())` — no JSON schema change needed. Each new bone's `Transform` is left at `ModelBone`'s own default (Identity); baking a real relative per-part offset is deferred to Task 938's own asset-regeneration note, out of this task's scope. `Model`'s own constructor (`root_ = bones_.bones_[0]`) already guarantees the Root bone pushed first stays at a stable index regardless of how many per-mesh bones get appended after it — confirmed by reading `Model.cpp` directly before relying on this ordering. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_model_json_reader_bone_hierarchy_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_ModelJsonReader_BoneHierarchy`) is a pure structural test (no rendering) — a `.model.json` fixture with 2 distinctly-named meshes ("Wheel", "Turret") loaded via `Content.Load<Model>()`, asserting `Bones.Count == 3`, `Bones["Wheel"]`/`Bones["Turret"]` both resolve to real, distinct, non-null bones via the real XNA `ModelBoneCollection` name indexer, each resolved bone's `Parent` is the model's own `Root`, and each `ModelMesh`'s own `ParentBone` matches its corresponding named bone exactly: 8/8 PASS. Reused verbatim on Vulkan (`Vulkan_ModelJsonReader_BoneHierarchy`, 8/8 PASS) since the fix lives entirely in shared `ContentManager.cpp`. **Discriminating power verified via `git stash` revert-and-rebuild**: reverting reproduced an even stronger failure than a graceful assertion mismatch — an uncaught `std::out_of_range` ("ModelBoneCollection: bone not found: Wheel"), since no per-mesh bone existed at all pre-fix; restored, reconfirmed 8/8 on both backends. Full regression: EasyGL 4359 gtest + 182 `ctest` cases, Vulkan 118 `ctest` cases — zero new failures beyond each backend's own already-documented pre-existing baseline. Unblocks the real motivation for this whole phase (Task 938): `SplitScreen`/`TankOnAHeightMap`/`SimpleAnimation`/`CustomModelClassSample`'s own multi-part rigid-model bone lookups (`Model.Bones["PartName"]`) now resolve to real bones instead of throwing. |
| 938 | Verification: SplitScreen (or an equivalent multi-part rigid model) renders with independently-posable mesh parts via `Content.Load<Model>` | ⬜ | End-to-end proof that Tasks 936/937 actually unblock the real samples that motivated this phase — `SplitScreen`, `SimpleAnimation`, `TankOnAHeightMap`, and `CustomModelClassSample`'s own multi-part case (all currently `🚧 Placeholder` in `../cna-samples/plan.md`, citing this exact gap). Regenerating `tank.model.json` via the already-fixed `tools/fbx_ascii2model.py` (see Task 927's own note on that tool's unrelated node-transform fix) may be needed to bake each wheel/turret/cannon/hatch mesh's own local translation correctly once bones are per-mesh — confirmed by `../cna-samples/plan.md`'s own note that this regeneration was deliberately deferred until a consumer actually needs independent per-part motion. This task belongs to whoever picks up the actual sample port in `cna-samples`, not `cna_graphics` — listed here only to close the loop on what "done" means for this phase. |

## Phase 77 — Skeletal animation playback (DEFERRED.md item #13)

> **Large, genuinely new engine capability — approved by the project owner 2026-07-10.** Unlike
> Phase 76 (rigid multi-part hierarchy, no smooth blending) or Phase 74 (compiled `.fx` bytecode
> parsing), this phase is about the standard XNA "Skinned Model Sample" pipeline: playing back a
> *skeletal* animation clip (smooth per-vertex bone-weight blending across a moving skeleton, not
> just discrete rigid per-mesh transforms). Confirmed via grep: `AnimationClip`/`Keyframe`/
> `AnimationPlayer` do not exist anywhere in `cna/include` or `cna/src` today, and `.model.json`
> has no bone-weight-per-vertex or keyframe/clip section at all — `ModelTypeReader` only ever
> parses a static bone hierarchy. Note this is a *different* problem from `SkinnedEffect`'s own
> GLSL shader (already implemented, `skinned3d.{vert,frag}.glsl` — can render a skinned mesh
> **given** a bone-transform array); the missing piece is producing that per-frame array from
> real animation data in the first place. Recommended approach (per `DEFERRED.md`'s own
> reasoning): prototype the data model + player against **SkinningSample** — the XNA reference
> sample this whole pipeline is named after — before attempting the rest of Phase 4's remaining
> animation-dependent samples (`CustomModelAnimation`, `SkinnedModelExtensions`, `CPUSkinning`,
> and `RolePlayingGame`'s own animated-character subset). Effort L/XL overall; task numbers below
> are a first-pass breakdown, expect this phase's own task list to grow once Task 939's schema
> design is actually attempted.

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 939 | Design the `.model.json`/sibling-file schema extension for per-vertex bone weights/indices and keyframe/clip data | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10, design decision: reuse the existing `.skinnedmodel.json`/`.skeleton.bin`/`.clip.bin` formats verbatim, rather than inventing a new schema.** Before designing anything, investigated whether CNA already has a working skeletal-animation data model — it does: `SkinnedModelEXT` (used by `AvatarRenderer::EnableRealRenderingEXT`) already has `KeyframeEXT`/`BoneTrackEXT`/`AnimationClipEXT` plus a proven, tested `ComputeBoneTransformsEXT()` sampler, and `SkinnedModelTypeReader` (`ContentManager.cpp`) already parses a working `"skeleton"` (→ `.skeleton.bin`: `int32 boneCount` + `int32[boneCount]` parent indices + `Matrix[boneCount]` bind pose + `Matrix[boneCount]` inverse bind pose) and `"animations"` (→ array of `{name, clip}`, each `.clip.bin`: `double duration` + `int32 trackCount` + per track `int32 boneIndex` + `int32 keyCount` + per key `double time` + 3+4+3 floats translation/rotation/scale) format — deliberately kept **separate** from `Model`/`ModelMesh` (per that class's own documented rationale: rigid multi-part hierarchy vs. per-vertex GPU skinning are different XNA concepts; Phase 76 already built the former). **Decision:** extend `.model.json` with the exact same 2 optional top-level fields (`"skeleton"`, `"animations"`), byte-for-byte reusing `.skeleton.bin`/`.clip.bin` and the existing `BinReaderEXT`/`ParseFlatObjectArrayEXT` parsing helpers already proven by `SkinnedModelTypeReader` — zero new binary format, zero new parsing code to write or test from scratch. Per-mesh vertex data reuses the **already-existing** `VertexPositionNormalTextureSkinned` (NOXNA) vertex type and its already-working stride-52 GPU pipeline on all 3 hardware backends (EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx all already dispatch stride 52 to a real skinned-vertex shader) — `.model.json`'s mesh entries just need a new `"vertexStride": 52` branch in `ModelTypeReader::Read()`'s existing stride-based vertex-parsing switch (Task 941), and `"effect": "SkinnedEffect"` to select a real `SkinnedEffect` instead of `BasicEffect`. **Also corrected a false premise in Task 942's own original wording**: `Model.Draw()` has no bone-transform parameter in real XNA — confirmed by reading `FNA/src/Graphics/Model.cs` directly, which declares only `Draw(Matrix world, Matrix view, Matrix projection)`. The real Skinned Model Sample pattern calls `SkinnedEffect::SetBoneTransforms()` (already implemented in CNA) directly on each mesh's own Effect, then `ModelMesh::Draw()` — not through `Model::Draw()` at all; Task 942 corrected accordingly. No source code changed by this task — pure design decision, informing Tasks 940/941. |
| 940 | Add `AnimationClip`/`Keyframe`/`AnimationPlayer` classes under `Microsoft::Xna::Framework` | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Added new `include/`/`src/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/AnimationPlayer.hpp`/`.cpp`, all correctly `NOXNA`-marked and placed in the `Graphics` sub-namespace (matching `SkinnedModelEXT`'s own precedent — corrects Task 939's original wording, which said "under Microsoft::Xna::Framework" with no NOXNA marker, even though neither `AnimationClip`/`Keyframe`/`AnimationPlayer` nor `SkinningData` were ever real XNA framework types; they're the Microsoft "Skinned Model Sample"'s own well-known SAMPLE code, never shipped in `Microsoft.Xna.Framework` itself). Per Task 939's reuse decision: `Keyframe`/`AnimationClip` are direct `NOXNA using` aliases of `KeyframeEXT`/`AnimationClipEXT` (zero duplicate data types — both this real-`Model`-facing path and `SkinnedModelEXT`'s Avatar-facing path share the exact same keyframe representation and, going forward, the exact same content-loading code once Task 941 lands). Added a new `SkinningData` struct (`BoneCount`/`SkeletonHierarchy`/`BindPose`/`InverseBindPose`/`AnimationClips`, mirroring the real sample's own well-known `SkinningData` class) as the container a loaded `Model`'s own `Tag` property will hold (matching the real sample's own convention, since real XNA's `Model` has no dedicated skinning-data slot). `AnimationPlayer` is a stateful wrapper (`StartClip()`/`Update(time, relativeToCurrentTime, loop)`/`GetBoneTransforms()`/`GetWorldTransforms()`/`GetSkinTransforms()`) around keyframe-interpolation math deliberately re-implemented as its own small, self-contained `SampleTrack()` (not literally shared with `SkinnedModelEXT.cpp`'s identical-looking function, to keep the two systems independent per `SkinnedModelEXT`'s own documented separation rationale) — same floor-mod-via-ticks looping approach, same topological-order parent-composition loop, same bind-pose fallback for untracked bones as `ComputeBoneTransformsEXT`. **Test:** new `tests/.../AnimationPlayerTests.cpp` (12 gtest cases) mirrors `SkinnedModelEXTTests.cpp`'s own 2-bone-rig fixture and coverage shape exactly (start/mid/end-of-clip sampling, clamp-vs-loop past the end, relative-time accumulation, parent-hierarchy composition, bind-pose-when-no-clip-started, skin-transform inverse-bind-pose application, mismatched-array-size and forward-referenced-parent validation, default-constructed `SkinningData`): 12/12 PASS. **Discriminating power verified via targeted mutation** (this is new functionality with no prior behavior to `git stash` revert to, matching Task 930's own established precedent for this situation): temporarily dropped the parent-transform composition multiply in `RecomputeTransforms()`, rebuilt, confirmed exactly `ParentHierarchyComposition` fails (and only that one) as predicted; reverted, reconfirmed 12/12. Full regression: `CnaTests` 4371/4373 passed (2 pre-existing hardware-dependent skips, 12 new tests, zero failures) — pure C++ math with no backend-specific code, so no separate Vulkan/Bgfx regression run needed. |
| 941 | Extend `ModelTypeReader::Read()` to parse Task 939's new schema (bone weights/indices, keyframe/clip data) into real `Model`/`SkinnedEffect`-consumable data | ✅ | **CLOSED — 2026-07-10.** Extended `ModelTypeReader::Read()` (`ContentManager.cpp`) per Task 939's reuse decision: an optional top-level `"skeleton"` field is parsed byte-for-byte via the existing `BinReaderEXT`/`.skeleton.bin` format into a new `Graphics::SkinningData` (`BoneCount`/`SkeletonHierarchy`/`BindPose`/`InverseBindPose`, same `kMaxSaneBoneCount` sanity check as `SkinnedModelTypeReader`), and an optional `"animations"` field is parsed via `ParseFlatObjectArrayEXT` into `SkinningData::AnimationClips`. The resulting `SkinningData` is attached to the returned `Model`'s own `Tag` property (`model.setTagProperty(...)`, null for a rigid `.model.json` with no `"skeleton"` field) — matches the real Skinned Model Sample's own convention, since real XNA's `Model` has no dedicated skinning-data slot. Added a new `stride == 52` branch to the per-mesh vertex-parsing chain (GPU-skinned `VertexPositionNormalTextureSkinned` layout) using `VertexBuffer::SetDataRaw()` — a raw byte copy with no C++ struct reinterpretation, so (unlike the 4 pre-existing stride branches) this one carries none of Task 927's vtable-inflation risk, matching `SkinnedModelTypeReader`'s own identical stride-52 handling. Added `"effect": "SkinnedEffect"` support (constructs a real `Graphics::SkinnedEffect` instead of `BasicEffect`) and extended the `"texture"` binding to also bind a `SkinnedEffect`'s `Texture` (`SkinnedEffect` has no `TextureEnabled` toggle in real XNA — always textured). **Refactor:** extracted the `.clip.bin`-reading logic (previously inline inside `SkinnedModelTypeReader::Read()`'s own `"animations"` loop) into a shared `ReadAnimationClipFileEXT()` free function, reused by both readers — preserves the Task 11.11 evaluation-order-safe separate-named-locals read pattern verbatim, verified as a behavior-preserving no-op via `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest.*`/`SkinnedModelEXTTest.*` (25/25 PASS before and after). Also had to relocate the `BinReaderEXT`/`FindMatchingBracketEXT`/`ParseFlatObjectArrayEXT`/`ReadAnimationClipFileEXT` helper block (previously declared only right before `SkinnedModelTypeReader`, further down the file) to above `ModelTypeReader` — a plain C++ forward-reference ordering fix, no behavior change. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_model_json_reader_skeleton_test.cpp` (structural, no rendering, mirrors Task 937's own bone-hierarchy test shape) — a 2-bone-rig `.model.json` fixture with a `"skeleton"`/`"animations"` field and a stride-52 `"effect": "SkinnedEffect"` + `"texture"` mesh, loaded via `Content.Load<Model>()`: verifies `Model.Tag` is a real `SkinningData` with correct `BoneCount`/`SkeletonHierarchy`/`BindPose`/`InverseBindPose`/`AnimationClips["Move"]` (duration, track, keyframes), that the mesh's own `Effect` is a real `SkinnedEffect` with its `Texture` bound, that the stride-52 vertex upload reached the GPU buffer (`ModelMeshPart::getNumVerticesProperty() == 3`), and end-to-end feeds this real loaded data into a real `AnimationPlayer` (`StartClip`/`Update`/`GetWorldTransforms()`) confirming it reaches the expected bone position at t=1s. 14/14 PASS. **Discriminating power verified via targeted mutation**: temporarily changed the `"skeleton"` field lookup key to a non-matching string, rebuilt, confirmed exactly the 1 skeleton-dependent check failed (4/5, all non-`SkinningData` checks unaffected) as predicted; reverted, reconfirmed 14/14. Registered for both EasyGL and Vulkan (shared code) in `CMakeLists.txt`. **Full regression:** EasyGL `CnaTests` 4371/4373 (2 pre-existing hardware skips, zero failures) + `ctest -R "^EasyGL_"` 181/183 (2 pre-existing failures: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` — the latter already documented as a known pre-existing gap in Task 872's own CMakeLists.txt comment; both confirmed present on the unmodified `git stash` baseline too). Vulkan: `cna_test_vulkan_model_json_reader_skeleton` 14/14 PASS, `ctest -R "ModelJsonReader\|Model_\|ModelDraw"` 7/7 PASS; `CnaTests`' `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest`/`SkinnedModelEXTTest`/`AnimationPlayerTest` suite crashes (segfault, exit 139) on 3 **pre-existing** tests that write a zero-length `.idx.bin` fixture (`PartNameWithUnbalancedEmbeddedBraceParsesCorrectly`, `TextureLoadsFromNestedButUnderRootManifestDirectory`, `TextureLoadsFromManifestOutsideContentRoot`) — confirmed via `git stash` to crash identically on the unmodified baseline (a pre-existing Vulkan zero-size-`vkCreateBuffer`/`vkAllocateMemory` bug, matching the earlier-documented "3 non-deterministic `ContentManagerSkinnedModelTest.*` segfaults" pattern), not introduced by this task; all other 40 tests in that filter PASS. |

Also confirmed `Model::Draw()`'s existing per-mesh loop already sets `World`/`View`/`Projection` on every unique effect in each mesh's own `ModelEffectCollection` (populated automatically by `ModelMeshPart::setEffectProperty()`, which the Task 941 content loader already calls) — so the only wiring genuinely missing was calling `SetBoneTransforms(player.GetSkinTransforms())` on the mesh's `SkinnedEffect` before `Model.Draw()`, exactly matching the real Skinned Model Sample's own game-code pattern. **Test:** new `examples/easygl_model_skinned_animation_playback_test.cpp` — a real `.model.json` fixture (1-bone rig, `"Move"` clip translating bone 0 from `(0,0,0)` at t=0s to `(+0.5,0,0)` at t=1s, a stride-52 quad covering NDC x:-1..0 with `"effect": "SkinnedEffect"` + texture, mirroring Task 407's own established GPU-skinning geometry/pixel-math convention) loaded via `Content.Load<Model>()`.
