## Phase 80 — `PreferPerPixelLighting`/`specularEnabled`: real per-vertex lighting on every backend (`plan_dx9.md` Divergence 1/4, 2026-07-16)

> **Cross-cutting, project-owner-approved 2026-07-16.** `plan_dx9.md`'s own "CNA's divergences
> from XNA 4.0" section found, while building the D3D9 backend, that `BasicEffect`/`SkinnedEffect`
> declare and store a real `PreferPerPixelLighting` property (default `false`, matching XNA) but
> `FillGpuDrawParams()` never forwarded it to any backend, and every backend's only lit shader
> computes lighting in the fragment stage unconditionally — **CNA renders per-pixel lighting
> always, on all 9 backends, which is the exact opposite of XNA's own default (per-vertex/Gouraud),
> and no per-vertex lighting shader exists anywhere in this codebase.** A second, narrower gap of
> the same shape: `EnvironmentMapEffect.specularEnabled` is tracked internally but never forwarded
> either (no lossless recovery from `envMapSpecular`'s RGB value alone, unlike `BasicEffect`'s
> `oneLight`/`AlphaTestEffect`'s `isEqNe`, which `plan_dx9.md` `D9-81` found ARE losslessly
> recoverable and needed no struct change). `plan_dx9.md`'s own Boundaries explicitly forbade
> fixing this from inside that backend's plan — a change touching `GpuDrawParams` and requiring a
> new shader variant on 7 of the other 9 backends is exactly the kind of decision that plan
> reserves for the project owner. Presented to the project owner 2026-07-16 with three options
> (a small additive-only struct change unblocking D3D9 alone; the full fix on every backend; leave
> it as a documented, permanent divergence) — **the project owner chose the full fix, sequenced one
> backend at a time**, not all at once.
>
> **Scope per backend.** `GpuDrawParams` gains 2 bool fields (Task 1100, done — see its own row).
> D3D9 needs no new shader (Microsoft's own `.fx` sources already contain both the
> `VSBasic{Vertex,OneLight}*`/`VSBasicPixelLighting*` families and their `EnvironmentMapEffect`/
> `SkinnedEffect` equivalents) — it only needs its dispatch to stop hardcoding the flags `false`
> and to complete `D9-73`'s own outstanding obligation (prove the 5 divergent `PixelLighting`
> vertex-shader variants byte-equivalent against the `D9-A` oracle). The other 7 rendering backends
> (`SdlRenderer` is 2D-only and never reaches this code path at all; `Headless` renders no real
> pixels) each need a genuinely new per-vertex-lit shader variant, ported from FNA's own
> `Lighting.fxh`/`BasicEffect.fx`/`SkinnedEffect.fx` (the same Blinn-Phong math already used, moved
> from the fragment stage into the vertex stage with Gouraud interpolation across the triangle —
> not a different lighting model, a different *stage*) plus the dispatch/pipeline-state wiring to
> select it when `preferPerPixelLighting == false`:
>
> | Backend | Shader language | New shader needed? |
> |---|---|---|
> | D3D9 | (vendored HLSL SM2, already compiled) | No — dispatch/flag-wiring only |
> | EasyGL | GLSL | Yes |
> | Vulkan | GLSL → SPIR-V | Yes |
> | Bgfx | own shaderc toolchain | Yes |
> | WebGPU | WGSL | Yes |
> | D3D11 | HLSL SM5 | Yes |
> | D3D12 | HLSL SM5 (may share D3D11's) | Yes |
> | Software | CPU rasterizer per-vertex path | Yes |
>
> **Known, accepted cost, stated up front (per `plan_dx9.md`'s own "Consequences of fixing these"
> section) — do not "fix" this away when it happens:** once a backend's dispatch honors
> `preferPerPixelLighting`'s real default (`false` = per-vertex), every existing lit-scene pixel
> test on that backend will very likely start producing different (correct) pixels — flatter,
> faceted on low-poly geometry, weaker/rounder specular lost to Gouraud interpolation — and its
> baseline needs regenerating, not reverting. `AlphaTestEffect`/`DualTextureEffect` have no
> lighting concept and are entirely unaffected.

| #    | Task | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 1100 | Add `preferPerPixelLighting`/`specularEnabled` bool fields to `GpuDrawParams` (`IGraphicsBackend.hpp`); wire `BasicEffect`/`SkinnedEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` to forward the real `preferPerPixelLighting_` value and `EnvironmentMapEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` to forward the real `specularEnabled_` value. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** Purely additive: both new fields default `false`, and no backend reads either yet, so this is a zero-behavior-change commit for all 9 backends — confirmed by rebuilding both the default (EasyGL) target and the D3D9 target clean, and re-running `BasicEffectDefaultsTest`/`SkinnedEffectDefaultsTest`/`EnvironmentMapEffectDefaultsTest` (123/123 PASS) plus the full D3D9 `ctest -L D3D9` suite (17/17 PASS) with zero new failures. Unblocks Task 1101 (D3D9) and each per-backend shader task below — none of which can start meaningfully until a real value reaches `GpuDrawParams` at all. |
| 1101 | D3D9: read `preferPerPixelLighting`/`specularEnabled` from `GpuDrawParams` instead of hardcoding `false` in `ComputeBasicEffectShaderIndex`/`ComputeSkinnedEffectShaderIndex`/`ComputeEnvironmentMapEffectShaderIndex`'s call sites (`D3D9EffectDraw.cpp`); verify against the real XNA oracle; complete `plan_dx9.md` `D9-73`'s outstanding obligation (prove the 5 divergent `PixelLighting` vertex-shader variants byte-equivalent, per-shader, against `D9-A`); advance `D9-84`. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16 — see `plan_dx9.md`'s `D9-73`/`D9-84` rows for the full record, cross-referenced from there too.** Confirmed via the vendored `.fx` sources' own `VSIndices`/`PSIndices` tables which newly-selectable `ShaderIndex` values are actually drawable given the existing 5 stride-keyed vertex layouts: `BasicEffect`'s pixel-lighting-textured bucket (28/29) and `SkinnedEffect`'s entire pixel-lighting bucket (12-17) reuse vertex shapes already in use by other buckets, so both are now reachable with no new vertex declaration; `BasicEffect`'s untextured pixel-lighting bucket (24/25) stays permanently blocked by the same missing-vertex-layout gap as the untextured vertex-lit bucket, unrelated to this task. `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s specular buckets (all 8) reuse the same vertex shader as their non-specular counterpart (only the pixel shader differs), so all are reachable. **A real bug was found and fixed while proving this against the oracle**: `ComputeLights()` runs in the pixel shader for the `PixelLighting` bucket only (every other lit bucket computes it in the vertex shader), but `D3D9EffectDraw.cpp`'s constant uploads all targeted the vertex shader — silently leaving the pixel shader's lighting registers at zero. Same category of gap in `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s case (`EnvironmentMapSpecular` was never uploaded to any stage at all). Fixed by uploading each affected constant to both stages via the existing soft, never-throws upload helpers (no-op wherever a stage's own register table doesn't declare that name). **`D9-73`'s obligation: 4 of 5 divergent `PixelLighting` shader variants now oracle-proven pixel-perfect** (`VSBasicPixelLightingTx`, `VSSkinnedPixelLighting{One,Two,Four}Bones`) via 5 new oracle scenes, all `0/65536` against real XNA 4.0, each confirmed genuinely discriminating first (a scratch vertex-lit twin diverges by `23409/65536` pixels before being accepted as proof — a flat single-normal quad cannot tell vertex-lit from pixel-lit apart at all, since `dot(light,N)` is linear in a constant `N`); the 5th (`VSBasicPixelLighting`, untextured) remains permanently unreachable. New `D3D9_DrawEx` Check R (19/19), mutation-verified (disabled all 29 newly-added pixel-shader-stage uploads; exactly Check R failed, nothing else; restored, reconfirmed). Full `D3D9_XNA_Diff`: 36/36 scenes pixel-perfect (31 pre-existing + 5 new), zero regressions. Full `ctest -L D3D9`: 17/17 green. |
| 1102b | EasyGL: `SkinnedEffect`'s identical fragment-lit-always gap (Task 1102's own explicitly-named follow-up — that task's own row text named only "a" shader variant, `BasicEffect`'s). Needs its own per-vertex-lit sibling shader (stride 52, `VertexPositionNormalTextureSkinned`) + dispatch, mirroring Task 1102's approach. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** New `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::EnsureSkinnedVertexLitProgram()` + `Prog3D prog_skinned_vertexlit_` — a real per-vertex-lit sibling to the existing `EnsureSkinnedProgram()` (stride 52, `SkinnedEffect`), built by the exact same technique Task 1102 already established for `BasicEffect`: the skinning math (bone-matrix blend, up to `WeightsPerVertex` weight/index pairs) is unchanged, only the Blinn-Phong `ComputeLights()` evaluation moves from the fragment stage into the vertex stage, passed to the fragment shader as `vLitRGB`/`vSpecularRGB` varyings. No separate `uAmbientColor` uniform here (matching `EnsureSkinnedProgram()`'s own shape — `SkinnedEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` already pre-folds ambient into `emissiveColor`), so `BindDrawParams()`'s existing `p.loc_ambient < 0` gating (which already distinguishes the ambient-having `BasicEffect` programs from the ambient-less `EnvironmentMapEffect`/`SkinnedEffect` ones) correctly routes this new program's uniform uploads too — zero `BindDrawParams()` changes needed, identical to Task 1102's own finding. `SelectProgram()` now picks it when `params.skinned && params.lightingEnabled && !params.preferPerPixelLighting`; `preferPerPixelLighting==true` keeps the existing per-pixel skinned shader. New program added to the context-recovery `reset_no_gl()` list. Same GLSL-ES precision-qualifier requirement Task 1102 found applies here too: `uDiffuseColor` (read by both the new vertex stage and the fragment stage) is qualified `highp` in both declarations. New `EasyGL_SkinnedEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting` CTest (3/3), reusing `easygl_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp`'s own "(a) eye straight on" discriminating scene (single Identity bone at 100% weight keeps skinning a no-op) — confirmed the expected vertex-lit (~127) and pixel-lit (~155) values are identical to `BasicEffect`'s own analogous test, since the scene (geometry, materials, light, eye position) is byte-for-byte the same and both effects share the same `Lighting.fxh` formula; rendered `(126,126,126)`/`(151,151,151)`, both within tolerance. Mutation-verified: forcing the dispatch to always select the pixel-lit program reproduces exactly the predicted failure (1/3 — checks (a) and (c) fail, (b) stays green); reverted, reconfirmed 3/3. **`easygl_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp`'s own case (a) baseline updated forward** (155→126, not reverted), same accepted-cost precedent Task 1102 already established for `easygl_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp` — that test never explicitly requested `PreferPerPixelLighting=true`, so it now correctly exercises the new real default; cases (b)/(c)/(d) were unaffected and left unchanged. Full regression: `CnaTests` gtest 4375/4377 (2 pre-existing hardware-sensor skips, zero `FAILED` in the complete log), full `ctest -R "^EasyGL_"` 201/203 (the 2 failures are the already-documented pre-existing `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`/`EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`). Scoped entirely to `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.{hpp,cpp}` and its own tests — zero touch to any other backend or to `BasicEffect`'s own Task 1102 work. **This closes Task 1102/1102b together: EasyGL now has real per-vertex-lit dispatch for both `BasicEffect` and `SkinnedEffect`.** |
| 1102 | EasyGL: real per-vertex-lit GLSL shader variant + dispatch. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** New `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::EnsureLit3DVertexLitProgram()` + `Prog3D prog_lit_textured_vertexlit_` — a real per-vertex-lit sibling to the existing `EnsureLit3DProgram()` (stride 32, `BasicEffect` lit-textured): identical Blinn-Phong math (FNA's `Lighting.fxh` `ComputeLights()`), moved into the vertex stage and passed to the fragment shader as `vLitRGB`/`vSpecularRGB` varyings instead of being recomputed per fragment — the fragment shader's own non-lighting math (texture sample, alpha test, fog) is otherwise identical. `SelectProgram()` now picks it when `params.lightingEnabled && !params.preferPerPixelLighting` (XNA's real default); `preferPerPixelLighting==true` keeps the existing per-pixel shader unchanged. Declares the SAME uniform names as the pixel-lit shader (just in the vertex stage) so `BindDrawParams()` needed zero changes. New program added to the context-recovery `reset_no_gl()` list (would have been a real bug otherwise). **Real bug found and fixed while building this**: GLSL ES requires a uniform shared across both stages to have matching precision; `uDiffuseColor` (read by both the new vertex stage for `vLitRGB` and the fragment stage for alpha) needed an explicit, matching `highp` qualifier on both declarations — the existing pixel-lit shader never hit this since it only ever declared `uDiffuseColor` in one stage. **`specularEnabled` (`EnvironmentMapEffect`) needed NO change on this backend, confirmed not just assumed**: `EnvironmentMapEffect::specularEnabled_` is literally *defined* as `environmentMapSpecular_ != (0,0,0)` (`EnvironmentMapEffect.cpp`'s own setter), and this backend's existing env-map fragment shader already uses the raw `uEnvMapSpecular` RGB value unconditionally — mathematically identical regardless of which of the two redundant signals is consulted, not merely "expected to be benign." Only `BasicEffect` got a new shader this task; `SkinnedEffect` has the identical fragment-lit-always gap on this backend (confirmed while reading its shader) but is out of this task's own explicit scope (`plan_graphics.md`'s own Task 1102 row text names only "a" shader variant) — left as a follow-up, not silently fixed or silently ignored. New `EasyGL_BasicEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting` CTest (3/3): reuses the specular test's own scene (a flat quad, one shared normal, which makes diffuse spatially constant but leaves specular genuinely position-dependent) to prove `PreferPerPixelLighting=false` (vertex-lit, ~127) and `=true` (pixel-lit, ~155) are real, different, live-selected results — both values independently re-derived via an offline Python script from FNA's own half-vector Blinn-Phong formula before being accepted, not copied from the rendered output. Mutation-verified: disabling the new dispatch branch reproduces exactly the predicted failure (both checks collapse to the same pixel-lit value); reverted, reconfirmed 3/3. **One existing test's own baseline updated forward, not reverted to** (the accepted cost `plan_graphics.md`'s own Phase 80 intro names): `easygl_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`'s case (a) (`eye straight on`) samples exactly the quad's diagonal seam, so it now reads the Gouraud-interpolated average of that seam's two vertices' own specular terms (~127) instead of a fresh per-pixel evaluation at that point (~155) — the new value was analytically re-derived first (offline Python), then confirmed to match the actual rendered output, before updating the test; case (b) still passes within its own existing ±10 tolerance and was left alone, with a comment recording the real current value for future reference. Full regression: `CnaTests` gtest 4375/4377 (2 pre-existing hardware-sensor skips, grepped the complete log for `FAILED` — zero), full `ctest -R "^EasyGL_"` 200/202 (the 2 failures are the already-documented pre-existing `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`/`EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`). Scoped entirely to `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.{hpp,cpp}` and its own tests — zero touch to any other backend. |

Identical Blinn-Phong math to `lit_textured3d.frag.glsl`/`skinned3d.frag.glsl` (FNA's `Lighting.fxh` `ComputeLights()`), moved into the vertex stage as varyings — the fragment shaders' own non-lighting math (texture sample, fog) is otherwise unchanged. VkPipeline ... has not been destroyed` error the very first test run produced (not a hypothetical). Fixed by adding both maps to the existing full-teardown destructor path and the existing `ApplyMultiSampleCount()`-triggered sample-count-change pipeline-cache invalidation path, mirroring exactly how `pipelinesLitTextured3D_`/`pipelinesSkinned3D_` are already torn down in both places — confirmed the validation error disappeared after the fix, re-running the identical test.<br><br>New `Vulkan_BasicEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting`/`Vulkan_SkinnedEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting` CTests (3/3 each), reusing `vulkan_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`'s/`vulkan_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp`'s own discriminating scene (shared-vertex-normal quad, sample the triangle seam — Gouraud-averaged specular differs from a fresh per-fragment evaluation even with one shared normal, since `pow(dot(H,N),power)` is non-linear). Measured values on this backend, confirmed against the real render rather than copied from EasyGL: `BasicEffect` vertex-lit 127 (EasyGL: also 127, exact match) / pixel-lit 155; `SkinnedEffect` vertex-lit 125 (EasyGL: 126, a 1-unit backend-rounding difference, not a bug). Scoped entirely to `VulkanGraphicsBackend`/`compile_shaders.py`/its own shaders and tests — zero touch to any other backend. |
| 1104 | Bgfx: real per-vertex-lit shader variant (own shaderc toolchain) + dispatch. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16 — both `BasicEffect` and `SkinnedEffect` in one pass** (this backend's `.sc`/shaderc build-time toolchain made splitting less natural, matching Vulkan's own Task 1103 choice). New `vs_lit_textured3d_vertexlit.sc`/`fs_lit_textured3d_vertexlit.sc` (stride 32) and `vs_skinned3d_vertexlit.sc`/`fs_skinned3d_vertexlit.sc` (stride 52): identical Blinn-Phong math to their existing per-pixel-lit siblings (FNA's `Lighting.fxh` `ComputeLights()`), moved into the vertex stage and passed to the fragment shader as new shared `v_litRGB`/`v_specularRGB` varyings (registered once in `varying.def.sc`, reused by both new shader pairs) instead of being recomputed per fragment from an interpolated normal/eye vector — the fragment shaders' own non-lighting math (texture sample, `EmissiveColor` add, fog) is otherwise identical to their siblings'. New `litTextured3DVertexLitProgram_`/`skinned3DVertexLitProgram_` program handles + `compile_shaders.py` registrations (`kLitTextured3dVertexLitShaders`/`kSkinned3dVertexLitShaders`); both new shader pairs compiled clean across all 4 bgfx target profiles this project supports on Linux (GLSL/ESSL/SPIR-V/WGSL). Dispatch at all 4 existing draw-call sites (2 each for `BasicEffect`'s lit-textured family and `SkinnedEffect`, non-indexed/indexed): `!params.preferPerPixelLighting && bgfx::isValid(...VertexLitProgram_)` selects the new program, falling back to the existing per-pixel-lit one if the vertex-lit sibling ever failed to compile on a given renderer. All existing shared uniforms (`u_ambientColor`/`u_light0-2Dir`/`u_light0-2Diffuse`/`u_light0-2Specular`/`u_specularColorPower`/`u_emissiveColor`/`u_eyePos`/`u_lightingEnabled`) are bound by name and already created once, so no new uniform objects were needed — only the two new program handles. **Confirmed (by reading `fs_env_map3d.sc`, not assumed) that `EnvironmentMapEffect.specularEnabled` needs no change on this backend either**: `u_envMapSpecular.xyz` is already used unconditionally in the existing blend formula, mathematically redundant with the boolean regardless of which of the two signals is consulted — same finding as every other backend so far. New `Bgfx_BasicEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting`/`Bgfx_SkinnedEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting` CTests (3/3 each, reusing the existing specular tests' own discriminating scene — a flat quad with one shared normal, sampled exactly on the diagonal seam between its two triangles, where DIFFUSE is spatially constant but SPECULAR still varies with position): measured vertex-lit 127/126 (BasicEffect/SkinnedEffect), pixel-lit 152/151 — both within this test family's existing ±10 tolerance of EasyGL's/Vulkan's own 127/125-126 and 155 measurements, confirming the same math, not merely "close enough by luck." Mutation-verified: forcing the dispatch condition to `false` at all 4 call sites reproduced the exact predicted failure (checks (a)/(c) fail, reading the old pixel-lit value; check (b) stays green) on both new tests; reverted (`diff` confirmed byte-identical), rebuilt, reconfirmed 3/3 on both. **Existing baselines updated forward** (same precedent as Tasks 1102/1102b/1103): `bgfx_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`/`bgfx_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp`'s own case (a) never set `PreferPerPixelLighting`, so both now correctly exercise the vertex-lit path — updated 155→127/126 with the old value's provenance documented in place; both back to 5/5 after the update. Full regression: `ctest -R "^Bgfx_(BasicEffect\|SkinnedEffect\|EnvironmentMap)"` 32/33 (`Bgfx_SkinnedEffect_WeightsPerVertex` is a real, confirmed **pre-existing** failure — re-verified via `git stash` that it fails identically against the unmodified code, unrelated to this task); `CnaTests` gtest broad filter 125/125. The full unfiltered `ctest -R "^Bgfx_"` sweep (built fresh in this worktree for the first time this session) also surfaced several other pre-existing failures/timeouts (`Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat`, `Bgfx_RasterizerState_CullMode` family, `Bgfx_SpriteFont_*`, `Bgfx_ModelJsonReader_Quad`) — spot-checked 3 of them via the same `git stash` method, all confirmed to fail identically pre-existing, not investigated further as out of this task's own scope. |
| 1105 | WebGPU: real per-vertex-lit WGSL shader variant + dispatch. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** **Scope-narrowing fact confirmed by reading this backend's source first**: WebGPU currently has a lit shader for `BasicEffect`'s lit-textured family only (`litTexturedShader_`, stride 32) — `SkinnedEffect`/`EnvironmentMapEffect` are not implemented on this backend at all yet (`DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` explicitly fall through to a throwing stub for `params.skinned`/`params.envMapping`, with an existing code comment noting this as deferred WGSL work, `Phase 58`). This task is therefore `BasicEffect`-only by necessity, not a scope cut — there is nothing to add a `SkinnedEffect`/`EnvironmentMapEffect` sibling shader to yet; `specularEnabled` is correspondingly moot here too (the effect it belongs to doesn't exist on this backend). New sibling WGSL shader (`vertexLitShaderSource`, same file) alongside `litTexturedShader_`: identical Blinn-Phong math (FNA's `Lighting.fxh` `ComputeLights()`), moved from `fs_main` into `vs_main`, passed onward as `litRGB`/`specularRGB` fields on `VertexOutput` — WGSL naturally interpolates any `@location(n)` struct field across the triangle, which is what gives Gouraud shading here, no separate interpolation logic needed. `fs_main` keeps the identical `lightingEnabled<=0.5` unlit branch and texture-sample math, just consuming the interpolated value instead of recomputing it. New `litTexturedVertexLitShader_` module + `litTexturedVertexLitPipelines_` cache (`GetOrCreatePipelineLitTextured3DVertexLit()`, mirroring `GetOrCreatePipelineLitTextured3D()`'s own key/cache shape exactly) — reuses `litBindGroupLayout_`/`litPipelineLayout_`/`texturedBindGroupLayout_` unchanged (identical UBO/binding shape), so only a new shader module + pipeline cache were needed, no new descriptor layout. New `LitTexturedDrawCommand::preferVertexLit` bool, computed once in `QueueLitTexturedDraw()` as `params.lightingEnabled && !params.preferPerPixelLighting` (XNA's real default), consumed by `RenderLitTexturedDraws()` to select the pipeline. Both the new shader module and pipeline cache are torn down in `DestroyLitTexturedResources()` alongside the existing ones (called internally by `CreateLitTexturedResources()`, which is what every existing recreate-on-resize/format-change call site already invokes — no separate registration list needed, unlike EasyGL's own `reset_no_gl()` mechanism). New `WebGPU_BasicEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting` CTest (`examples/webgpu_basiceffect_preferperpixellighting_test.cpp`, 3/3), reusing the exact discriminating scene EasyGL's/Vulkan's/Bgfx's own `PreferPerPixelLighting` tests already established (a flat quad, one shared normal — diffuse spatially constant, specular still position-dependent — sampled at the two-triangle seam). **A genuine, already-documented backend characteristic affected the expected values, confirmed empirically rather than assumed**: this backend's own `webgpu_littextured3d_test.cpp` already notes its swapchain prefers an sRGB format, so a raw readback of a linear mid-tone fragment value comes back gamma-encoded — measured centre values were `(187,187,187)` vertex-lit / `(203,203,203)` pixel-lit, NOT EasyGL's/Vulkan's/Bgfx's own linear `~127`/`~152`; confirmed these are the SAME underlying result by computing the sRGB encode of 127/255 and 152/255 by hand (≈187, ≈203) — an exact match, proving this is a display/readback characteristic of this experimental backend, not a math error. Mutation-verified: forcing `preferVertexLit=false` unconditionally reproduced the exact predicted failure (both checks collapsed to the pixel-lit `(203,203,203)` value, check (c) correctly failed too); reverted (`git diff` confirmed byte-identical), reconfirmed 3/3. No existing WebGPU `BasicEffect` lit-scene test needed a baseline update — `webgpu_littextured3d_test.cpp`'s own checks all use pure black/white extremes under a spatially-uniform lighting configuration (N·L either fully aligned or fully opposed everywhere on the quad), which Gouraud-interpolates to the identical extreme regardless of which stage evaluates it; reconfirmed 4/4 unchanged. Full regression: `ctest -L WebGPU` 10/10 (this worktree's first WebGPU build — configured `cmake-build-webgpu` from scratch, wgpu-native v29.0.1.1 fetched clean), `CnaTests` gtest broad filter (`*BasicEffect*:*Lighting*:*Lit*`) 42/42. Scoped entirely to `WebGPUGraphicsBackend`/its own new test/`CMakeLists.txt` registration — zero touch to any other backend. |
| 1106 | D3D11: real per-vertex-lit HLSL SM5 shader variant + dispatch. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** New `lit_textured3d_vertexlit.vert/frag.hlsl` (stride 32) and `skinned3d_vertexlit.vert/frag.hlsl` (stride 52) in shared `D3DCommon/shaders/` (this task and Task 1107 share the HLSL source and compiled DXBC, per that row's own anticipated note): identical Blinn-Phong math to their existing per-pixel-lit siblings (FNA's `Lighting.fxh` `ComputeLights()`), moved into the vertex stage as new interpolated semantics (`TEXCOORD3`/`TEXCOORD4` `LitRGB`/`SpecularRGB` for `BasicEffect`; `TEXCOORD2`/`TEXCOORD3`/`TEXCOORD4` `FogFactor`/`LitRGB`/`SpecularRGB` for `SkinnedEffect`). New `D3DShaderVariant::LitTextured3dVertexLit`/`Skinned3dVertexLit` enum values (`D3DShaderCache.hpp`/`.cpp`) — the shared cache mechanism needed no other changes (input-layout cache is keyed by stride, not variant-specific fields; constant-buffer upload code is keyed by the same booleans as before, unaffected by which variant is selected). `D3D11GraphicsBackend::DrawPrimitivesExImpl()`'s variant-selection switch now picks the vertex-lit variant when `params.lightingEnabled && !params.preferPerPixelLighting` (XNA's real default) for both the `needsLitTextured`/`needsSkinned` branches. `EnvironmentMapEffect.specularEnabled` confirmed (by reading the shared `env_map3d.frag.hlsl`) to need no change — same finding as every other backend so far. New Check R2/V2 in `examples/d3d11_smoke_test.cpp` (this backend's own established single-file-per-target convention — no dedicated per-effect test files exist for D3D11 the way EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx have; extending the existing smoke test matches that precedent instead of introducing a new one): reused the exact scene/analytically-derived values (~127 vertex-lit, ~155 pixel-lit) already independently verified in `easygl_basiceffect_preferperpixellighting_test.cpp`/`easygl_skinnedeffect_preferperpixellighting_test.cpp` (Tasks 1102/1102b) — a flat quad with one shared normal, sampled exactly at the diagonal seam between its two triangles. **Real bug found and fixed while compiling, unrelated to the shader math**: `compile_shaders_hlsl.py`'s own `run-wine-dxvk.sh` invocation has no `CNA_D3D11_SKIP_DXVK_GATE=1` set, so the DX-85 DXVK-authenticity gate rejected every compile (including the 20 pre-existing shaders, not just the 4 new ones) — `hlsl_compiler_tool.exe` only ever calls `D3DCompile()`, never `D3D11CreateDevice`, so it can never legitimately produce a "DXVK: <version>" log line; ran with the env var set externally rather than editing the script (out of this task's own narrow scope to fix permanently). Verified: `ctest -R "^D3D11_"` 6/6 (was already 6/6 before this task — the smoke test's own hand-maintained `totalChecks` running-sum literal needed a `+3+3` update for the 6 new checks, itself a real, easy-to-miss gate this task's own first build run caught: the binary printed all-PASS individually but exited failure until that literal was updated); `CnaTests` gtest `*BasicEffect*:*SkinnedEffect*` filter (property-level only, not pixel-rendering) 81/81 through the Wine cross-compile path. |
| 1107 | D3D12: real per-vertex-lit HLSL SM5 shader variant + dispatch (may reuse D3D11's compiled shader if the byte layout matches). | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** As anticipated by this row's own note, the HLSL source and compiled DXBC were already shared from Task 1106 — this task only needed `D3D12GraphicsBackend::DrawPrimitivesExImpl()`'s variant-selection to pick `D3DShaderVariant::LitTextured3dVertexLit`/`Skinned3dVertexLit` when `params.lightingEnabled && !params.preferPerPixelLighting`, mirroring D3D11's identical branch — the PSO cache (`D3D12PipelineStateCache`) already resolves shader bytecode generically via the same shared `D3DCommon::GetVertexShaderBytecode`/`GetPixelShaderBytecode` functions, so no PSO-cache-specific change was needed either. New Check O3/S2 in `examples/d3d12_smoke_test.cpp` (mirroring D3D11's Check R2/V2 exactly — same scene/analytically-derived values, ~127 vertex-lit/~155 pixel-lit). **Real regression found and fixed along the way, in a PRE-EXISTING check, not new code**: Check FF1 (`DX-139`, `lit_textured3d`'s Blinn-Phong specular exactness proof) implicitly relied on always-per-pixel dispatch — its own geometry is a single triangle with one shared normal but a FINITE eye position, so the eye vector genuinely varies per vertex, and its "dot(H,N)=1 exactly" claim only holds at the sampled fragment under a fresh per-fragment evaluation, not under Gouraud-interpolated per-vertex values. Unlike Tasks 1102/1102b/1103/1104's own pre-existing specular tests (which accepted the new vertex-lit default as their new expected value, since they were testing "specular exists and differs," not a specific per-pixel exactness coincidence), Check FF1's whole purpose is to prove per-pixel exactness at that coincidence — so it was fixed by explicitly setting `sp.preferPerPixelLighting = true`, preserving its original intent rather than changing its expected value. Also found and fixed a second, unrelated regression: adding 2 new RTV-allocating check blocks exhausted `D3D12GraphicsBackend::kRtvHeapCapacity` (a real "heap exhausted" throw, not a hypothetical) — raised from 32 to 40, same "bump allocator, sized for observed demand" precedent this constant's own history already established (previously raised from 8). Verified: `cna_test_d3d12_smoke.exe` direct run and `ctest -R "^D3D12_"` (1/1, `D3D12_Smoke`) both clean. `CnaTests` gtest under D3D12/vkd3d-proton could not be verified either way — even a single trivial property-only test case (no rendering) exits nonzero with no test output at all, matching this project's own documented pattern of `CnaTests`-under-cross-compiled-backend fragility being a separate, pre-existing concern unrelated to this task (only `D9-123` ever explicitly fixed/proved this combination, for D3D9 specifically — D3D12 was never proven either way before this task, so this is not a regression this task introduced). |
| 1108 | Software: real per-vertex-lit CPU rasterizer path. | ⬜ | **Row corrected 2026-07-16 — this task's own premise doesn't hold, found while scoping it, before dispatching any implementation work.** Unlike every other backend in this phase, `SoftwareGraphicsBackend` has **no lighting engine at all** — confirmed by reading `RasterizeTriangleShaded()`'s own doc comment and `DrawPrimitivesEx()`'s own header comment (`SoftwareGraphicsBackend.cpp`): `DualTextureEffect`/`EnvironmentMapEffect` are supported, but "the 'lit' base color is just `vertexColor*diffuseColor*texture0`" — no per-light diffuse/specular sum is computed at all, per this backend's own pre-existing, deliberate "design decision 6: no lighting engine in v1." There is therefore no existing per-pixel-lit path to move into a vertex stage, unlike the other 6 backends this phase touched (all of which already had a real, working per-pixel Blinn-Phong implementation that only needed relocating). Doing this for real would mean building a CPU lighting engine from scratch (both a vertex-lit AND, implicitly, deciding whether a pixel-lit mode is even in scope for "v1"), a materially larger and different-shaped task than every other row in this phase — project owner chose to defer this, log the finding, not implement now (2026-07-16). Do not dispatch this as "same as the other backends" without re-reading this note first. |
| 1109 | Regenerate/update every existing lit-scene pixel-test baseline across all touched backends once their own dispatch honors the real default; update `docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md`. | 🟨 | **Baseline updates done per-backend as each of Tasks 1101-1107 closed** (exactly as this row's own note asked — not a giant deferred sweep): `easygl_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`/`easygl_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp`, `vulkan_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`/`vulkan_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp`, `bgfx_basiceffect_specular_test.cpp`/`bgfx_skinnedeffect_specular_test.cpp` all updated forward to their new, analytically-derived vertex-lit values; D3D12's pre-existing Check FF1 was fixed by pinning `preferPerPixelLighting=true` instead (preserving its actual per-pixel-exactness intent) rather than changing its expected value. `docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md` updated 2026-07-16 (this task): new `PreferPerPixelLighting` row (all 6 real columns), `BasicEffect` real-specular D3D9 cell corrected (now oracle-tested), `EnvironmentMapEffect.specularEnabled` D3D9 cell corrected (was stale — said "structurally unreachable," fixed by Task 1101), and the "Remaining genuine D3D9 limitations" section's 3 already-fixed bullets (render-target-as-texture crash, `PreferPerPixelLighting`/specular, `CnaTests` under D3D9) corrected with strikethrough + fixed-date notes rather than silently deleted. Still 🟨, not ✅: `Software` (Task 1108) remains deferred, so this phase's own baseline-regeneration/matrix-update work isn't literally "every touched backend" complete until that's resolved one way or another. |

## Phase 81 — `Texture2D` `SurfaceFormat` breadth for the D3D9 XNA-oracle corpus (`plan_dx9.md` D9-121's own gap, 2026-07-16)

> **Scoped out, not implemented — plan only, per explicit project owner request 2026-07-16.**
> `plan_dx9.md`'s own divergence report (`D9-121`, `docs/d3d9-divergence-report.md`) names
> "every `SurfaceFormat` besides `Color`" as one of the corpus's honestly-tracked, not-yet-exercised
> gaps: the `D9-A5` oracle scene format (`tools/xna-oracle/scenes/*.scene`) can only describe an
> inline, procedurally-generated `SurfaceFormat.Color` texture today (`texturewidth`/
> `textureheight`/`texturepixel` keys), so no scene has ever exercised `Bgr565`/`Bgra5551`/
> `Bgra4444`/`Dxt1`/`Dxt3`/`Dxt5`/`NormalizedByte2`/`NormalizedByte4`/etc. against real XNA at all
> — not a D3D9-backend gap specifically, a gap in the oracle *tooling's* own texture-authoring
> surface.
>
> **Why this is bigger than "add an oracle scene key":** the oracle's `CnaOracleRender.cpp` side
> constructs its texture via CNA's own real, public `Texture2D` API (matching `D9-A3`'s own
> "byte-for-byte equivalent CNA app... via CNA's real public API" contract) — so testing a
> non-`Color` format means `Texture2D` itself needs a real, working `SetData`/constructor path for
> that format first. Some of these (`Bgr565`/`Bgra5551`/`Bgra4444`) are plain uncompressed formats
> and likely a smaller lift; the DXT-compressed formats need real block-compressed data authored
> by *something* (hand-encoded, mirroring the existing DXT1 cubemap fixture precedent in Task 934's
> own test) on both the C# (`Oracle.cs`) and C++ (`CnaOracleRender.cpp`) sides, in the same format,
> byte-for-byte — a real, nontrivial authoring task in its own right, not just a config flag.
>
> **Decision needed before implementation starts**: which formats are worth the effort (the full
> Reach/HiDef whitelist `plan_dx9.md` `D9-100` already researched, or a smaller representative
> subset — e.g. one uncompressed 16-bit format + one DXT format, enough to catch a genuine format-
> conversion bug without authoring all 9/20 Reach/HiDef formats), and whether this belongs to the
> D3D9 oracle specifically or should feed a more general cross-backend `SurfaceFormat` conformance
> effort (Phase 34, already closed/archived for the pre-existing backends — this would reopen that
> question with the oracle as a new, stronger verification method). Not decided as of this entry.

| #    | Task | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 1110 | Decide scope: which `SurfaceFormat` values justify oracle coverage, and whether `Texture2D`'s own API needs new construction/`SetData` paths for any of them before the oracle scene format can even describe one. | ⬜ | Project-owner decision needed before any implementation task is scoped further — see this phase's own intro. Not started. |

## Phase 82 — Two real EasyGL bugs found by running the D3D9 XNA-oracle corpus against EasyGL (`plan_dx9.md` D9-A6, 2026-07-16)

> **Logged per the project owner's request 2026-07-16 — not fixed here.** `plan_dx9.md`'s `D9-A6`
> task ran the D3D9 backend's 31-scene XNA-oracle corpus (built for Phase D9-A, pixel-perfect
> against real XNA 4.0 on D3D9) against EasyGL too, via a new `cna_oracle_render_easygl` target and
> `scripts/run-oracle-corpus-diff-easygl.sh` (both already committed, `2a5acdb7`). Result: 10/31
> pixel-perfect, 21/31 diverge in three evidenced patterns (see `docs/d3d9-divergence-report.md`'s
> own "Cross-backend measurement (D9-A6)" section for the full record) — 17 are a systemic
> rasterization-boundary/fill-rule difference (not tracked as a bug here, a separate, larger
> question), 2 are ordinary floating-point rounding noise, and **2 are genuine, evidenced EasyGL
> bugs**, listed below. Both are confirmed EasyGL-local: the identical scene, identical scene file,
> renders pixel-perfect (`0/65536` diff) on D3D9 against real XNA, so the divergence is not a
> shared `GpuDrawParams`/cross-cutting gap — it is specific to `EasyGLGraphicsBackend`'s own shader
> code.

| #    | Task | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 1111 | EasyGL: `fog_gradient_quad` renders the entire quad solid black (fully fogged) instead of the intended linear white→black gradient, even at the near (`z=0`, should be near-white) edge. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** Root cause confirmed by reading the shader: `vFogFactor=clamp((uFogEnd-aPos.z)/max(uFogEnd-uFogStart,1e-6),0.0,1.0)` clamped its denominator to a minimum of `+1e-6` unconditionally, discarding the true (negative) sign whenever `FogEnd<FogStart` — for this scene's `FogStart=0`/`FogEnd=-1`, the denominator collapsed to `1e-6`, blowing up the fraction to ±∞ and saturating to 0 (fully fogged) almost everywhere, exactly the reported symptom. Deeper investigation (cross-checked against `/rv/data/library/github.com/FNA-XNA/FNA/src/Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/EffectHelpers.cs`'s real `SetFogVector`/`Common.fxh`'s `ComputeFogFactor`) found the bug was NOT just the denominator clamp: the original formula's numerator (`FogEnd-z`) was never actually equivalent to FNA's real dot-product formula even for ordinary `FogStart<FogEnd` configurations — it only coincidentally matched at each test's own `z=0` midpoint. Fixed formula (all 10 identical shader-string occurrences in `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp`, `EnsureColored3DProgram()`/`EnsureTextured3DProgram()`/`EnsureColTextured3DProgram()`/`EnsureLitTextured3DProgram()`/`EnsureLitTexturedVertexLit3DProgram()`/`EnsureEnvMapped3DProgram()`/`EnsureSkinnedProgram()` and siblings): `vFogFactor=(abs(FogEnd-FogStart)<1e-6)?0.0:clamp((z+FogEnd)/(FogEnd-FogStart),0,1)`, derived from FNA's `fogFactor=saturate(scale*(z+FogStart))`, `scale=1/(FogStart-FogEnd)`, converted to EasyGL's own inverted `vFogFactor` convention (`mix(FogColor,color,vFogFactor)` vs. FNA's `lerp(color,FogColor,fogFactor)`), cross-validated by hand against `fog_gradient_quad.scene`'s own worked derivation comment. **A real, easy-to-miss consequence surfaced by this fix**: with `View=Identity`, `FogStart` is not universally "the near/unfogged boundary" the property name might suggest — which boundary reads as fogged depends on the sign relationship in the formula above, not the literal Start/End naming. This meant **5 existing "passing" EasyGL fog tests were actually validating incorrect, never-oracle-verified expected values** sharing the same broken formula (one, `easygl_alphatest_fog_test.cpp`, explicitly and wrongly claimed in its own header comment to be "equivalent to FNA... for these axis-aligned test cases"): `easygl_alphatest_fog_test.cpp`/`easygl_dualtextureeffect_fog_test.cpp` (`FogStart=-0.9`/`FogEnd=0.9`) had their `z=FogStart`/`z=FogEnd` expected-color labels exactly backwards (confirmed empirically: post-fix output at the old labels was almost byte-for-byte the OTHER label's expected value); `easygl_basiceffect_fog_test.cpp`/`easygl_skinnedeffect_fog_test.cpp`/`easygl_environmentmapeffect_fog_test.cpp` used `FogStart=0` configurations that collapse to a constant (always fully unfogged) under the real formula and could not demonstrate a gradient at all, so their (b)/(c) sub-tests were redesigned to the same `FogStart=-0.9`/`FogEnd=0.9` pattern the other two already use, for a consistent, genuinely-gradient-producing family of 5 fog tests. All 5 rewritten with corrected formulas/labels/values and explanatory comments (referencing this task and `fog_gradient_quad.scene`'s own lesson), rebuilt, and reconfirmed: **15/15 checks PASS** across all 5 files (up from 5/15 immediately post-shader-fix, before the test corrections). **Direct oracle verification against the actual flagged scene** (`cna_oracle_render_easygl` + `scripts/xna-diff.py`): `fog_gradient_quad` improved from 23,716/65,536 (pre-fix, mostly huge deltas up to 237) to 307/65,536 at `--tolerance 0`, and **0 pixels differ beyond `--tolerance 3`** — the remaining 307 are concentrated entirely at the quad's left edge column (`x=51`, matching NDC `x=-0.6`'s pixel boundary), the same pre-existing, separately-documented "systemic rasterization-boundary/fill-rule difference" affecting 17 other scenes in this corpus (not this task's scope — see this phase's own intro paragraph and `docs/d3d9-divergence-report.md`). The interior gradient itself matches the real-XNA reference to within ±1-3 (ordinary GPU float rounding), confirmed pixel-by-pixel. **Full regression**: only these 5 files in the whole repo reference `FogEnabled`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd` against EasyGL; the two backend-agnostic property round-trip tests (`alpha_test_effect_test.cpp`/`basic_effect_test.cpp`) don't render and are unaffected. |
| 1112 | EasyGL: `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s per-vertex Fresnel term does not Gouraud-interpolate correctly across a triangle — most of the surface reads close to one vertex's Fresnel value instead of a smooth gradient. | ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-07-16.** Root cause confirmed by reading both shaders side by side: FNA's `EnvironmentMapEffect.fx` (`ComputeFresnelFactor`) evaluates `pow(max(1-abs(dot(eyeVector,worldNormal)),0),FresnelFactor)*EnvironmentMapAmount` **per-vertex**, in the vertex shader, then Gouraud-interpolates the resulting scalar — `EnsureEnvMapped3DProgram()`'s fragment shader instead recomputed the same `dot`/`pow` **per-fragment** from a renormalized interpolated normal and eye vector, a fundamentally different (non-equivalent) computation once a triangle's vertices carry different normals, exactly matching the task's own best-guess. Fix: moved the Fresnel computation into the vertex shader (using each vertex's own un-interpolated `aNormal`/eye vector, normalized locally, not via the varying), output as a new `out float vFresnel` Gouraud-interpolated varying; the fragment shader now uses `vFresnel` directly with no further `dot`/`pow`, matching FNA's `pin.Specular.rgb` usage exactly. `uEnvMapAmount`/`uFresnelEnabled`/`uFresnelFactor` moved from fragment- to vertex-shader-only uniforms (matching FNA, where `EnvironmentMapAmount`/`FresnelFactor` are VS-only constants). New `EasyGL_EnvironmentMapEffect_Fresnel_Gradient` test (`examples/easygl_environmentmapeffect_fresnel_gradient_test.cpp`) reuses the oracle scene's exact geometry (top edge normal `(0,0,1)`, bottom edge normal `(1,0,0)`) and hand-derives the correct barycentric-interpolated Fresnel value at 3 points along the vertical centre line: near-top `(0,0.5)→(188,94,47)`, an interior discriminating point `(0,0.3)→(165,82,41)`, near-bottom `(0,-0.5)→(70,35,17)`, all within tolerance on first build. **Discriminating power verified by mutation** (`git stash` the shader fix, rebuild): pre-fix output was `(200,100,50)`/`(199,100,50)`/`(198,99,50)` at all 3 points — an almost exact reproduction of this row's own originally-reported symptom (`~(198,99,49)`–`(200,100,50)` everywhere) — 2/4 checks correctly failed; reverted (`git stash pop`) and reconfirmed 4/4 PASS. **Full regression, zero regressions**: all 11 pre-existing EasyGL `EnvironmentMapEffect`-family tests (`fresnel`, `fog`, `combined`, `golden`, `specular`, `amount_one`/`amount_zero`, `eyeposition`, `worldtransform`, `multilight`, plus `easygl_env_map`) individually rebuilt and re-run, all still PASS unchanged (the pre-existing `fresnel` test uses a uniform normal across all 4 vertices, so per-vertex and per-fragment computation coincide there — not discriminating for this bug, which is exactly why a new test was needed). Diagnosis and fix confirmed via direct comparison against `/rv/data/library/github.com/FNA-XNA/FNA/src/Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/HLSL/EnvironmentMapEffect.fx`. |

---

## Phase 83 — Real bugs found while implementing `CNA::GraphicsCapability` (2026-07-17)

> Not fixed here — logged per the project owner's request while implementing the throw-vs-ignore
> toggle (`ba478f01`/`59076f6f`) and its SDL_Renderer/DX3/Canvas regression suites. Both are
> genuine, pre-existing, unrelated to that feature.

| #    | Task | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 1113 | `GraphicsDevice::Clear(ClearOptions, ...)` masks `DepthBuffer`/`Stencil` out of the request whenever the active target's `SupportsDepthStencil()`/`HasRealDepthBuffer()` is false — so a depth/stencil-only clear silently becomes a no-op *before it ever reaches the backend*, on EVERY backend without a real depth buffer, not just SDL_Renderer. | ⬜ | Found running the full `SDL_Renderer` `ctest` suite while regression-testing what was then called `Unsupported3DGraphicsCallBehavior` (since replaced by `CNA::GraphicsCapability`, same day): `SDL_Renderer_ClearOptions_Audit` and `SDL_Renderer_RenderTarget_DepthDecision` both fail — `examples/sdlrenderer_clearoptions_audit_test.cpp`'s own `ClearOptions::DepthBuffer`-alone/`Target\|DepthBuffer`/`DepthBuffer\|Stencil`/`Target\|DepthBuffer\|Stencil` checks all expect `dev.Clear(options, ...)` to throw (SDL_Renderer has no depth buffer), but `GraphicsDevice.cpp`'s own masking (`options &= ClearOptions::Target` when `!hasRealDepthBuffer`) strips the `DepthBuffer`/`Stencil` bits out first, so `Clear()` returns having called nothing on the backend at all — no throw ever happens. **Confirmed pre-existing, not a regression from this session's own changes**: reproduces identically via `git stash` against the unmodified baseline (commit `11405fb1`). **DX3's own `examples/freedirect_no3d_test.cpp` (Check D) already documents and correctly designs around this exact masking behavior** — it tests the backend's `ClearColorAndDepth`/etc. directly rather than through the public `Clear()` API, explicitly noting these are "provably unreachable from the public API" through the masked path. The real question for whoever picks this up: is `SDL_Renderer_ClearOptions_Audit`'s own expectation simply wrong (should be updated to expect a graceful no-op, mirroring DX3's test design), or does `GraphicsDevice::Clear()`'s masking itself need to distinguish "no depth buffer, request masked to a harmless color-only clear" from "caller explicitly asked for something this backend cannot do, should surface that"? Check real XNA/FNA behavior before picking either. |
| 1114 | Bgfx: `SetDepthTestEnabled`/`SetBlendEnabled`/`SetDepthWriteEnabled` unconditionally throw `std::runtime_error` via `ThrowNo3DState()` — but Bgfx is a fully 3D-capable backend (unlike SDL_Renderer/DX3/Canvas), so this isn't a "no 3D support" situation, it's 3 specific legacy state-toggle methods that were never wired into bgfx's own state-flag model. | ⬜ | Found via a full sweep of all 14 backends for the `ThrowNo3D`/"2D-only" pattern while extending what was then called `Unsupported3DGraphicsCallBehavior` per the project owner's request (`59076f6f`'s own investigation; that mechanism was itself replaced by `CNA::GraphicsCapability` the same day, per the project owner's own follow-up request). `src/CNA/Internal/Backends/Bgfx/BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp`'s own `ThrowNo3DState()` comment says outright: `"Bgfx backend: SetDepthTestEnabled / SetBlend* are not yet wired into bgfx state flags."` — every other 3D entry point on this backend (`Clear*`, `CreateVertexBuffer`, real draw calls) is genuinely implemented; only these 3 toggle methods are stubs. **Deliberately NOT folded into `CNA::GraphicsCapability`**: that enum's entries each mean "permanently unsupported by design" (2D-only backends, or a genuine device-dependent feature) — this is an unfinished implementation gap on an otherwise-3D-capable backend instead, a different kind of problem a capability query would mischaracterize (Bgfx really does support depth-test/blend state, just not via these 3 specific legacy toggle methods yet). The real fix is wiring these 3 methods into `bgfx::setState()`'s own flags (`BGFX_STATE_DEPTH_TEST_*`/`BGFX_STATE_BLEND_*` per bgfx's own API) the same way `ApplyBlendState()`/`ApplyDepthStencilState()` (the real `BlendState`/`DepthStencilState` property path most games actually use) presumably already do — check whether those two full-state-object paths already make these 3 narrower toggles redundant in practice, or whether real callers (`GraphicsDevice.SetDepthTestEnabled` etc. specifically, not the state objects) can still reach them. |
| 1115 | EasyGL: `FillMode::WireFrame` is silently ignored on two routes it reports support for — a MULTI-STREAM draw and a `SpriteBatch` batch both render filled polygons while `SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` says true. | ⬜ | Found by `plans/plan_webgpu.md` `WEBGPU-153`/`154` while giving WebGPU the same edge-expansion wireframe EasyGL pioneered (REMED-GFX-219), and recorded here rather than fixed there, per `WEBGPU-154`'s own direction. **Two separate routes, one shape:** (a) `EasyGLRenderer::DrawWireframe()` is gated on `!multiStream` and is skipped for indirect draws, so a multi-stream wireframe draw renders solid; (b) `DrawWireframe()` is called only from the 3D draw entry points, so a `SpriteBatch` batch is never expanded at all. **(b) is MEASURED, four ways** (2026-09-05, a 1x1 opaque texture drawn as a 24x24 sprite into a 128x64 backbuffer, counting drawn pixels against the clear colour): a plain `Begin()`, a `Solid` `RasterizerState` passed to `Begin`, a `WireFrame` one passed to `Begin`, and a `WireFrame` one set on the device only — **576/576 filled in every case**, i.e. the fill mode changes nothing. Real XNA applies `RasterizerState` to `SpriteBatch`'s own triangles, so a wireframe batch should draw quad edges. **Why this matters beyond tidiness:** `WireFrameTriangleOracle.hpp`'s `RendersEdges()` set now includes every measured renderer, and `SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` is true here, so a caller has no way to discover either gap — which is precisely the shape `WEBGPU-115` existed to prevent on the other renderer. **The fix, if taken:** for (a), lift the `!multiStream` gate — the expansion only rewrites the INDEX stream and is indifferent to how many vertex streams feed it; for (b), each sprite is 6 consecutive vertices forming two triangles, so a shared 12-entry line-index buffer plus a per-sprite base vertex would do it. Note that fixing (b) makes EasyGL diverge from every other renderer that also fills sprites solid, so it is a decision about XNA fidelity rather than a local bug fix — take it deliberately or record the divergence. |
